- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the `[ScanCode]` spelling of keys is refused (`KeybindingScanCodeUnsupported`); `Key` has no Mac arrow labels; Windows and Mac keymaps are not kept. An entry of `keybindings.json` with an unknown key or a bad when-clause is skipped and reported (VS Code keeps it as an unbound command). The dispatcher does not detect a double press of a lone modifier (`shift shift`; the resolver handles those bindings); that needs key-up events and goes with the workbench. `Run` does not check preconditions. Default bindings of commands that later steps implement (clipboard, find, scrolling, `deleteAllLeft`, snake/camel case, `duplicateSelection`, …) are added by those steps; the scroll commands are not registered here because the editor widget owns them. `Describe` of a `Problem` has no line number (the entry's position in the array is given).

## 2.6 Globs

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-2.6-globs
- **Summary:** `core/glob` has VS Code's glob engine: `Parse(pattern, Options{TrimForExclusions, IgnoreCase})` (`*`, `?`, `**`, `{a,b}`, `[0-9]`, `[!0-9]`, both slashes as separators, the trivial-pattern fast paths and their `BasenameTerms`/`PathTerms`), `ParseRelative` for a pattern relative to a base folder, and `ParseExpression` for `files.exclude`-style expressions (ordered clauses, off clauses, `when: "$(basename).ts"` sibling clauses, basename patterns merged into one lookup); `ExpressionFromSetting` reads a settings value. `Ignore` is a gitignore matcher: `AddFile(dir, text)` for each `.gitignore` or `.ignore` as a walk meets it, `AddGlobal` for lower-priority files, `Ignored(path, isDir)` (a path below an excluded folder is excluded) and `Check` for one path without its parents.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/glob/{glob.go,expression.go,ignore.go}`, tests `internal/core/glob/{glob_test.go,expression_test.go,ignore_test.go,match_cases_test.go,ignore_git_test.go}`; `docs/SPEC.md` §10.5.
- **Tests:** ported all of `glob.test.ts` except the promise test and the Windows branches: 395 pattern/path checks converted once by a script that is not kept (simple, dot hidden, file pattern, star, file / folder match, questionmark, globstar, issue 41724, brace expansion, brackets, full path, ending path, prefix agnostic, cached properly, invalid glob, caseInsensitiveMatch), and by hand split glob aware, the expression tests (single, multiple, disabled, empty, falsy value, basenames, ignore case, siblings), basename and path terms and their optimizations, trailing slash, relative patterns, patternsEquals, isEmptyPattern and the URI match. Emetos's own: bracket cases JavaScript reads differently from Go (`[]`, `[^]`), `isEqualOrParent`, `normalize`, expression from a setting, a table of 40 gitignore cases (comments, escapes, trailing spaces, CRLF, BOM, anchoring, folder-only, `**` forms, classes, negation, re-inclusion below excluded folders, nested files, global files, case). `TestIgnoreAgainstGit` (build tag `emetos_git`) runs the same table through `git check-ignore` in a temporary repository with the user's git configuration switched off, and agrees on every case. Coverage 97.8%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** patterns run on Go's `regexp`; JavaScript's `[]` (matches nothing) and `[^]` (matches anything) are translated, and the pattern cache of VS Code is not kept (no global state: a caller keeps the parsed matcher). The sibling function is synchronous; the promise-returning form of VS Code is not ported. A relative pattern whose base contains `..` is normalized for the parent check but cut at the length of the original base, as in VS Code. `IgnoreCase` defaults to false (Linux). `ExpressionFromSetting` orders clauses by pattern, since a decoded JSON object has no order; the ordered form `Expression` is there for callers that read the file with `core/jsonc`. The gitignore matcher does not read files itself and does not implement `core.excludesFile` lookup or `.git/info/exclude` discovery (the workspace step passes their text to `AddGlobal`).

## 3.1 Application shell

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-3.1-application-shell
- **Summary:** `emetos [folder] [file...]` opens one window that shows the first file as plain text with line numbers (a placeholder editor, replaced in 3.2), in the editor font of the settings on Dark Modern's colors. `gui/app` has `Process` (paths, logger, settings, command and default-keymap registries, notifications, logs) and `Window` (folder, settings store with the workspace layer read from `<folder>/.emetos/settings.json`, root context key scope, documents, keybinding resolver), passed down by the `WindowScope` inherited widget (`WindowOf`, `PeekWindow`); the core never sees Glutter types. `core/notify` is the notification model: severity, message, source, actions, a service that keeps them, tells listeners and writes each to the log. `kit.Theme` is an inherited widget with the 19 workbench colors of the step, valued from Dark Modern (`kit.DarkModern`, each one marked as from the theme file or from the color registry's dark default). ## 3.2 View model and text layer

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-3.2-view-model-text-layer
- **Summary:** `gui/editor` replaces the placeholder editor of 3.1. `ViewModel` holds the document, the cursors, the options, the scroll position (`ScrollTop`, `ScrollLeft`, `ViewState` with `RestoreViewState`), the viewport height and the shared paragraph cache, and implements `edit.LineLayout`; `DocumentChanged` re-syncs it after an edit and tells its listeners. `Projection` is the interface that folding, wrapping and view zones will extend (row count, rows of a model line, the line and columns of a row, row top and height, row at a y offset); `NewIdentityProjection` is the one used now. `LineHeight` follows VS Code's `fontInfo` rule (0 means round(1.35 × size), a value below 8 is a multiplier, at least 8). `ParagraphCache` is an LRU of built paragraphs with hit and build counters. `RenderEditorText` is a leaf `RenderBox` with a vertical `ViewportOffset` (from `Scrollable`) and the horizontal scroll: it lays out the visible rows plus 10 on each side, one paragraph per row with `TabSize` and the font features of the options, and paints inside a clip; `RenderEditorGutter` paints the line numbers of the visible rows (`editor.lineNumbers`: on, off, relative, interval; the active line is left-aligned and absolute) with the width rules of VS Code (glyph margin + digits × digit width + decorations and folding). `Editor` is the widget: a `Scrollable` with the gutter and the text layer side by side, each a repaint boundary. `OptionsFromSettings` reads the `editor.*` settings (font families, size, line height, ligatures, line numbers, glyph margin, decorations width, folding, `scrollBeyondLastLine`, minimum number characters); the shell follows the settings and calls `SetOptions`.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/editor/{lineheight.go,projection.go,linenumbers.go,cache.go,viewmodel.go,text.go,render.go,gutter.go,widget.go,options.go}`, tests `internal/gui/editor/{logic_test.go,options_test.go,widget_test.go}`, goldens `internal/gui/editor/testdata/goldens/`, `internal/gui/app/app.go` (the shell shows `editor.Editor`; `editor.go` is removed), `internal/core/settings/builtin.go` (new `editor.*` settings).
- **Tests:** only the visible rows are built (5000 lines, a few dozen paragraphs), scrolling builds new rows only and scrolling back builds none, the scroll extent with and without `scrollBeyondLastLine`, an edit of one line builds one paragraph and the other rows hit the cache, a remembered scroll position is restored, no digit measures or numbers with line numbers off, a new font size builds new paragraphs, the line height table, the projection, the line number modes, the gutter widths, the options from settings. Goldens (each one viewed): plain Go text, tabs at size 4 and 8, relative line numbers, interval line numbers. Coverage of `gui/editor` 88.2%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:**
  ![3.2 text layer](implemented-proof-images/3.2-text-layer.png)
  `go test ./internal/gui/editor -run TestGoldenPlainGoText -proof`: the sample Go file in Go Mono with the gutter; the last line is cut at the right edge, there is no wrapping yet.
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** there is no horizontal scrollbar or wheel handling yet (`ScrollLeft` is set by the view model; 3.4 adds the scrollbars and hit testing). The gutter has no glyph margin content, folding controls or decorations (the widths are reserved only when the options ask for them). No cursor, selection or current line is drawn (3.3), and there is no syntax color (4.x). The digit width is not measured when line numbers are off.

## 3.3 Selections and cursors

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-3.3-selections-cursors
- **Summary:** the editor draws the current line, the selections and the cursors in two layers around the text layer, as a `Stack` inside the scrollable: `RenderEditorOverlay` below the text (current line, then selections) and above it (cursors), each a repaint boundary that listens to the view model's own selection notifier, so a move does not lay the text out again and a blink repaints only the cursors. The gutter paints its part of the current line highlight (`editor.renderLineHighlight`: `none`, `gutter`, `line`, `all`, `editor.renderLineHighlightOnlyWhenFocus`) and follows the selections (the number of the cursor's line and the relative numbers change with a move). Selections are ported from VS Code's `selections.ts`: `editor.roundedSelection`, 3-pixel corners, reversed corners drawn as the selection piece plus a piece in the editor's color (`styleSelectionRows`, `selectionPieces`), rows extended by one character where the line break is selected, a one-pixel inset between several selections, the inactive color without focus. The current line follows `currentLineHighlight.ts` (no text highlight while a selection has text; the 2-pixel border when the theme's highlight color is transparent). Cursors are line cursors of `editor.cursorWidth` (0 means 2) at the active end of each selection. Blinking (`cursorBlink`, `BlinkInterval` 500 ms) uses the timers of the window's binding through `ViewModel.SetTimer`: every move or edit (`SelectionsChanged`, `DocumentChanged`, `SetOptions`) shows the cursors and starts counting again; an editor without focus has no cursors and no timer; the widget observes `DidChangeViewFocus` and removes its timers when it is disposed. `ViewModel` got `Focused`/`SetFocused`, `CursorsVisible`, the selection and blink listeners and `RowOfPosition`; `geometry` measures a byte column of a row through the cached paragraphs. New settings: `editor.renderLineHighlight`, `editor.renderLineHighlightOnlyWhenFocus`, `editor.roundedSelection`, `editor.cursorWidth`, `editor.cursorBlinking`.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/editor/{blink.go,selection.go,geometry.go,overlay.go,gutter.go,widget.go,viewmodel.go}`, tests `internal/gui/editor/{decoration_test.go,widget_test.go,options_test.go}`, goldens `internal/gui/editor/testdata/goldens/`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.4.
- **Tests:** corner styles of the rows of a selection (seven shapes, including the quarter-character tolerance), the pieces drawn (rounded, square, several selections), where the current line is highlighted (eight combinations of setting and focus), the blink with a fake clock (period, restart on a move, an edit and an options change, no timer without focus or without a timer source, the styles of the setting), listeners, `RowOfPosition`; in a pumped window: columns measured with tabs, trailing spaces and a two-byte character, the rows of a selection, the blinking seen in the pixel of the cursor, no blinking and no cursor after the window lost the focus and its return, no timer left after the editor is removed, the border of the current line for each setting. Goldens (each one viewed): multi-line selection, staircase selection, square corners, inactive selection, three cursors, several selections, current line, current line in the gutter and in the text, current line in the gutter only; the earlier goldens changed by the border of the current line on the first row. Coverage of `gui/editor` 91.6%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:**
  ![3.3 cursors](implemented-proof-images/3.3-cursors.png)
  `go test ./internal/gui/editor -run TestProofCursors -proof`: a Go file with a multi-line selection (rounded corners, the inner corners where a row reaches further than the one above), two more cursors, line numbers, and the current lines of the three cursors highlighted in the gutter (`editor.renderLineHighlight` is `all`; the text itself has no highlight while a selection has text, as in VS Code).
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** only the line cursor style is drawn (no block, underline or thin styles); `smooth`, `phase` and `expand` blink like `blink`. The border of the current line is drawn when the highlight color is transparent, without checking whether the theme defines the border color itself (`kit.Colors` does not carry that). The inactive line highlight uses the focused color (VS Code's default). Selections have no text color override (`editorSelectionForeground`), and the viewport-edge hiccup optimization of VS Code is not needed because the neighbors of the visible rows are measured. The editor has no focus of its own yet: it has the focus when the window has (input and focus management come with 3.5 and later).

## 3.4 Scrollbars and hit testing

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-3.4-scrollbars-hit-testing
- **Summary:** `kit.ScrollbarState` ports VS Code's scrollbar arithmetic (slider size with the 20-pixel minimum, slider position, the scroll position a click on the track, a page click or a drag asks for). `kit.ScrollableElement` (first version) puts overlay scrollbars over any child, driven by a `kit.ScrollModel` (listeners, `Metrics`, `ScrollTo`): a vertical one 14 pixels wide and a horizontal one 12 pixels high, the horizontal one leaving room for the vertical one and starting after an `InsetLeft` (the editor's gutter), shown while the pointer is over the element and after a scroll, faded away 500 ms later (100 ms in, 800 ms out; a pointer over the element or a drag keeps them), the slider in the theme's slider, hover and active colors, a click on the track jumping the slider there (`ScrollByPage` pages instead), a drag that continues outside the scrollbar, `Visible`/`Hidden`/`Auto` per axis, and a shadow at the top and left of scrolled content. The editor is wrapped in it: `editorScroll` is its model (the controller's vertical position, the view model's horizontal one, the sizes the text layer measures in `SetTextMetrics`); the content width is the widest row laid out plus four characters (reset when the document or the font changes), `SetScrollLeft` stays within it, and `editor.scrollBeyondLastLine` was already in the vertical extent. Hit testing: `geometry.positionAt` (row from the projection, column nearest to x at a grapheme boundary) and `geometry.rectOf` (the rectangle of a character), exposed by `RenderEditorText.PositionAt` and `RectOf` in the layer's coordinates, scroll included. The column is counted in characters for plain ASCII without tabs in a monospace font (the fast path) and found otherwise by a binary search over the grapheme boundaries with the x of each from the paragraph's boxes.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/kit/{scrollbar_state.go,scrollable_element.go}`, `internal/gui/editor/{geometry.go,render.go,widget.go,viewmodel.go}`, tests `internal/gui/kit/{scrollbar_state_test.go,scrollable_element_test.go}`, `internal/gui/editor/{hittest_test.go,scrollbar_test.go}`, golden `internal/gui/editor/testdata/goldens/scrollbars.png`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.1.
- **Tests:** scrollbar math tables (ten slider cases, the desired positions of clicks, pages and drags, arrows, setters, a slider that cannot move); the element with a fake model: appearing with the pointer, staying under it, the delay and the fade (halfway through it too), appearing on a scroll and going away, no scrollbar without a need, the three visibilities, a vertical drag (also away from the scrollbar and past the end), hover and active colors, clicks on the track (jump, clamps, pages up and down, none on the slider), a drag that goes on from a click, the horizontal scrollbar (drag, click, the inset), shadows, a new model; hit testing tables (ASCII on the fast path with halves and clamps, tabs), the fast path equal to the paragraph at hundreds of x, Greek, CJK, emoji, a ZWJ family, a flag, combining marks and mixed text checked through the paragraph (always a grapheme boundary, a boundary's x gives it back, a quarter and three quarters into a character go to the nearer side), the rectangles of positions (tabs, two-byte characters, the end of a line, an emoji), rect and position round trip, hit testing in a scrolled window; in the editor: no scrollbar for short text, dragging the vertical and the horizontal scrollbar moves the text, clamps, the content width following the document, scrollbars appearing on a wheel scroll and fading, a golden with both scrollbars and the shadows (viewed). Coverage: `kit` 96.4%, `gui/editor` 92.0%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** no arrow buttons (`verticalHasArrows` is false in VS Code too), and the wheel is still Glutter's `Scrollable` (the editor's own wheel, horizontal wheel and smooth scrolling come with the input steps). The shadow is a 4-pixel gradient, not VS Code's inset box shadow. The width of the content is the widest row laid out since the last change, not a measure of the whole document (VS Code measures rendered lines too). The slider does not snap back when the pointer wanders far away (VS Code does that on Windows only). A `ScrollableElement` is not yet used by other widgets; the tree and list steps will.

## 3.5 Key dispatch

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-3.5-key-dispatch
- **Summary:** keys run commands through the window's dispatcher (SPEC §5.4). `app.KeyDispatcher` is a widget above the app (a root `Focus` node plus an early key handler of the focus manager, so a bound key is used before focus traversal and before the focused widget). `app.KeyOf` and `ChordOf` convert Glutter key events to `core/keybinding` chords (Latin letters and digits from the logical key, everything else and letters of non-Latin layouts from the physical key's US name, the numpad's Enter as Enter, modifiers from the hardware keyboard, key ups ignored). `Window` got `Dispatcher`, `Scopes` (`kit.FocusScopes`: which context key scope belongs to which focus node) and `OnChordStatus`; `Window.DispatchKey` resolves a chord in the scope of the focused widget, runs the command through the registry (`SourceKeybinding`), logs a failure and tells whether the key was used (an unbound key is not, so Glutter types its character). The editor (`editor.EditorOpts`: `Commands`, `Keys`, `Scopes`, `Autofocus`) has a focus node, its own context key scope (`editorTextFocus`, `editorFocus`, `textInputFocus`, `editorHasSelection`, `editorHasMultipleSelections`, `editorColumnSelection`, `editorReadonly`, `editorTabMovesFocus`, `editorLangId`) kept up to date with its selections and focus, and it binds the handlers of its commands while it has the focus (`editor.BindCommands`, on the registry's handler stack). `editor/commands.go` implements all 100 commands of milestone 1 on the view model (`actions`; `ViewModel.RunCommand`): movement and its Select twins (word, word part, page, line, document), column selection, cancelSelection, removeSecondaryCursors, expandLineSelection, select all, cursor undo and redo, type, tab, outdent, lineBreakInsert, the deletes (character, word, word part, inside word), undo and redo, the line commands, the cursor commands and the occurrence and smart-select sessions. Results are applied as one undo step with the selections before and after; a command that is not part of a session (column selection, occurrences, smart select) ends it; every command reveals the primary cursor (`SetScroller`, `RevealPrimary`, vertical only) and restarts the blink. `ViewModel` got the cursor history and the session state.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/app/{keys.go,window.go,app.go}`, `internal/gui/kit/focusscopes.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{commands.go,widget.go,viewmodel.go}`, tests `internal/gui/app/keys_test.go`, `internal/gui/kit/focusscopes_test.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{commands_test.go,commands_cover_test.go}`; `docs/SPEC.md` §5.4.
- **Tests:** every default keybinding of the keymap (all of them, in four situations: a cursor, a selection, several cursors, a column selection) sent as key events reaches its command; a table of keys with their effect on text and selections (tab, backspace, delete, select all, arrows, line commands, indent, add cursor, escape); key conversion (US, Dvorak, AZERTY digits, Greek and Cyrillic letters, punctuation on other layouts, numpad enter, modifiers, unnamed keys) and `ChordOf`; chords and modifier keys (pending chord, a lone Shift or Alt does not cancel it, status messages, a failed chord is used up); Greek `ψ` on the key of `]` runs `ctrl+]` and Greek `α` on the key of `a` runs `ctrl+a`; unbound printable keys are left to the text input; Tab is used by the editor and traversal does not move the focus; keys are resolved in the context of the focus (no editor key once the focus is elsewhere); the editor's context keys follow its selections, focus, read-only state and column selection; a read-only document keeps its text; handlers follow the focus. In the editor package: every registered command has a handler and every handler a registered command; 20 absolute movements, the Select twin of 22 movements from six starts, word parts, smart home, column selection (session and its end), selection commands, cursor undo and redo (and its end after an edit), typing and deleting (21 cases), delete inside word, undo and redo with the cursors, typing joined in one undo step, 17 line commands, the cursor commands, occurrence sessions, smart select (and the end of its session), read-only refusal (route asserted), an unknown command (route asserted), revealing the cursor, which layers are told, and binding and unbinding handlers (stacking of two editors). Coverage: `gui/editor` 91.0%, `gui/app` 83.9%, `kit` 96.6%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** unbound printable keys are only left alone here; typing them (the text input client, IME, the Enter key) is step 3.6, so there is no way to type a letter into the editor with the keyboard yet (the `type` command works). Enter has no default binding in VS Code either: it is typed. Revealing the cursor scrolls vertically only (horizontal reveal comes with the scrolling of 3.7). Clipboard, find, scroll and fold commands are not registered yet and so have no handlers. A key whose command fails is used up and logged, not shown to the user. The dispatcher does not know the `Alt`-graph or compose keys. An arrow key that no editor binding takes is still taken by Glutter's focus traversal (WidgetsApp's default shortcuts), as in any Glutter app.

## 3.6 Text input and IME

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-3.6-text-input-ime
- **Summary:** the editor takes text from the keyboard and from input methods through Glutter's text input (SPEC §9.3). `editor/input.go` has `textInput`, the editor's `DeltaTextInputClient`: it connects when the editor's focus node gets the focus (`EnableDeltaModel`, multiline, Enter adds a line) and closes when it loses it; the editing value it gives the input method is the current line of the primary cursor with line-local offsets (a selection over several lines is a caret at its active end), given again whenever that line, its text or the selection differs, so the input method's window follows the cursor from line to line. A delta becomes a document change: the text the input method holds apart from its composing range is compared with the line it was given, the difference is one replaced stretch (`alignedAffixes`, moved to character boundaries), and it is typed at every cursor: `Commands.Type` for a stretch that is the selection or a pure insert (a typed "\n" is a new line with the indentation), `Commands.CompositionType` with the characters before and after the cursor for a replacement around it (delete before, delete after, commit with surrounding deletion). The composing range is `ViewModel.Preedit` (`SetPreedit`), shown by the cursor layer as an underlined overlay at the primary cursor on the color of the editor, with a thicker underline under the part the method highlights and the cursor inside it; it never enters the document or the undo history and ends when the editor loses the focus. After every frame in which the editor changed, scrolled or moved, `SetEditableSizeAndTransform` (the text layer's size and its transform to the window) and `SetCaretRect` (the cursor's rectangle, or the method's cursor inside the preedit) are sent. Typing with keys works through Glutter's `HandleUnhandledKey`: a key that no binding takes and that has a character commits it. `EditorOpts.OnError` receives the errors of input (typing into a read-only document); after a refused change the input method is given the line the document still has.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/editor/{input.go,widget.go,overlay.go,viewmodel.go}`, tests `internal/gui/editor/input_test.go`, `internal/gui/app/keys_test.go`, golden `internal/gui/editor/testdata/goldens/preedit.png`; `docs/SPEC.md` §9.3; `docs/MANUAL-CHECKS.md`.
- **Tests:** the input method gets the line of the primary cursor, with its selection, and follows it when the cursor changes line and after edits (a selection over lines is a caret); `SendTextInputEvent` commits (a character, two-byte, CJK and emoji text, over a selection, at several cursors, over several selections, a new line with and without indentation), deletions around the cursor (before, after, a two-byte character, around, at every cursor, a replacement with surrounding deletion, the selection, before the start of the line), the preedit (shown and not typed, replaced by another, committed, a commit with a new preedit in one event, cancelled, over a selection, ended by losing the focus, never in the undo history), typing with `SendKeyEvent` and characters (Latin, space, Greek, symbols, Enter, Control and arrow keys type nothing, typing is one undo step), a read-only document (error with its route, the input method resynchronized), the caret rect in window coordinates (position, after moving, after scrolling, inside the preedit), `alignedAffixes` (11 cases including characters that share a first byte), a golden of the preedit (viewed), and through the whole app typing letters, Enter, Tab and Ctrl+Z. `TextInputStates()` is used to check the window and the caret rect. Coverage: `gui/editor` 91.2%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** pending, see docs/MANUAL-CHECKS.md (Greek layout and an IBus or Fcitx5 input method: does the candidate window follow the cursor)
- **Glutter issues:** none new (the step names the known one: `SetComposingRect` does not reach the platform, so the caret rect is the anchor)
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the preedit does not push the text after the cursor to the right; it covers it (VS Code shows the composition inline). A key that an input method consumes never reaches the key dispatcher, so shortcuts do not run while composing. Auto-closing brackets and quotes, and auto-indent beyond keeping the indentation, come with the language steps. A paste, a drop and the middle click come with the mouse and clipboard steps. The text typed with a key comes from Glutter's `HandleUnhandledKey` (the platform backend does not send text-input events for keys it has handed to the application), as its SPEC §8.6 says.

## 3.7 Mouse selection

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-3.7-mouse-selection
- **Summary:** the mouse selects text as in VS Code (SPEC §9.3). `core/edit/mouse.go` has the pure parts: `RangeSelection` (the selection from the range that was entered to a position), `Mover.WordSelect` and `WordSelectDrag` (double click and its drag, ported from `WordOperations.word`), `Mover.LineSelect` and `LineSelectDrag` (triple click, line numbers and their drag, ported from `CursorMoveCommands.line`); `Cursors` gained `LastAdded` and `Replace`, so that a drag keeps moving the cursor added last. `gui/editor/mouse.go` is `ViewModel.DispatchMouse`, a port of `ViewController.dispatchMouse`: a click moves the cursor; Shift extends; a double or triple click selects a word or a line and the drag extends by words or lines; four clicks select everything; `editor.multiCursorModifier` (`alt` or `ctrlCmd`) adds a cursor (clicking inside a cursor or selection removes it, as VS Code's toggle), a word or a line, and drags the last added cursor; Shift with it, Alt when it is not the modifier, or `editor.columnSelection` drag a column (a session shared with the keyboard's column commands), with the column taken from the pointer's x in characters of the font, also past the end of a line; a press on the line numbers selects lines. `gui/editor/pointer.go` is the widget side: a `Listener` on the row of gutter and text, click counting (400 ms, 5 pixels), the modifier keys read from the binding's `HardwareKeyboard`, drags that skip events inside one character, and a `MouseRegion` over the text with the cursor of `editor.mouseStyle`. The three settings are registered (`editor.multiCursorModifier`, `editor.columnSelection`, `editor.mouseStyle`) and read into `Options`.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/edit/{mouse.go,cursor.go}`, `internal/gui/editor/{mouse.go,pointer.go,widget.go,geometry.go,render.go,viewmodel.go,options.go}`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`; tests `internal/core/edit/{mouse_test.go,cursors_test.go}`, `internal/gui/editor/{mouse_test.go,options_test.go}`, golden `internal/gui/editor/testdata/goldens/mouse_column_selection.png`; `docs/SPEC.md` §9.3.

The middle button, the right button and `editor.doubleClickSelectsBlock` are not handled here: middle-click paste and dragging text come with 3.8, the context menu with the workbench. Ctrl+click stays free for go-to-definition (area 3). Dragging past the edges does not scroll yet (3.8). A touch drag scrolls (Scrollable) and does not select.

## 3.8 Scrolling and text drag

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-3.8-scrolling
- **Summary:** the editor scrolls with the mouse wheel and the touchpad, drags selected text, pastes with the middle button, and scrolls while a selection is dragged past an edge (SPEC §9.3). `editor/scroll.go`: `pointerSignal` claims a wheel event through the `PointerSignalResolver` when it would move the editor; `wheelDelta` is VS Code's rule (50 pixels a notch of Glutter's 53, `editor.mouseWheelScrollSensitivity`, Alt with `editor.fastScrollSensitivity`, Shift to the side, rounded away from zero); a touchpad pan (`PointerPanZoomUpdate`) scrolls 1:1 with the same multipliers; `editor.smoothScrolling` animates the vertical wheel scroll over 125 ms (ease-out cubic) with a per-frame `JumpTo`, and further notches add to the target; the `Scrollable` is wrapped in a `ScrollConfiguration` in which only touch and stylus drag. `autoScroll` follows the pointer past an edge frame by frame at VS Code's speed (`scrollSpeed`), moving the end of the selection to the edge line (`edgePosition`) with the cursor reveal off (`MouseEvent.NoReveal`). `editor/drop.go` and `pointer.go`: a click on the primary selection arms a drag of its text (`textDrag`), the drop caret (`ViewModel.DropCaret`, drawn dotted by the cursor layer), the copy/default pointer while dragging, Escape to cancel (a `HardwareKeyboard` handler for the time of the press), and the drop (`ViewModel.drop`) with `edit.Commands.DragAndDrop` (ported from `DragAndDropCommand`; one undo step; the dropped text is selected). The middle button places the cursor and on release pastes the primary selection (`ViewModel.PasteText` with the new `edit.Commands.Paste`), or selects a column when `editor.selectionClipboard` is off. Settings registered and read into `Options`: `editor.mouseWheelScrollSensitivity`, `editor.fastScrollSensitivity`, `editor.smoothScrolling`, `editor.dragAndDrop`, `editor.selectionClipboard`. `EditorOpts.Context` is the context of the clipboard read (the window's).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/edit/{drag.go,paste.go}`, `internal/gui/editor/{scroll.go,drop.go,pointer.go,mouse.go,widget.go,overlay.go,render.go,commands.go,options.go,viewmodel.go}`, `internal/gui/app/app.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`; tests `internal/core/edit/{drag_test.go,paste_test.go}`, `internal/gui/editor/{scroll_test.go,drop_test.go,mouse_test.go,options_test.go}`, golden `internal/gui/editor/testdata/goldens/drop_caret.png`; `docs/SPEC.md` §9.3.
- **Tests:** `DragAndDrop` (15 cases: moves right, left, of a reversed selection, copies, copies beside the selection, lines down and up, on the line the selection ends on, dropped inside, at its own edge) and `Paste`; in the window: `ScrollAt` (a notch, several, back, high-resolution deltas, the ends of the document, sensitivity, Alt, Shift and sideways wheels, over the gutter; never a double scroll), trackpad pans (1:1, diagonal, sensitivity), smooth scrolling with the fake clock (starts at the top, halfway, at the end, adds up over several notches, off moves at once, a pan is not animated), `scrollSpeed` bands, a drag below, above, left and right of the editor (scrolls, selects up to the edge, stops at the top, stops when the pointer comes back, stops on release), dragging text (move, backwards, Control copy, one undo step, dropped on the selection, the drop caret shows and hides, a press without a move places the cursor, Shift extends, other selections stay, many selections, Escape cancels, dropped outside, `editor.dragAndDrop` off, Alt, double click, press past the end of a line, a read-only document, `editor.columnSelection`, the copy and move pointers), the middle button (pastes the primary selection and not the clipboard, an empty primary selection, a drag does not paste, a read-only document reports an error, a column without `editor.selectionClipboard`), a golden of the drop caret (viewed). Coverage: `gui/editor` 92.2%, `core/edit` 94.5%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none new (`Scrollable` ignores pointer events while its own animation runs, as Flutter's does; the editor avoids it by animating with `JumpTo`)
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the animation covers the wheel only (not the reveal of the cursor, which VS Code also animates), and sideways scrolling is immediate. A touchpad pan has no fling (the compositor ends the gesture; Glutter reports no inertia). The editor does not scroll horizontally to the cursor yet. The middle click pastes on release, as GTK does, where the browser pastes on its own schedule. The primary selection is not set when the selection changes (3.9). `editor.mouseWheelZoom`, `editor.scrollOnMiddleClick` and `editor.doubleClickSelectsBlock` are not implemented.

## 3.9 Clipboard

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-3.9-clipboard
- **Summary:** copy, cut and paste (SPEC §9.3). `core/edit/clipboard.go`: `Commands.Copy` (ported from `getPlainTextToCopy`: the selections in the order of the text, the line of an empty selection once per line, the pieces kept for a paste by as many cursors), `Commands.Cut` (`DeleteOperations.cut`), `Commands.Paste` with `PasteOptions` (`PasteOperation`: the same text at every cursor, the pieces of a copy, `editor.multiCursorPaste` spread, a block pasted down the lines (`ColumnSelectionPasteCommand`), a line copied from an empty selection pasted above the cursor's line, overtype mode); it replaces the plain paste of 3.8. `gui/editor/clipboard.go`: the `Clipboard` interface (clipboard and primary selection) with the Glutter implementation, `ClipboardMemory` (how the last copy was made, matched by its text; the `Window` has one for all its editors), `ViewModel.SetClipboard`, the actions of the three commands, `PasteText` (consults the memory) and `PastePrimary` (the middle button of 3.8 now goes through the same paste). The paste runs in the clipboard's callback on the UI thread as one command; copy leaves the column, occurrence and expansion sessions alone and does not scroll (`keepSessions`, `skipReveal` of `Run`). The primary selection is set by a 100 ms timer after the selections change, when all cursors have selections and the editor has the focus. Registered: the three commands, their keys (Ctrl+X, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V, Shift+Insert) and the settings `editor.multiCursorPaste` and `editor.emptySelectionClipboard`.

## 3.10 Editor display options

- **Date:** 2026-10-23

- **Summary:** the display settings of SPEC §3.1 that need no later step, all applied live (the shell already re-reads `editor.*`). New settings and options: `editor.fontWeight` (`ParseFontWeight`: normal, bold or 1 to 1000), `editor.letterSpacing`, `editor.cursorStyle`, `editor.mouseWheelZoom`; `fontWeight` and `letterSpacing` are in the text style and in the paragraph cache key. Cursor styles (`overlay.go`, `paintCursor`): `line`, `line-thin`, `block`, `underline`, `underline-thin`, `block-outline`, as `viewCursor.ts` and `viewCursors.css` (a block covers one character cell, also at the end of a line and on a tab, with the character redrawn in the opposite color of the cursor, which is what VS Code does without an `editorCursor.background`; a line wider than 2 pixels shows its character the same way). Blinking: `smooth`, `phase` and `expand` are real animations now (`blink.go`: `animationFrame` ports the CSS keyframes — half a second shown, then 20 half-second iterations, every other one backwards, ease-in-out; `smooth` and `phase` fade, `expand` shrinks the height towards the middle), driven by a frame loop of the widget (`startCursorAnimation`, restarted by every move or edit and stopped when the animation ends, the focus goes or the style changes); `blink` and `solid` are as before. Zoom (`zoom.go`): `Zoom` is the level (-5 to 20) shared by the editors of a window (`Window.Zoom`), `zoomed` scales the font and line height by `1 + level/10` over the options of the settings (`ViewModel.base` and `opts`), the commands `editor.action.fontZoomIn`, `fontZoomOut` and `fontZoomReset` are registered and implemented, and Control with the wheel (a notch per level, the level goes by the delta for high-resolution wheels) or with a touchpad pan (200 pixels a level) zooms when `editor.mouseWheelZoom` is on. `editor.fontFamily`, `fontSize`, `lineHeight` and `fontLigatures` were done in 3.2.

## 3.11 Performance check

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-3.11-performance-check
- **Summary:** measured the targets of SPEC §14 marked 3.11. Go benchmarks (`internal/gui/editor/bench_test.go`: typing a character in a 10,000-line file, scrolling a page of a 100,000-line file, opening 10 MB) and `scripts/perf.sh [--session] [-n runs] [-s seconds]`, which builds with `-tags glutter_release,emetos_dev`, generates the files in a temporary folder, runs each measurement in an isolated app (`scripts/isolated.sh`) with `GLUTTER_FRAME_STATS=1` under a headless sway (or in the session with `--session`), drives it with dev commands, and prints a table of frame times, cold start (median of N starts, with Glutter's `counter` as baseline), idle CPU, RSS and the 10 MB open. New dev commands for it (registered with `emetos_dev` builds and debug builds only, SPEC §15.2): `dev.type <text>` (one character per frame) and `dev.scroll {rows, frames}` (scrolls a number of rows per frame without moving the cursor), implemented in `gui/editor/dev.go` through a `framer` the widget gives the view model, and registered in the process with a fallback that reports `DevNoEditor` when no editor has the focus. Results and method: `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`.
- **Results (headless, software rendering):** typing 0.28 ms build on average and 0.90 ms worst (< 4 ms) ✅; scrolling 0.56 ms and 2.2 ms worst (< 8 ms) ✅; open 10 MB: 30 ms more than the empty window ✅ (the first frame is 890 ms from the process start); idle focused 0.5–0.8% of a core ✅; memory 271 MB against Glutter's counter at 287 MB ✅ against the revised target; **cold start 860 ms against < 400 ms ✗** — Glutter's counter takes 940 ms in the same run, 0.85 s of it is Glutter's first draw (renderer, GL context, shaders, with a failed Vulkan probe first), and Emetos builds its first frame in 0.05 ms.
- **Main files:** `scripts/perf.sh`, `internal/gui/editor/{dev.go,bench_test.go,dev_test.go,widget.go,viewmodel.go,commands.go}`, `internal/gui/app/{devcommands.go,process.go,devcommands_test.go}`, `docs/{PERFORMANCE.md,GLUTTER-ISSUES.md,SPEC.md}`.
- **Tests:** the two dev commands in an editor with a fake framer (typing one character per frame, the cursor and the text after it, scrolling by rows and by frames, the arguments' errors with their routes, no reveal of the cursor undoing the scroll), and through the process (`TestTheEditorDevCommandsAreInTheProcess`: the commands are registered, with `DevNoEditor` when no editor has the focus). The cover test of the commands skips the `dev.` ids (they are not bound to keys).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** pending, see docs/MANUAL-CHECKS.md (`scripts/perf.sh --session`, the numbers with the GPU decide SPEC §14)
- **Glutter issues:** two entries in `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`: the cold start (first draw 0.85 s in a software-GL headless run), and the headless compositor giving windows no keyboard focus events (the unfocused idle target cannot be measured headless).
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the cold start is not met headless and the step says to ask the user whether to go on; the instruction for this run was to continue without asking, and SPEC §14 says headless numbers do not decide, so the work went on, with the `--session` run as the check that decides — **tell me if you want to stop at this point instead**. The memory target of SPEC §14 was revised from "< 200 MB" to "Glutter's empty window plus 50 MB" as the step allows ("revisited against Glutter's own baseline"). The unfocused-idle target is verified by the fake-platform tests of 3.10 only, and by `--session`. Typing and scrolling are measured without syntax highlighting; 4.9 repeats typing with it.

## 4.1 Regular expression translation

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.1-regex-translation
- **Summary:** `core/regex` compiles Oniguruma patterns (SPEC §8.2). `regex.Compile(pattern, Options)` parses a pattern into a tree (`parse.go`) and writes it out in regexp2's syntax (`emit.go`), instead of rewriting the text, because several features need to know the extent of an atom or the groups of the pattern: possessive quantifiers become atomic groups, named groups become numbered ones (regexp2 numbers them after the others; vscode-oniguruma numbers all groups by their parentheses), `(?i)`/`(?x)`/`(?m)` have Oniguruma's scope (to the end of the group, alternatives included) and `(?m)` is dot-all, `^` is the start of a line that is not after a final break, classes with nested classes, `&&`, POSIX brackets and `\p{..}` (POSIX names, categories, scripts from Go's tables) are written as regexp2 classes or lookahead compositions, `\h \H \R \X \N \O \x{A B} \o{}`, `\K` (an empty marker group; `Find` moves the start), the absence operator, conditionals, the odd interval rules of the Ruby syntax (`{n,m}+`, `{n}?`, `{,m}`, `{3,1}`), and calls of groups (`\g<name>`, numbers, relative, `\g<0>`), which are written out as copies of the group to a depth of 6 (the deepest copy never matches) and shallower when the pattern would pass 256 KB beyond its source. `Options` has the syntax, ignore case, the timeout, and `DisableA`/`DisableG` for the scanner of 4.2; `Regexp.Find(runes, from)` gives rune spans and fails with `RegexTimeout`. `regex.Cache` memoizes compilations, failures included (no global cache: the tokenizer owns one). The ECMAScript syntax (`ecma.go`) uses regexp2's ECMAScript option with the group numbering and `[^]`/`[]` of JavaScript; step 9.8 and 4.5 extend it if they need more. regexp2 v1.12.0 is added as a direct dependency (allowed, only in this package).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/regex/{regex.go,parse.go,emit.go,charset.go,props.go,ecma.go}`; tests `translate_test.go`, `corpus_test.go`, `fuzz_test.go`; `docs/SPEC.md` §8.2; `go.mod`.
- **Tests:** 190 subtests in all: table cases of patterns against texts with the expected spans (anchors and `DisableA`/`DisableG`, escapes, options and their scope, repetition rules, POSIX/property/class cases, groups and references, conditionals, `\K`, group calls including recursion and its depth limit, absence, rune offsets) and 33 errors, each with its own tag and a route check (`RegexUnbalancedParen`, `RegexUnterminatedClass`, `RegexNothingToRepeat`, `RegexInvalidEscape`, `RegexInvalidGroup`, `RegexInvalidGroupName`, `RegexInvalidRange`, `RegexUnknownGroup`, `RegexUnknownProperty`, `RegexUnsupportedFeature`, `RegexCompileFailed`, `RegexTooLarge`, `RegexTimeout`); ECMAScript cases; the cache; `FuzzCompile` (no panic, bounded time; 7 million inputs run once). The corpus test walks every `match`, `begin`, `end` and `while` string of the 85 JSON grammars under `reference/vscode/extensions/*/syntaxes/` and `reference/vscode-go/extension/syntaxes/` (5,900 distinct patterns; `\N` in an `end` or `while` is replaced by sample text, as the tokenizer will replace it by the captured text) and requires all to compile: **no pattern is unsupported**, so the list of expected failures is empty (the test fails when a pattern on the list compiles, and when one off the list fails). It is skipped when `reference/` is missing. The slowest compile is about 30 ms (a 280 KB pattern); all 5,900 take 0.4 s. Coverage 94.7%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the one grammar in plist format (`reference/vscode/extensions/javascript/syntaxes/Regular Expressions (JavaScript).tmLanguage`) is not part of the corpus: the plan names the JSON grammars and the loader of 4.3 reads JSON. Known approximations: the absence operator `(?~x)` is "any characters that do not start x", which differs only for text that ends inside a match of x; group calls do not update the captures of the called group and are limited in depth; `\w`, `\d`, `\b` and the POSIX classes use .NET's Unicode categories (`[:alpha:]` is letters, letter numbers and marks); `\X` is an approximation of a grapheme cluster. The comparison with the real engine is by the behavior tests above and, from step 4.3 on, the first-mate cases of vscode-textmate.

## 4.2 Scanner and limits

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.2-scanner
- **Summary:** the multi-pattern scanner of SPEC §8.2 (`core/regex/scanner.go`). `NewScanner(patterns, ScannerOptions{Cache, Timeout})` compiles every pattern (through a `Cache` when given); a pattern that fails to compile never matches and `Scanner.Err(i)` has its error (with the tag of 4.1). `NewLine(text)` converts a line to runes once and keeps the byte offset of every rune (invalid UTF-8 bytes are U+FFFD, one rune each; ASCII lines keep no offset table); `Scanner.Find(line, pos, anchorPos, isFirstLine)` returns the match that starts first, ties to the lowest pattern index (it stops at the first match that starts at `pos`, as vscode-oniguruma does), with captures as byte offsets (`{-1,-1}` for a group that did not match). `\G` is allowed when `pos == anchorPos` and `\A` on the first line (`FindOption.NotBeginPosition`/`NotBeginString` of vscode-textmate, resolved as `compileAG` does): the scanner compiles a variant of the patterns that use these anchors with `DisableA`/`DisableG` (4.1) the first time a line needs it, behind a mutex, and shares the rest. The limit is 100 ms (`ScanTimeout`, a field of the options for tests) for each search and for the call as a whole; past it, `Find` fails with `RegexTimeout`.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/regex/scanner.go`; tests `scanner_test.go`; `docs/SPEC.md` §8.2.
- **Tests:** 26 cases of `Find` (the earliest start beats the first pattern, ties, the start at `pos`, no match, no patterns, `pos` past the end, an empty match at the end, unmatched groups, byte offsets after multi-byte text, `pos` in bytes, a four-byte character, an invalid byte, bytes after invalid bytes, a truncated sequence, `\G` at the anchor, elsewhere and with no anchor, a pattern without the anchor beating one with it, `\A` on the first line and on another, `\A` with `\G`, a lookbehind before `pos`, a pattern that does not compile, `^` after a break), each run with and without a cache; the errors of patterns that do not compile (with their tags); the offsets of `Line` for ASCII, empty, two-byte and invalid text; a catastrophic pattern `(a+)+$` times out with the default limit (checked to take under 3 s) and with a shorter one (route `Find.RegexTimeout`); concurrent use from 8 goroutines under the race detector. Coverage of the package 96.6%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the step names `Find(line, pos, anchorPos, isFirstLine) (Match, bool)`; `line` is a `*Line` (so that the conversion to runes is made once per line and not once per search) and there is an `error` result for the timeout. The anchor position `-1` means none. `\z` and `\Z` are not made to fail on a line that is not the last (`NotEndString` is never set by vscode-textmate).

## 4.3 TextMate tokenizer

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.3-textmate-tokenizer
- **Summary:** the grammar model and the core of the line tokenizer (SPEC §8.3), a port of vscode-textmate's `rule.ts`, `grammar.ts` and `tokenizeString.ts`. `rawgrammar.go`: `ParseGrammar` reads a JSON grammar into `RawGrammar`/`RawRule` (all the keys of the format, injections in file order for 4.4; errors have the tag `GrammarInvalid`). `grammar.go`: the `Registry` interface (scope name → `*RawGrammar`, which 4.5 implements), `NewGrammar`, the rule factory (`compileRule`, captures, patterns with every kind of `include`: `$self`, `$base`, `#name`, `scope`, `scope#name`; the `$base` of an included grammar is the grammar tokenized; a rule whose patterns are all missing is left out; a rule is compiled once for all the places that include it), `collectPatterns` and the scanner of a rule (`regex.Scanner` of 4.2 over the patterns of the rule with its end pattern first or last by `applyEndPatternLast`; an end with `\1` references gets a scanner of its own for each text it was completed with). `rule.go`: `regexSource` (the `\z` replacement, back references completed with the escaped captured text) and `template` for names with `$1` and `${1:/upcase}`. `tokenize.go`: `Grammar.TokenizeLine(line, prev, timeLimit)`, the scan loop with begin/end, match rules, captures (including captures with patterns, which tokenize the captured text again, and nested captures with their local stack), contentName, the anchor position and the beginning of a line after a begin that matched the line break, and the endless-loop guards of vscode-textmate. `state.go`: `State`, a persistent list that is `Equal` when the open rules, their end patterns and the scopes are (the positions the tokenizer keeps in a state are tied to the line that made them, so a state is never changed after it is returned and can be shared by threads); `scopes.go`: the persistent `ScopeStack`. The limit of `editor.maxTokenizationLineLength` (20,000 bytes by default) makes a line one token without scopes; a pattern past its 100 ms ends the line as plain text.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/textmate/{rawgrammar.go,grammar.go,rule.go,tokenize.go,state.go,scopes.go}`; tests `firstmate_test.go`, `tokenize_test.go`, `grammar_test.go`; `docs/SPEC.md` §8.3.
- **Tests:** the first-mate cases of `reference/vscode-textmate/test-cases/first-mate/tests.json`: 59 of 64 are run, with the tokens compared as text (value and scopes of each); **5 are skipped** (#42, #45, #47, #49, #64) because their grammars use injections (step 4.4). 28 table cases of tokenizer features (match, begin/end across lines, contentName, names with several scopes and with captures and their commands, captures nested, begin/end captures and the `captures` default, an empty `beginCaptures`, a capture with patterns, back references in `end` and their escaping, `applyEndPatternLast`, a begin without an end, the anchor, every kind of include, a repository inside a rule, includes that are not there, a rule with all its patterns missing, a pattern that does not compile, a rule that does not advance, a begin that matches the line break); 13 `ParseGrammar` errors with their route and every field read; `NewGrammar` of a missing grammar; scope stacks; state equality (9 cases, both ways); the long-line limit; the time limit; a pattern past its limit (route `RegexTimeout` in `Problems`); problems reported once (`RegexUnbalancedParen`, `RegexUnknownProperty`, `RuleWhileUnsupported`); 8 goroutines tokenizing with one grammar under the race detector; includes of other grammars; back reference escaping and `\z`. Coverage 97.7%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** `begin`/`while` rules are 4.4's: until then such a rule is compiled as one with its patterns missing, so it is left out of the rules that include it, and `Problems` has a `RuleWhileUnsupported` (a `TODO(step 4.4)` marks the place). The same for injections, the standard token type and the embedded language of tokens (`TODO(step 4.4)` where the scan looks for rules). The suite1 cases of vscode-textmate need plist grammars and are not part of the step. The tokens are not merged when their scopes are equal (vscode-textmate merges them only after the theme gives them the same style, which Emetos does when it resolves the styles). The scan loop has a guard of `4 * length + 64` rounds for a grammar that goes on without advancing in a way the checks of vscode-textmate do not see. The cycle of include-only rules that include each other is cut when patterns are collected (vscode-textmate overflows its stack).

## 4.4 TextMate: while, injections, embedded languages

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.4-textmate-while-injections
- **Summary:** the rest of the tokenizer (SPEC §8.3). `begin`/`while` rules (`ruleBeginWhile`, `Grammar.compiledWhile`; `tokenizer.checkWhile` checks the while patterns of the open rules at the start of each line, outermost first, closing the rule whose pattern fails and taking its `whileCaptures`; a while pattern with `\1` is completed with the text of the begin like an end pattern). `matcher.go`: scope selectors (`L:`/`R:`, `-`, `,`, `|`, parentheses) and the matcher of names that match a scope or its prefix before a dot. Injections (`attributes.go`, `tokenizer.scan`): the `injections` of the grammar in file order and, through the new `Registry.Injections(scope)`, the grammars injected into it with their `injectionSelector`, sorted by priority; they are looked for after the rule of the state and win when they start earlier or, with `L:`, at the same place. Embedded grammars through the registry were in 4.3 (an include of a scope name); what 4.4 adds is that each token has its **type** (comment, string, regex, other; `meta.embedded` resets to other; `GrammarOptions.TokenTypes` selectors override) and its **language id** (`GrammarOptions.EmbeddedLanguages`, `InitialLanguage`), kept in each `ScopeStack` node by `Grammar.push` and read by `lineTokens.produce`. `Token` gained `Type` and `Language`; scope stacks are now made by the grammar (`pushPath`). The `TODO(step 4.4)` marks of 4.3 are gone.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/textmate/{matcher.go,attributes.go,grammar.go,rule.go,tokenize.go,scopes.go}`; tests `features_test.go`, `fuzz_test.go`, `firstmate_test.go`, `grammar_test.go`; `docs/SPEC.md` §8.3.
- **Tests:** all **64 of 64** first-mate cases (the 5 that 4.3 skipped for their injections now run, with the injections the tests give through the registry), the **9 while cases** of vscode-textmate (`suite1/whileTests.json`, run on its `whileLang` grammar written as JSON; its file is a plist), the **27 selector cases** of `matcher.test.ts` plus priorities and prefix matching of names, 4 begin/while feature cases (a quote that lasts while lines start with `>`, a while pattern with a reference to the begin, while captures, rules inside a while rule), 3 injection cases (priorities, earlier wins, injection grammars from the registry with unknown and missing ones), token types and languages (the table of scope names, 5 lines through a grammar with strings, embedded expressions, regexes, embedded languages by prefix and a `tokenTypes` selector, a long line), `FuzzTokenizeLine` over a grammar with while, backreferences, captures with patterns, includes of itself, empty matches, a begin that takes the line break and injections (800,000 inputs run once: no panic, tokens contiguous over the line, bounded time) and `FuzzParseGrammar` (6 million). Coverage 97.7%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the balanced-bracket selectors of vscode-textmate (`balancedBracketSelectors`) are not ported; bracket pair colorization (step 5.x) decides how to tell brackets in strings and comments. The `Registry` interface grew `Injections` (a change to what 4.3 defined; 4.5 implements it from the `injectTo` of grammar contributions). The scopes of a token are looked at by the selectors as `[]string` made once for each scan step (cached while the scopes do not change).

## 4.5 Languages and configurations

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.5-languages
- **Summary:** `core/language` (SPEC §8.1). `manifest.go`: `ParseManifest` reads the languages and grammars of a `package.json` with VS Code's validation (a contribution that is not valid is left out and listed as `LanguageContributionInvalid`/`GrammarContributionInvalid`). `registry.go`: `Registry`, filled by `AddExtension(fs.FS)`; several extensions that contribute to one language id are merged as `languagesRegistry.ts` does (aliases and display name, extensions with the configuration-first order, MIME type `text/x-<id>` by default, file names, patterns, configuration files); lookups by id, name (alias, any case) and MIME type; language numbers (0 none, 1 plain text, then in order) for the language of tokens; it implements `textmate.Registry` (`Grammar` reads and parses the grammar file the first time and remembers a failure; `Injections` includes the injections of the scopes that start the scope, as `TMGrammarFactory`); `NewGrammar(language, cache)` makes the tokenizer with the `embeddedLanguages` (of the grammar and of the grammars injected into it, only for registered languages) and `tokenTypes` of its contribution. `detect.go`: `Detect(path, firstLine)` is VS Code's `languagesAssociations.ts` (the user's associations, then file name, glob, extension; the first line last; last registered wins and the longest glob or extension; globs with a slash match the whole path; case ignored; a first line that matches the empty text is left out; BOM and end of line handled) and `SetFileAssociations(value)` takes the value of `files.associations`. `configuration.go`: the types of a language configuration (comments, brackets, auto-closing pairs with `notIn` and `beforeText`, surrounding and colorized pairs, `autoCloseBefore`, word pattern, indentation rules, on-enter rules, folding with `offSide` and markers) and `ParseConfiguration` (JSONC through `core/jsonc`; strings or `{pattern, flags}` compiled by `core/regex` in ECMAScript mode; what is not valid is left out and listed, the rest kept), `Configuration.Merge`, and `Registry.Configuration(language)` which merges the configuration files of a language in order. Changes elsewhere: `core/regex` ECMAScript mode now has the `Unicode` (`u`) and `DotAll` (`s`) flags, with `\p{..}` property escapes (general categories, scripts, `Script=` forms, `Alphabetic`, `Number`, `Nonspacing_Mark`) for the markdown word pattern; `core/textmate` ignores the notes among the rules of a repository or a `captures` object (`"comment": "..."`), as vscode-textmate does; `files.associations` is registered in `core/settings` (VS Code's description, object, default `{}`).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/language/{manifest.go,registry.go,detect.go,configuration.go,doc.go}`; tests `registry_test.go`, `detect_test.go`, `configuration_test.go`, `grammar_test.go`; `internal/core/regex/ecma.go`, `internal/core/textmate/rawgrammar.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`; `docs/SPEC.md` §8.1.
- **Tests:** the manifest (valid fields and 11 invalid contributions with their tags); merging of languages, lookups, codes, `AddExtension` errors; detection (23 cases: extension, case, file name before extension, glob before extension, the longest extension `.d.ts`, last registered wins, globs with and without a slash, plain text, first line with and without a BOM and end of line, a first line that matches everything, the path before the first line) and `files.associations` (8 cases, with a language that is not registered, a value that is not a string, replacing the associations); `ParseConfiguration` reading every field, 38 problem cases each with its tag and field, the merge, the merged configuration of a registry with unreadable and invalid files; **every language configuration file of VS Code's extensions (53 files) and of vscode-go (1) loads with no problem**, with all their regular expressions compiled in ECMAScript mode, and the Go configuration is checked field by field; the registry as a `textmate.Registry`: a host grammar with an embedded guest grammar, an injected grammar with its own embedded languages, token types and language numbers of tokens, grammars that are not registered, not valid or not there (tags and routes), injections of the parent scopes; and with the real extensions go, markdown-basics, json, yaml and typescript-basics: detection by name, extension and first line, the Go tokenizer, Go code in a Markdown fenced block tokenized as the Go language, and the Go configuration. Coverage `language` 99.2%.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the user's associations with the same length of pattern are tried in alphabetical order, as the setting is a map without an order (VS Code tries them in the order of the file). The grammar of `source.js.regexp` is a plist file in the JavaScript extension; the registry reads JSON grammars only, so that file is reported as `GrammarFileInvalid` in `Problems` until `update-assets.sh` converts it (step 4.6b). The `Configuration` types hold regular expressions as `regex.Regexp` and do not yet have the runtime pieces VS Code builds on them (`StandardAutoClosingPairConditional`, auto-closing pair indexes): the steps that use them (5.x) add those. `balancedBracketScopes` and `unbalancedBracketScopes` of grammar contributions are not read.

## 4.7 Theme loading and token colors

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.7-themes
- **Summary:** `core/theme` (SPEC §8.4). `rules.go`, `trie.go`, `theme.go`, `color.go` port vscode-textmate's `theme.ts`: `ParseRules` (comma lists, descendant parts, invalid colors dropped, font styles), `NewTokenTheme` (defaults, sorted rules, the trie of scope parts with parent-scope rules, the color map) and `TokenTheme.Match` (the most specific rule whose parent scopes fit, `>` child combinators). `Resolve` merges the rule of every prefix of a token's scopes onto the defaults, as the tokenizer's `mergeAttributes` does. `file.go` loads theme files as `colorThemeData.ts` does (JSON with comments through `core/jsonc`; `include` chains, `colors` with `default`, inline `tokenColors` or a `.tmTheme` file, `semanticHighlighting`, `semanticTokenColors` stored, the old `settings` format with `themeCompatibility.ts`'s mapping of settings to colors); `resolve.go` builds the token rules with the editor's default rule, custom rules and the `token.*-token` defaults. `bundled.go` embeds the themes (`scripts/update-assets.sh` copies the eight files, the manifest and its translations) and lists the six offered; `user.go` reads `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/emetos/themes/` (`storage.Paths.ThemesDir`). The plist reader moved to its own package, `core/plist`, which `plist2json` and the `.tmTheme` loader share.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/theme/{color,rules,trie,theme,file,resolve,bundled,user}.go` and tests, `internal/core/plist/`, `internal/core/language/internal/plist2json/`, `internal/core/storage/paths.go`, `scripts/update-assets.sh`, `NOTICE`, `docs/SPEC.md` §5.2, §8.4.

The big fixtures of `themes.test.ts` (tokenizing files with `.tmTheme` themes of many languages) are not ported: they need grammars that are not bundled, and the oracle above covers the same ground with VS Code's own results.

## 4.9 Tokenization in documents and highlighted painting

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.9-tokenization
- **Summary:** `core/tokens` (SPEC §8.3): a per-document `Tokenizer` with per-line tokens and end states, invalidation from the first edited line, early stop on an equal end state, `Ensure` (synchronous, budgeted), `Run` (background, 5 ms slices on snapshots, stale results dropped by a generation counter), `SetGrammar`, `OnChange`. Tokens are compact (`End`, interned `ScopeID`, language, type; `Scopes` is a trie). `language.Registry` gives `NewGrammarWith`, `GrammarSettings` and `HasGrammar`; `editor.maxTokenizationLineLength` is registered. `kit.Theme` carries an optional `TokenTheme` (`TokenThemeOf`), `gui/app` builds it from the theme service. The editor builds spans from tokens plus the theme, caches paragraphs with the spans in the key, and repaints the lines that the background loop finishes. `core/regex` gained a per-(line, scanner, pattern) cache of the leftmost match (valid for later start positions up to the match start; a "no match" stays valid; patterns with a live `\G` are not cached), which made tokenization 2.5× faster.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/tokens/`, `internal/core/regex/scanner.go`, `internal/core/textmate/scopes.go` (`ScopeStack.Parent`), `internal/core/language/registry.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `internal/gui/app/{theme,process,window,app}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{highlight,tokens,text,cache,render,widget,viewmodel}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.3, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`.
- **Tests:** a fuzz-style test comparing incremental tokenization after random edits with a fresh tokenization (Go, Markdown, JSON); stale results dropped; early stop; `SetGrammar`; `Ensure` budgets; scope interning; the grammar settings and routes; scanner cache cases (including `\G` and "no match"); all oracles (language and theme) still pass; editor widget tests (spans per token, theme change, repaint after background work). Goldens `highlighted_go.png` and `highlighted_go_light.png` in `internal/gui/editor/testdata/goldens/` (both looked at). Benchmarks: `BenchmarkTokenizeGoLines` (about 160 µs per line), `BenchmarkTypeCharacter10kLinesHighlighted` (0.62 ms), `BenchmarkScrollPage100kLinesHighlighted` (8.2 ms for a page of unseen lines).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none

- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** measuring and hit-testing use paragraphs without spans (the font is the same for every token, so the layout is identical). The first view of unseen lines in a very large file spends up to the 6 ms synchronous budget and exceeds the 8 ms scrolling target slightly (8.2 ms); the remaining lines are tokenized in the background and painted when ready. The remaining cost is regexp2's word-boundary and character-class lookups. An earlier run of the scroll benchmark with `-benchtime 2000x` ran out of memory (it tokenizes new lines each iteration); the notes in `PERFORMANCE.md` say to use a small count.
