## 4.11 More languages: systems and git

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.11-systems-git
- **Summary:** C, C++ (and CUDA C++), Rust, Diff, Git commit, Git rebase, Ignore files and the Go module files (`go.mod`, `go.work`, `go.sum`) are bundled. `copy_extension` now copies a `languages/` folder too (git-base keeps its language configurations there). The Go module grammars come from `golang/vscode-go` (v0.59.0): `scripts/update-assets.sh` writes the extension `go-mod` from its two grammars and its language configuration, with a `package.json` that names only the three languages (`go.work` uses the `go.mod` grammar, scope `go.mod`); the license is in `third_party/vscode-go/`. All grammars have permissive licenses (cgmanifest.json: MIT, TextMate bundle license), so none was left out.
- **A fix in `core/regex` that the C++ grammar needed:** one pattern of the grammar (a function definition) took 130 to 200 ms on a line such as `puts("\n***…")`, doubling for every two `*`, so the 100 ms limit stopped the line. The pattern has a loop over `&` and `*`, each followed by the grammar's whitespace group, which ends in the empty alternatives `(?<=\W)|(?=\W)|^|…`: each `*` can be followed by an empty match in several ways, and a failed search tries every combination. Oniguruma 6.9.9 and later remembers where a search has failed; regexp2 does not. `emitter.alternation` now writes a run of neighbouring branches that consume nothing and capture nothing as one atomic group, which cannot change the result (after one of them has matched, the others lead to the same state). SPEC §8.2 says so.
- **Main files:** `scripts/update-assets.sh`, `internal/core/regex/emit.go`, `internal/core/language/{bundled.go,bundled_test.go,oracle_test.go,systems_test.go,assets/}`, `third_party/vscode-go/`, `NOTICE`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.1, §8.2.
- **Tests:** the oracle checks 17 more fixtures (`test.c`, `test.cc`, `test.cpp`, `test.cu`, `test-23630.cpp`, `test-23850.cpp`, `test-78769.cpp`, `test-80644.cpp`, `test.rs`, `test-166781.rs`, `test-6611.rs`, `test.diff`, `COMMIT_EDITMSG`, `git-rebase-todo`, and `test.code-snippets`/CSS ones already passing), and every line equals VS Code's, with no expected difference. Detection of 19 more names (`.h` as C++, `.cu`, `.rs`, `.patch`, `MERGE_MSG`, `.gitignore_global`, `go.mod`, `go.work`, `go.sum`, …); fixtures of Emetos's own for the languages VS Code has none for (`go.mod`, `go.work`, `go.sum`, Git rebase, Ignore: a token and the scope it must have); the C++ timing test (a string of 200 `*`, and the eight C and C++ fixtures under 20 ms per line, none stopped early); in `core/regex`, nine cases showing that runs of empty alternatives match as before (first branch wins, a later one matches, none matches, a consuming branch after the run, a capturing branch outside it, negative lookarounds, a loop) and a test that fails without the fix (the whitespace pattern over 60 `*` in under 200 ms; it took over 2 s before). Under the race detector, which makes regexp2 about ten times slower, the language tests give the grammar a longer scan limit (new `ScanTimeout` in `textmate.GrammarOptions` and `language.GrammarSettings`, default unchanged) and skip their wall-clock assertions; a `textmate` test covers the option.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** none. Other grammars can have the same kind of pattern; the fix is general, and the 100 ms limit still stops the rest.

## 4.12 Bracket pairs

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.12-brackets
- **Summary:** `core/brackets` (SPEC §8.5). `Language` holds the brackets of a language, made from its configuration as VS Code's `LanguageBracketsConfiguration` does (`brackets` plus `colorizedBracketPairs`, whose pairs are brackets too; the default colorized pairs leave out `<` `>`; a closing text may close several openings, as `endcase` closes `case` and `casez`; case-insensitive; a bracket that starts or ends with a letter is found at the edge of a word only). `Index` reads a `Source` (lines, tokens, document language, whether the tokens are complete), keeps the brackets of every line until an edit (`Apply`, `Splice`, `Reset`) or new tokens (`Invalidate`) touch it, and pairs all of them lazily in one pass with a stack, as VS Code's parser does: a closing bracket closes the innermost pair it fits, the pairs above stay unclosed, one that fits none is unexpected, and an opening bracket deeper than 150 pairs is text. Queries: `Pairs`, `Brackets` (with `onlyColorized`), `Match`, `Enclosing`, `MatchingUp`, `Prev`, `Next`, `HasUnmatchedClosingAfter`. Brackets are found in tokens of the standard type "other" only, in the language of the token. `Attach` follows a document and its tokenizer; `RegistryLanguages` makes the languages from the language registry; a document in large file mode has an index that finds nothing.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/brackets/`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.5, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`.
- **Tests:** ported from VS Code: `brackets.test.ts` (the Verilog and LaTeX brackets with their closing sets), `tokenizer.test.ts` (the bracket tokenizer on tokens that are split, a comment that holds a bracket), `getBracketPairsInRange.test.ts` (all 10: Basic 1, Basic 2, Basic Empty, Basic All, `getBracketsInRange` with the levels of equal brackets, Test Error Brackets, colorized brackets against brackets in both modes, and the three about unmatched closing brackets: after a position, waiting for the tokens, and scoped to a language), and `textModelWithTokens.test.ts` (brackets1 for previous and next bracket from every column, bracket matching 1 to 4, issue #95843 with two languages in a line, #88075 with strings in a template, #11856). Emetos's own: a fuzz test that edits a text at random (replacing ranges with random text, with some lines without tokens, and some tokens changing) and checks that the index that followed the edits finds the same pairs, brackets (both modes), matches and enclosing pairs as a new index on the same text (60 seeds of 60 edits); the pairs against a plain stack on 200 random texts; edits that move and invalidate lines; `Enclosing`, `MatchingUp`; unclosed pairs and their ends; the depth limit; the document and tokenizer attached (Go, with brackets in strings and comments, an edit that changes the tokens of the lines below it, undo, `SetText`, large file mode); the languages of the bundled configurations (HTML lists no colorized pairs, JSON has braces).
- **Not ported:** the tests of the tree, `beforeEditPositionMapper`, `combineTextEditInfos`, `concat23Trees`, `length` and `smallImmutableSet`, because the tree of VS Code (a 2-3 tree reused across edits) is not ported (SPEC §8.5 allows a simpler scheme that passes the ported tests and the fuzz test); the old matching code for models too big to build the tree (`canBuildAST` false), because large files have no index.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** an edit costs one pass over all the brackets of the document (18 ms at 100,000 lines and 600,000 brackets, about 2 ms at 10,000 lines; `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`), where VS Code reuses the nodes an edit did not touch. Pairing only from the first edited line and stopping when the open pairs match the old ones would fix it; it is left until the editor of 4.13 shows it matters. `getBracketPairsInRangeWithMinIndentation`, which the guides of 4.14 need, is added there. Tokens with the same scopes are merged as VS Code merges tokens with the same metadata (the colors are not part of Emetos's tokens). The balanced-bracket selectors of grammars (`unbalancedBracketScopes` in the markdown extension) are not read yet; brackets are looked for in every token of type "other".

## 4.13 Brackets in the editor

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.13-brackets-editor
- **Summary:** the bracket features of the editor (SPEC §8.5.1). `core/edit/brackets.go`: `JumpToBracket`, `SelectToBracket` (with and without the brackets), `Commands.RemoveBrackets`, and `NewBracketSelectionRanges`, the provider of the bracket steps of Expand Selection (VS Code's `bracketSelections.ts`: inside, the pair, the line it starts on or the one above); `core/edit/stringselect.go`: `NewStringSelectionRanges`, the string steps (inside the quotes, the string), from the tokens of type "string". The commands `editor.action.jumpToBracket` (Ctrl+Shift+\\), `editor.action.selectToBracket` and `editor.action.removeBrackets` (Ctrl+Alt+Backspace) are registered, with their keybindings, and run in the editor. Settings `editor.matchBrackets`, `editor.bracketPairColorization.enabled` and `editor.bracketPairColorization.independentColorPoolPerBracketType`. Colors `editorBracketMatch.{background,border,foreground}`, `editorBracketHighlight.foreground1..6` and `editorBracketHighlight.unexpectedBracket.foreground` in the registry and in `kit.Colors`. `ViewModel.SetBrackets` takes the index (`brackets.Attach`), which the shell makes next to the tokenizer. The layer below the text boxes the brackets that match the cursors (a background and a border of 1 pixel inside, only while the editor has the focus, for up to 100 cursors; `always` also boxes the pair around a cursor); the text layer draws each bracket in the color of its level (`overlaySpans` puts the bracket spans over the token spans and keeps the font style of the token), a bracket that closes nothing in the unexpected color, a transparent color leaving the token's. A document in large file mode has no brackets, so none of this happens there.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/edit/{brackets,stringselect}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{brackets,overlay,render,widget,commands,options,viewmodel}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,process}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `internal/core/{settings/builtin,theme/colors_builtin,command/builtin,keybinding/defaults}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.5.1.

Command tables for Go to Bracket, Select to Bracket (with and without brackets, from a closing bracket, from a selection, touching pairs) and Remove Brackets (several cursors, a pair with two cursors); the string steps (escapes, two strings, quotes at the ends, no quotes, no tokens); in the editor: the commands through `RunCommand` (and undo of Remove Brackets), `matchedBrackets` for every mode of the option with and without focus, with a selection, with several cursors and more than 100, Expand and Shrink Selection through brackets and strings, the colors by level (repeat after the sixth, a transparent level, unexpected brackets, a row of a wrapped line, independent pools, the option off, no index), `overlaySpans`, the bracket colors of the bundled theme, the settings and their defaults, and the shell giving the editor its brackets. Goldens `brackets_dark.png` and `brackets_light.png` (`internal/gui/editor/testdata/goldens/`, both looked at): Go with nested brackets of three levels, brackets in a string and a comment left plain, a bracket that closes nothing in red, and the pair of `(2+3)` boxed.

- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none

VS Code gives the bracket provider 30 ms in two rounds; here it looks at up to 100,000 brackets in each direction. The string steps are Emetos's (VS Code has none of its own); SPEC §3.1 and this step name them. The bracket colors and the box follow the settings of the language of the document only through `Options`; a change of `editor.bracketPairColorization.*` takes effect with the next `SetOptions` (the shell calls it on every change of an `editor.*` setting).

## 4.14 Indentation detection

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.14-indentation-detection
- **Summary:** `core/indent.Guess`, a port of VS Code's `guessIndentation` (first 10,000 lines; tabs or spaces by the lines that start with each; the tab size from the differences between lines that follow each other, in the order 2, 4, 6, 8, 3, 5, 7; alignment after a comma skipped; 2 over 4 with two thirds of its occurrences). `Documents.OpenFile` now gives a file the indentation its language asks of the settings (`editor.tabSize`, `editor.insertSpaces`, which no code applied to documents before) and, with `editor.detectIndentation` on, the one `Guess` finds. The `configurationDefaults` of the bundled extensions are read (`Manifest.ConfigurationDefaults`, `Registry.ConfigurationDefaults`) and registered as language defaults of the settings (`RegisterLanguageDefaults`): Go and Makefile indent with tabs, YAML and Docker Compose with two spaces, and the other defaults (JavaScript's `editor.maxTokenizationLineLength`, Markdown's unicode highlight, …) are there for the steps that read them. Commands: `editor.action.indentationToSpaces` and `editor.action.indentationToTabs` (`edit.Commands.ConvertIndentation`), `editor.action.indentUsingSpaces`, `editor.action.indentUsingTabs`, `editor.action.changeTabDisplaySize` and `editor.action.detectIndentation`; a change of the document's tab size also changes how wide a tab is drawn.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/indent/{guess,doc}.go`, `internal/core/edit/convertindent.go`, `internal/core/language/{manifest,registry}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{indentation,commands,viewmodel}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{window,theme,process,app}.go`, `internal/core/command/builtin.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.6.1.

Emetos's own: ten edge cases of `Guess` (empty text, white-space-only lines, a tie, tabs winning, 3 spaces, 2 over 4 and 4 over 2, a difference over 8, more than 10,000 lines); `ConvertIndentation` (tabs to spaces with sizes 2 and 4, spaces to tabs with leftovers, white-space-only lines, the selection following the text, nothing to convert, an empty text, tabs after the text, several cursors collapsing to the primary); in the editor: the commands through `RunCommand` (with undo of the conversion), the arguments of "Indent Using…" (a number, an object, the display size keeping what Tab inserts, nine invalid arguments with the `TabSizeRequired` route), Detect Indentation with and without defaults; `configurationDefaults` of a manifest (several languages in one key, a key that is not a language block, a block that is not an object) and of the bundled languages; the shell opening files: 10 cases (two spaces and tabs found in text, the settings when the text says nothing, detection off, detection over settings, Go, YAML, Makefile, empty).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** "Indent Using Spaces", "Indent Using Tabs" and "Change Tab Display Size" take the tab size as an argument (`{"tabSize": 4}`) and fail with the tag `TabSizeRequired` without one: VS Code asks with a quick pick of 1 to 8, which needs the quick input of step 6.17 (the status bar of 6.x can then offer the commands). Changing `editor.tabSize`, `editor.insertSpaces` or `editor.detectIndentation` while a file is open does not re-apply them to it (VS Code does for files whose indentation was not detected); the settings apply when a file opens. `indentSize` (`editor.indentSize`) is not a separate option yet (it equals the tab size, as `edit.Options` already says).

In the editor: settings `editor.autoIndent` (default `full`), `editor.autoIndentOnPaste` (`false`) and `editor.autoIndentOnPasteWithinString` (`true`), the commands `editor.action.reindentlines` and `editor.action.reindentselectedlines`, a paste at one cursor followed by the indentation as its own undo step, and `ViewModel.SetIndenter`, which the shell calls with the process's `Indenter` and the document's language code; the tokens are brought up to date on the UI thread within the text layer's budget, and a line that cannot be tokenized in time makes Enter keep the indentation. `internal/core/indent/indenttest` holds VS Code's test configurations (JavaScript, Ruby, PHP, Go, C++, HTML, Visual Basic, LaTeX, Lua) and a text with given tokens.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/indent/{autoindent,enter,reindent,rules,onenter,language,text,doc}.go`, `internal/core/indent/indenttest/`, `internal/core/brackets/language.go`, `internal/core/edit/{autoindent,type,lines,options}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{indentation,commands,clipboard,viewmodel,options}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,process}.go`, `internal/core/{settings/builtin,command/builtin}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.6.2.

The patterns are compiled by `core/regex` (ECMAScript); the brackets of `onEnter` use the same, with `\s` and `\b` as JavaScript's. Reindent moves the cursors with the text as decorations do (VS Code's `executeEdits` without a cursor state). ## 4.16 Guides

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.16-guides
- **Summary:** indent guides and bracket pair guides (SPEC §8.6.3). `core/indent` gets `Levels` (the indentation of each line in columns, computed when asked, forgotten when an edit touches the line, with the smallest indentation of each block of 64 lines; `AttachLevels` makes it follow a document) and `Guides` with `LinesIndentGuides`, `ActiveIndentGuide`, `BracketGuides` and `LineGuides`, a port of `guidesTextModelPart.ts` and of `IndentGuidesOverlay.getGuidesByLine` (the options `editor.guides.*`, the interplay of the two kinds: a bracket guide takes the place of the indent guide at its column and the active indent guide is not highlighted on a line that has bracket guides unless `highlightActiveIndentation` is `"always"`). The smallest indentation inside a pair (VS Code's `minVisibleColumnIndentation`) is the smallest level among the lines after the opening bracket's up to the closing bracket's, which `Levels.MinLevel` answers in a few hundred steps even for a pair around most of a file. In the editor: the settings `editor.guides.indentation`, `highlightActiveIndentation`, `bracketPairs`, `bracketPairsHorizontal` and `highlightActiveBracketPair`; the colors `editorIndentGuide.background2..6`, `editorIndentGuide.activeBackground` and `1..6`, `editorBracketPairGuide.background1..6` and `activeBackground1..6` in the registry and in `kit.Colors`; `ViewModel.SetGuides` (the shell makes the levels next to the tokenizer and the brackets, with the language's `folding.offSide`); the layer below the text draws the guides after the selections and before the bracket boxes, in the colors of `NewGuideColors` (a level with a transparent color is left out and the levels go round the rest; a bracket guide with no color of its own takes the bracket color of its level, at 30% opacity when inactive).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/indent/{levels,guides,attach}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{guides,overlay,widget,options,viewmodel}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `internal/gui/app/app.go`, `internal/core/{settings/builtin,theme/colors_builtin}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.6.3.
- **Tests:** ported from `textModelWithTokens.test.ts`: all 17 cases of `TextModel.getLineIndentGuide` (each line's number of guides, the active guide of every line: one level, two, three, decreasing indent, Java, Javadoc, white space, tabs, checker.ts, #8425, #8952, #11892, #12398 and the four tweaks) and #49173 (the active guide with limits). VS Code has no test of the bracket pair guides, so Emetos's own: `BracketGuides` for a block, a part of the screen, the active pair alone, inactive pairs with and without highlight, text before the closing bracket, horizontal guides (all and active), a pair on one line, nested pairs with the cursor inside, independent color pools; `LineGuides` combining the kinds and the options (highlight always and off, everything off); `Levels` (levels with tabs and white space, the smallest level of ranges, a random test of 200 splices against fresh levels, `Invalidate`), `AttachLevels` against a document with random batches of edits and `SetText`, the off-side rule. In the editor: `rowGuides` for indent guides (the active one in the arguments of a call), for bracket pairs (columns and the active pair), with the options off and without levels, following edits, a line inserted and a change of the tab size; `NewGuideColors` for Dark Modern (one indent color, three bracket colors derived at 30%), going round the list and a theme's own colors; the options read from the settings; the shell giving the editor its levels; the colors in the registry and `kit.Colors`. Goldens `guides_dark.png` and `guides_light.png` (looked at): Go with tabs, indent guides, bracket pair guides for every pair with horizontal lines, and the pair of the call around the cursor highlighted.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the rows that continue a wrapped line have no guides (VS Code repeats them with `getViewLinesIndentGuides`/`getViewLinesBracketGuides` and the `forWrappedLines…` columns, which `indent.Guide` already carries): step 5.3 adds word wrap and with it that handling. Folded lines are not in the projection yet either (step 8.7). The vertical position of a guide is `(visible column − 1)` times the width of `0` in the font (VS Code uses the width of a space; they are equal in a monospace font). `Levels` is built for the document's tab size (`SetTabSize` follows a change); Emetos has no separate `editor.indentSize`, an indent level is a tab size. The guides are not drawn in large file mode for bracket pairs (the index finds none) but the indent guides are.

## 4.17 Auto-closing and surrounding

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.17-auto-closing
- **Summary:** auto-closing pairs, quotes and comments, typing over, deleting a pair and surrounding a selection (SPEC §8.6.4). A new `core/autoclose` holds the pairs of a language (VS Code's `CharacterPairSupport` and `StandardAutoClosingPairConditional`: `notIn`, `beforeText`, the neutral character, the maps by first and last character of the opening and the closing, `autoCloseBefore`, the surrounding pairs) and the values of `editor.autoClosingBrackets/Quotes/Comments/Delete/Overtype` and `editor.autoSurround`. `edit.Commands.Type` with one character now types over a closing character, closes a pair (longest opening, `beforeText`, the allowed characters after the cursor, the bracket balance from `brackets.Index.HasUnmatchedClosingAfter`, quotes after a word character, the token before the cursor, a neutral character asked of the tokenizer through the new `tokens.Tokenizer.TypeIfInserting`, a pair contained in another, an opening of several characters with its second undo step through `Result.FollowUp`) or surrounds a selection, in VS Code's order after the type-time indentation, which now also inserts the closing text (`Result.AutoClosed`). `edit.AutoClosedTracker` keeps the closing characters as decorations and forgets a pair when a cursor leaves it (`Cursors.SetOnChange` tells it), and Backspace deletes a pair the editor typed. The view model, the settings, `Process.AutoClosing()` and the app wire it up.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/autoclose/autoclose.go`, `internal/core/edit/{autoclose,autoclosed,autoindent,command,cursor,delete,options,type}.go`, `internal/core/tokens/tokenizer.go`, `internal/core/indent/indenttest/indenttest.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{commands,indentation,options,viewmodel}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,process}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.6.4.
- **Tests:** ported from VS Code's `cursor.test.ts`: open parens default, before whitespace, brackets before whitespace with quotes never, configurable `autoCloseBefore`, auto-pairing disabled, auto-wrapping configurable (`autoSurround` in all four values, several selections), quote, multi-character (`begin`, `/**`, `autoClosingComments: never`), undo of a multi-character pair (#338693) and with auto-indentation, `beforeText` (three cases and the shorter pair), contained pairs (#72177), #55314, #27937, #61070, #132912 (with VS Code's test tokenizer ported), brackets balance (#6841, with a stand-in for the bracket index), #25658, #37315 (four cases), #118270, #78527, #85983, #78833, #53357, #84998, #11476, #206774, #41825, "all cursors do the same thing when deleting left"; from `indentation.test.ts` the parts that 4.15 had to leave out: #209802 (`{` on an Allman line) and #225916. Emetos's own: the parse functions and the maps of `core/autoclose`, `TypeIfInserting` on a real grammar, the tracker (follows the text, several lines, a cursor outside, clear), the change hook of the cursors, a line that is not cheap to tokenize, and, in the editor with the real Go and TypeScript grammars and configurations: close, type over, stop typing over once the cursor left, Backspace on a typed pair and on a pair that was there, a quote that begins and one that ends a string, `/**` and its undo, surround, and every setting; the options read from the settings.
- **Not ported:** the tests of the input method (`startComposition`, `compositionType`, `endComposition`: #15825, #90016, #26820, #144690, #144693, #82701, #2773 and the composition cases of the `beforeText` and `begin` tests), which need the interceptors of the end of a composition; the tests that depend on VS Code's decoration events (#205598, #78975 snippet edits).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the interceptors of an input method's commit (VS Code's `compositionEndWithInterceptors`) are not done: after a composition nothing is typed over, closed or surrounded. The bracket balance test of the ported #6841 uses a small stack-based stand-in for the bracket index (the index has its own tests, and the editor test uses the real one). The pairs of the language are read when the file is opened, like the indentation rules: a change of the language of the document does not reload them. The legacy `docComment` electric support and the electric characters of VS Code are not ported (no bundled language uses them). `cheapToTokenize` now asks the token source whether or not there are indentation rules.

## 4.18 Comments

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.18-comments
- **Summary:** line and block comments (SPEC §8.6.5). A new `core/comment` holds the comment tokens of a language (`lineComment` with `noIndent`, `blockComment`) and a registry-backed `Languages`. `edit.Commands` gets `ToggleLineComment`, `AddLineComment`, `RemoveLineComment` and `ToggleBlockComment`, ports of `LineCommentCommand` (the analysis of the lines, the insertion points that line up, the block comment fallback of a language with no line comment, the tracked selection with VS Code's stickiness and `deltaColumn`) and `BlockCommentCommand`, and of the multi-selection handling of `comment.ts` (selections sorted, a shared line commented once). The language is read from the token at the start of the first line, so embedded code uses its own tokens. Commands `editor.action.commentLine`, `addCommentLine`, `removeCommentLine` and `blockComment`, keys Ctrl+/, Ctrl+K Ctrl+C, Ctrl+K Ctrl+U and Ctrl+Shift+A, settings `editor.comments.insertSpace` and `editor.comments.ignoreEmptyLines`, and the wiring of the view model, `Process.Comments()` and the app.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/comment/comment.go`, `internal/core/edit/{comment,options}.go`, `internal/core/{command/builtin,keybinding/defaults,settings/builtin}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{commands,indentation,options,viewmodel}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,process}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.6.5.
- **Tests:** all the cases of `lineCommentCommand.test.ts` and `blockCommentCommand.test.ts`, 45 line cases and 28 block cases converted to Go tables by a script (the suites: line comment, ignoreEmptyLines false, line comment as block comment, as block comment 2; insertSpace false; the case-insensitive and fixed-column tokens), `_analyzeLines`, `_normalizeInsertionPoint` (13 cases) and the three cases in mixed modes (#24047 twice, #36173); Emetos's own: several selections (a shared line, toggling twice, two cursors on a line, add after remove), a language with nothing to comment with, a block comment with an end token inside; the comment package; in the editor with the real grammars: toggle over lines with undo, add and remove, block comment, the two settings, and Markdown with a Go fence (the fence gets `//`, the Markdown around it `<!-- -->`); the options read from the settings.
- **Not ported:** none (the mixed-mode tests were written by hand because they register tokenizers in VS Code's harness).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none

- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the commands run on the UI thread and read the tokens of the first line of a selection within the tokenization budget; a line that is not tokenized yet is read with the language of the document. Columns are bytes, as everywhere in Emetos; the comment tokens are compared by byte, which is the same as VS Code's for ASCII tokens (a token with a non-ASCII letter is case sensitive).

## 4.19 Folding model

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.19-folding-model
- **Summary:** `core/folding` (SPEC §8.7.1). `Regions` (VS Code's `FoldingRegions`: nested regions by first and last line with the parent of each, the folded flag, the source and the kind; `FindRange`; the packing of the parent index into the line arrays is not needed in Go), `SanitizeAndMerge` (the merge of the regions the provider finds with the folded and the user-made ones), the indentation provider `ComputeRanges`/`IndentProvider` (markers, `offSide`, the region limit that keeps the least indented regions, cancellable), the `Model` over a document (each region a decoration with the options a provider function gives, `Update`, `UpdatePost`, `ToggleCollapseState` with the hidden state of the regions inside a folded one, `RemoveManualRanges`, `Memento`/`ApplyMemento` with a checksum, `AllRegionsAtLine`, `RegionsInside`), the collapse functions (`SetCollapseStateLevelsDown`, `LevelsUp`, `Up`, `AtLevel`, `ForRest`, `ForMatchingLines`, `ForType`, `ToggleAt`) and the jumps (`ParentFoldLine`, `PreviousFoldLine`, `NextFoldLine`), the action-level commands (`Fold`, `Unfold`, `Toggle`, the recursive ones, `FoldAll`, `UnfoldAll`, `FoldLevel`, `FoldAllExcept`, `UnfoldAllExcept`, `FoldAllBlockComments`, `FoldAllMarkerRegions`, `UnfoldAllMarkerRegions`), and `Attach`, the background updater (a debounce of 200 ms, a goroutine on a snapshot, cancelled by a newer change, a result for an older text dropped, nothing in large file mode). `Document.SetDecorationOptions` changes the options of a decoration; `indent.Level` is the indentation of a line; the setting `editor.foldingMaximumRegions` (10 to 65,000, default 5,000). The package list of the SPEC now has `autoclose/` and `comment/`, which steps 4.17 and 4.18 had left out.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/folding/{regions,indent,model,commands,actions,provider}.go`, `internal/core/document/decorations.go`, `internal/core/indent/levels.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.7.1.
- **Tests:** ported from VS Code: all of `indentRangeProvider.test.ts` (the 33 calls of its 24 tests: one to three levels, decreasing indent, Java, Javadoc, whitespace in Java and Python, tabs, the 17 region cases, #35981, #79359, the markers with their flags), all of `foldingModel.test.ts` (getRegionAtLine, collapse, update, delete, getRegionsInside, with level, getRegionAtLine2, the set-collapse functions, the decorations, fold jumping, #129503 and the five `removeManualRanges` cases), and `foldingRanges.test.ts` (the maximum, `findRange`, `setCollapsed`, `sanitizeAndMerge` 1 to 4); the harness keeps VS Code's one-based lines. Emetos's own: the regions' text form and helpers, the route tests of `FoldingInvalidRegions`, `FoldingLineOutOfRange` and `FoldingCancelled`, the limit with its report, the merge keeping or unfolding a region by the cursor, the 19 action-level commands, the change events, folds following edits, the memento (restored, text changed, entries not regions), the updater (one computation for quick edits, stop, large file), `SetDecorationOptions`.
- **Not ported:** `hiddenRangeModel.test.ts` and `syntaxFold.test.ts` (the hidden ranges belong to the view, step 4.20; syntax ranges come from language servers, area 3), and `indentFold.test.ts` (it tests the provider through VS Code's editor harness; the same cases are in the ported `indentRangeProvider.test.ts` and in the model tests).

- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the start and end marker patterns are tested one after the other, start first, where VS Code joins them into one pattern when their flags agree and takes the leftmost match: the two give the same answer for markers anchored at the start of a line, which the bundled ones are. A pattern of the language configuration carries no flags after it is compiled, so the markers are used as compiled. The commands are functions on the model for step 4.20 to bind (command ids, keys, `editor.foldLevel1` to `7`, the view state, the context key); `NextFoldLine` stops at the last region where VS Code's loop reads one past the end. `editor.foldingStrategy`, `editor.foldingHighlight`, `editor.foldingImportsByDefault`, `editor.showFoldingControls` and `editor.unfoldOnClickAfterEndOfLine` are for step 4.20.

## 4.20 Folding in the view

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-4.20-folding-view
- **Summary:** folding in the editor (SPEC §8.7.2). `foldedProjection` hides the lines of the folded regions from layout, scrolling, hit testing, selections and line numbers (the lines come from `Model.HiddenLines`, read from the decorations of the folded regions, so they follow edits before the regions are computed again); `edit.ModelLineLayout.Hidden` makes vertical and page movement count shown rows and Left and Right step over a folded range. The view model owns the model and the background updater (`StartFolding`, `UpdateFolding`), keeps the cursors out of folds (`adjustSelections`) and unfolds what hides the start of a selection (`revealCursor`), runs the 22 folding commands with VS Code's ids and keys (`editor.fold` … `editor.removeManualFoldingRanges`, the `foldingEnabled` context key), and keeps the folds in `ViewState.Folds`. The gutter draws the Codicon chevrons (`editor.showFoldingControls`: always, mouseover, never), the folded line has `editor.foldBackground` (`editor.foldingHighlight`) and a `…` placeholder; a press on a control or the placeholder toggles on release (Alt: the regions that are neither around nor inside, Shift or the middle button: the regions inside too); `editor.unfoldOnClickAfterEndOfLine`. Colors `editor.foldBackground`, `editor.foldPlaceholderForeground`, `editorGutter.foldingControlForeground` (and `icon.foreground`, which the last refers to) joined the color registry and `kit.Colors`. The app starts the folding of each document with the language's `folding.offSide` and `folding.markers`. `dev.openFile {"path","line","column"}` shows a file in the window's editor (SPEC §15.2); the dev runner now waits two frames after each command so that what a command made is there for the next one. A pre-existing gap fixed on the way: the editor widget did not give the poster of the window to a view model it was given in place of another.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/editor/{folding,folding_commands,projection,viewmodel,pointer,gutter,overlay,widget,options}.go`, `internal/core/edit/{layout,move}.go`, `internal/core/folding/model.go` (`HiddenLines`), `internal/core/command/builtin.go`, `internal/core/keybinding/defaults.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `internal/core/theme/colors_builtin.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,devopen,devcommands}.go`, `testdata/proof/4.20/`, `docs/SPEC.md` §8.7.2, §9.4, §15.2.
- **Tests:** projection tables with folds (rows, lines, hidden spans, spans cut to the document); fold commands hiding rows; the states of the controls and `editor.folding` off and on; the cursor moved out of a fold; movement over folds (down, up, end, right, left, select, page); reveal by go to line, by undo and by a click; folds following edits; `ToggleFoldAt` (control, placeholder, recursive, Alt); the view state (restored now, restored when the regions are known, a changed region not restored); the commands' arguments, jumps, manual ranges, the updater and stopping; `edit` layout tables (vertical moves over folds, left and right over folds, folds at the end of the text); widget tests that click the controls and the placeholder and check hit testing after folding; goldens `folding_always`, `folding_mouseover`, `folding_hover`, `folding_never`, `folding_light` (each viewed); app tests for `dev.openFile` (arguments with route tests of `DevOpenFileInvalid`, showing a file at a position, a file that cannot be opened, replacing the editor) and for the shell folding its file. Ported from VS Code: `hiddenRangeModel.test.ts` (hasRanges), as `TestHiddenRangeModelHasRanges` over `Model.HiddenLines`; `HiddenRangeModel.adjustSelections` is covered by Emetos's own tests above (the upstream test file does not test it).
- **Not ported:** the folding controller's debounce statistics and range-limit report (the 5,000 limit is applied by the provider), `editor.toggleImportFold` and the folding of imports by default (kinds need a language server: area 3), folding strategies other than indentation, hover-to-reveal of the folded content.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** `scripts/proof.sh docs/implemented-proof-images/4.20-folding.png --config testdata/proof/4.20/config --after 4s --commands '[["dev.openFile",{"path":"testdata/proof/4.20/sample.go"}],["editor.foldLevel1",null],["editor.unfold",{"selectionLines":[24]}]]' --` (the config sets `editor.showFoldingControls` to `always`): the Go sample with the import block, the struct and the functions `All` and `Languages` folded (chevron right, folded background, `…`), `Say` unfolded with the chevrons down on the lines of its regions and the indent guides, as I saw by viewing the image. With `--after 2s` the capture came before the last step: each step now waits two frames, which takes about a second in the headless compositor.
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the plan's command is extended with the unfold step and the config so that both states of the control show. The chevron cell is the first 16 pixels of the line decorations (VS Code draws it at their right end with its own spacing). The reveal of a click-folded range uses the editor's smallest scroll (VS Code centers the position in the viewport if it is outside). A click on a placeholder places the cursor as any click does, then unfolds on release. `Model.Update` of a result computed before an edit that moved the lines compares with the decorations, as `SanitizeAndMerge` does; between the edit and the next result the regions (not the hidden lines) are from the older text, so a control may show for a line for up to 200 ms after it moved. Word wrap (5.3) must give wrapped rows of a folded line the same hidden span handling; indent guides are not drawn on folded rows' hidden lines by construction.

## 5.1 Find model

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-5.1-find-model
- **Summary:** `core/find` (SPEC §9.8.1). `Params`/`Query` (VS Code's `SearchParams.parseSearchRequest` and `TextModelSearch`): literal, whole word, case, ECMAScript regular expressions through `core/regex`, multiline detection, the plain substring fast path, `FindMatches`, `FindMatchesIn` (several scopes, the touching ones joined), `FindNext` and `FindPrevious` with wrap-around, empty matches stepped over by a character, captured groups, byte columns. A multiline search reads the text in windows of 5,000 lines with 500 of lookahead (SPEC §9.8: "a bounded window of lines") and a regular expression gives up after one second. `ReplacePattern` (`ParseReplaceString`: `\n \t \\ \u \U \l \L $$ $& $0 $n $nn` with JavaScript's reading of a number that is not a group) and the preserve-case builder. `State` (VS Code's `FindReplaceState`: the strings, the switches with their one-change overrides, the scopes, `loop`, position, count and current match, change events). `Model` over a `Host` interface (the selection, scrolling, word separators, `editor.find.cursorMoveOnType`, timers): the matches are decorations of the document (`findMatch`, `currentFindMatch`, `rangeHighlight`, `findScope`), searched again 100 ms after an edit, `MoveToNextMatch`, `MoveToPrevMatch` (from the decorations below 19,999 matches, by searching above), `MoveToMatch`, `Replace`, `ReplaceAll` (one undo step, the touching matches one edit, the selection tracked through the edit; at 19,999 matches or more with no scope the whole text is replaced at once), `SelectAllMatches` (the selection that is a match stays primary), find in selection as the search scopes. Settings `editor.find.cursorMoveOnType` and `editor.find.loop`.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/find/{doc,query,replace,state,decorations,model}.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.8.1.
- **Tests:** ported from VS Code: `textModelSearch.test.ts` (27 find cases, each checked with `findMatches`, `findNextMatch` from the start and from every match and `findPreviousMatch` from the end and from every match; the 5 `findNextMatch` step tests, the 6 capturing tests, #74715, `\r` never found, `parseSearchRequest` (17 cases, on `Query`), both `isMultilineRegexSource` tests); `replacePattern.test.ts` (all 8 tests: the parse table of 43 strings, the JavaScript semantics, 4 groups of replace expectations, #19740, `buildReplaceStringWithCasePreserved` and "preserve case"); `findModel.test.ts` (all 44: 41 converted by a script and 3 by hand: #32522, content changes, #288515). Emetos's own: the multiline search over windows (matches on both sides of the window border, in the lookahead, running past a window, wrap-around, the limit across windows), `FindMatchesIn`, non-ASCII text and byte columns, the timeout, the state (events, overrides, match info, navigation, scopes), the model (the user's cursor, `cursorMoveOnType`, the scope following edits, the large replace, a read-only document).
- **Not ported:** the second run of each `findModel.test.ts` test over a piece-tree buffer built from chunks (Emetos has one buffer), the CRLF runs of `textModelSearch.test.ts` (the text is held with line feeds only, SPEC §6.1: `\n matches \r\n` has no equivalent, `\r can never be found` is run over a text of line feeds), the comparison of the compiled JavaScript `RegExp` in `parseSearchRequest`, the overview-ruler decorations of more than 1,000 matches (step 5.2 draws the ruler from the matches), the history and `findOnType` options of the widget (5.2).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the step names none)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** `State.Change` of a search scope: VS Code's test (`!newScope.every(n => old.some(o => !equals(o, n)))`) never replaces a scope by another one that differs, which cannot be the intent; Emetos replaces the scope unless it is equal. The large replace (19,999 matches or more) takes whole words and the case into account like the other path; VS Code's version ignores the word separators there. `edit.OccurrenceSession` (Ctrl+D) keeps its own literal search; moving it onto `core/find` is left for when the find widget (5.2) shares its options with it. The model takes a `Host` interface and timers so that it is tested without an editor; the view model implements it in step 5.2.

## 5.2 Find widget

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-5.2-find-widget
- **Summary:** the find and replace widget (SPEC §9.8.2). The view model is VS Code's `CommonFindController`: it owns the `find.State`, makes the `find.Model` over a `find.Host` of its own while the widget is open and drops it (with every decoration of owner 3) when it closes. `startFind` seeds the search string from the selection or the word at the cursor, forces the replace row for Replace, applies `editor.find.autoFindInSelection`, and focuses an input. The 17 commands with VS Code's ids and Linux keys (`actions.find` Ctrl+F, `actions.findWithSelection`, `editor.actions.findWithArgs`, `editor.action.nextMatchFindAction` F3 and Enter in the find input, `previousMatchFindAction`, the two `SelectionMatchFindAction`s, `startFindReplaceAction` Ctrl+H, `closeFindWidget` Escape, the five toggles Alt+C/W/R/L/P, `replaceOne`, `replaceAll`, `selectAllMatches` Alt+Enter) and the context keys `findWidgetVisible`, `findInputFocussed` and `replaceInputFocussed` (the inputs have a child scope of the editor's, which the key dispatcher finds through `FocusScopes`; the editor's commands are bound while the editor or the widget has the focus). The widget (`findWidget`) is an overlay at the top right of the editor, outside the editor's `Focus`: the chevron that shows the replace row, the find input with the match case, whole word and regular expression toggles inside, the match count, previous, next, find in selection and close; the replace input with the preserve case toggle, replace one and replace all. The matches are drawn under the text (`findMatch`, `currentFindMatch`, and `rangeHighlight` and `findScope` as whole lines, with the colors and borders of VS Code) and on an overview ruler layer over the vertical scrollbar. First versions of `kit.InputBox` (on `EditableText`), `kit.Toggle` and `kit.IconButton`, `kit.TextStyleOf`/`UIFontOf` and the `Theme` option `UIFont`. New settings `editor.find.seedSearchStringFromSelection`, `autoFindInSelection`, `closeOnResult`; the editor now reads `editor.wordSeparators` (the word commands and find use it) and `editor.find.cursorMoveOnType` and `loop`. New colors (`editor.findMatchBackground` and the other six find colors, `editorOverviewRuler.findMatchForeground` and `rangeHighlightForeground`, `editorWidget.*`, `widget.border`, `toolbar.hoverBackground`, `input.*`, `inputOption.*`, `inputValidation.errorBorder`) in the registry and `kit.Colors`. The window shares the switches of the editors (`FindMemory`).
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/editor/{find,find_commands,find_paint,findwidget,findstate,ruler,widget,overlay,options,viewmodel,commands}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/{inputbox,toggle,iconbutton,text,theme}.go`, `internal/core/find/query.go` (`EscapeRegExp`), `internal/core/{command/builtin,keybinding/defaults,settings/builtin,theme/colors_builtin}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,window}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.8.2.
- **Tests:** the view model: seeding (word, selection, several lines, `never`, `selection`, an empty line, regular-expression escaping), `findWithSelection`, `findWithArgs` (all arguments, in selection, none; the string asked for is not replaced by the seed), moving between matches (wrap-around, no loop, opening the widget from F3), the selection-match commands, closing (scope, decorations, focus), disposing, the five toggles, find in selection (empty selection, a selection ending at column 0, lifting it), `autoFindInSelection`, replace one and all (one undo step), select all, a closed widget, a read-only document, where Replace starts, `closeOnResult`, the switches shared by the editors, the arguments' parser, the overview ruler marks. Widget tests: the widget shows and hides with the state and the focus follows; typing searches and fills the count; the buttons (previous, next, close); the three switches and find in selection (also: the focus stays in the input); the replace row (chevron, replace, replace all, hiding it again); a read-only document; a click in the widget does not move the cursor; a narrow editor; the decorations on screen; goldens `find_replace_dark`, `find_replace_light`, `find_no_results`, `find_in_selection` (each viewed). App tests with real key events: Ctrl+F (seeded, focus), Enter, Shift+Enter, Alt+C, typing, Escape, F3 from the editor, Ctrl+H, typing in the replace input, Ctrl+Alt+Enter, Alt+Enter; `TestEveryDefaultKeybindingRunsItsCommand` now also tries every key with the find input and with the replace input focused. Kit: `IconButton`, `Toggle`, `InputBox`.
- **Not ported:** `editor.action.goToMatchFindAction` (needs the quick input, area 6), the find options widget VS Code shows when a switch changes while the widget is closed, the search and replace history (`editor.find.history`, `replaceHistory`), the global find clipboard (macOS only), the widget's resize sash, the animation of its entry, the multi-line find input, `editor.find.addExtraSpaceOnTop` (needs view zones: the widget covers the first lines of the text until the user scrolls; the setting is not registered), accessibility alerts.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** `go test ./internal/gui/editor -run TestProofFind -proof` → `docs/implemented-proof-images/5.2-find.png`: the widget with the replace row open (`<$1>`), regular expression on (`b(e)ta`), "2 of 5", the matches highlighted, the current one and its line in their own colors, and the ruler marks on the right, as I saw by viewing the image.
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** a search string that `findWithArgs` is given is not replaced by the word at the cursor (VS Code does). The buttons and toggles of the widget do not take the focus (they are used with the mouse; the keys work from the inputs): step 6.5 adds focus, tooltips and keyboard operation. The find input is one line. The state of the toggles is shared by the editors of a window but not saved between runs (VS Code keeps them in the workspace storage): the UI state step (area 6) persists them. `edit.OccurrenceSession` (Ctrl+D) still has its own literal search. `editor.wordSeparators` is now read by the editor, which changes nothing for the default. Word wrap (5.3) must make `RowOfPosition`-based painting of matches work on wrapped rows (the matches already go through `selectionRows`), and the ruler's lines through the wrapped projection. The matches over 1,000 are drawn on the ruler one by one (merged by pixel), which is fast at the 19,999 cap.

## 5.3 Word wrap

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-5.3-word-wrap
- **Summary:** word wrap through the line projection (SPEC §9.2). `core/wrap` computes the breaks of one line (`Compute`, a port of VS Code's `MonospaceLineBreaksComputer`: kinsoku rules, break after spaces and some punctuation, hard break for a word wider than a row, continuation indent from `wrappingIndent`). The view model holds the breaks of every line (`wordwrap.go`): the wrap key (columns, character width, indent, tab size, strategy, font) decides when they are recomputed; the document is broken in slices through the view model's timer (edited lines first, then the viewport, then the rest; synchronously when there is no timer) and an edit splices the breaks of the changed lines; the scroll position is anchored to the top visible row when rows above it change. ASCII lines in a monospace font are broken by counting columns, other lines through the paragraph's measurements. `wrappedProjection` (with folds) replaces the identity or folded projection while wrapping; `edit.ModelLineLayout` takes an `edit.RowMap` so Up/Down, Home and End work on rows. Painting: continuation rows are indented by an x offset, the current line highlight covers all rows of a line, selections and find matches cross rows, the cursor and hit testing use the row of a position. Settings `editor.wordWrap`, `wordWrapColumn`, `wrappingIndent`, `wrappingStrategy`, `stopRenderingLineAfter`; command `editor.action.toggleWordWrap` (Alt+Z), which turns wrapping on or off for one editor over the setting. No wrapping in large file mode or for lines over `editor.stopRenderingLineAfter`.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/wrap/{doc.go,breaks.go}`, `internal/core/edit/layout.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{wordwrap,wordwrap_commands,wrapproj}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{viewmodel,options,text,render,geometry,overlay,pointer,commands}.go`, `internal/gui/app/app.go`, `internal/core/{settings/builtin,command/builtin,keybinding/defaults}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.2 and §9.4.
- **Tests:** `wrap`: break tables (plain words, hyphens, long words, tabs, wide characters, kinsoku, indentation modes, a narrow width). `edit`: row-aware vertical moves, Home/End on rows, the pixel column kept across rows. `wrapproj`: tables with wraps and folds together, rows, indents, `RowStarts`, `Indent`, empty document. View model: wrap columns for each mode, breaks after edits (typing, joining and splitting lines, undo), the breaks are known by slices with a timer, resizing, the anchor, folds with wraps, cursor movement on wrapped lines, toggling, large file mode, lines over the stop limit, paragraph route for non-ASCII. Widgets: wrapping on screen, clicks on continuation rows and past the end of a line, the rectangle of a cursor on a row, resizing both ways, the current line over all rows, toggling live; goldens `wordwrap` (selection over wrapped rows), `wordwrap_cursor` (cursor on a continuation row, current line), `wordwrap_fold_find` (a fold and find matches on wrapped rows), each viewed.
- **Not ported:** `editor.wordBreak`, `editor.wordWrapBreakAfterCharacters` and `BeforeCharacters` (not registered; VS Code's defaults are used), the browser-specific `advanced` details (the strategy uses paragraph measurements), the cursor affinity at a wrap point, indent guides on continuation rows, wrapping by view zones.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none (the plan lists none; the goldens show the result)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** breaks run on the UI thread in slices (SPEC says background): a goroutine adds nothing until the paragraph route needs the text engine, which only the UI thread may use. Tabs after a wrap are measured from the start of the row. `editor.stopRenderingLineAfter` is registered but only stops wrapping: the render cutoff of §6.4 is not implemented yet. The cursor has no affinity at wrap points, so End on a row and the start of the next row are one position drawn at the start of the next row. Step 5.4 (whitespace) must draw on rows through `RowSpan`; the minimap (5.5) and sticky scroll (5.6) must use the projection's rows, not lines.

## 5.4 Whitespace, rulers and highlights

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-5.4-whitespace-rulers-highlights
- **Summary:** the remaining display options of SPEC §3.1. `editor.renderWhitespace` (`none`, `boundary`, `selection` (the default), `trailing`, `all`) draws VS Code's dot and arrow on the spaces and tabs it says (`whitespaceMarks`, a port of the SVG whitespace overlay, with the selection parts of each row, the single trailing space of a row that continues, and `editor.stopRenderingLineAfter`). `editor.renderControlCharacters` (default true) draws a dark red box with the Control Pictures glyph over the C0 controls, DEL and the directional formatting characters, above the text. `editor.rulers` (numbers or `{column, color}`, sorted) draws 1-pixel lines at their columns in `editorRuler.foreground` or their own color. `editor.occurrencesHighlight` (`off`, `singleFile`, `multiFile`) and `editor.occurrencesHighlightDelay` mark the other occurrences of the word at the cursor (VS Code's textual document highlight: case sensitive, whole words, after the delay, kept while the cursor moves inside a marked word, dropped by typing and edits); `editor.selectionHighlight`, `selectionHighlightMaxLength` and `selectionHighlightMultiline` mark the text that equals the selection with the find widget's switches, apart from the selections and from what the open find widget already marks. Both highlights are document decorations (owners 4 and 5) made by the view model (`highlights.go`) from `edit.Commands.WordQuery`, `SelectionQuery`, `Occurrences` and `OccurrencesApartFrom` (`core/edit/highlight.go`, over the literal search of the occurrence commands), follow the settings live, and are drawn between the guides and the find matches. New colors `editor.selectionHighlightBackground` and `Border`, `editor.wordHighlightBackground`, `StrongBackground`, `TextBackground` and the three borders.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/edit/highlight.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{whitespace,display_paint,highlights}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{overlay,options,viewmodel,folding,widget}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `internal/core/{settings/builtin,theme/colors_builtin}.go`, `internal/gui/app/app.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.2 and §9.4.
- **Tests:** `edit`: the selection query (16 cases: single, several, case, spaces only, lines, length in characters), the word query (10), the occurrences (5: whole words, case, parts of words), the occurrences apart from the selections (5). `editor`: 24 `whitespaceMarks` cases (every mode, tabs, a continuing row, the limit, non-ASCII), 13 `selectionSpans`, 10 `controlMarks`, the options read from the settings and the `rulers` parser (8), the word highlight (the delay to the millisecond, moving inside a mark, to another word, no word, typing, edits, every setting, large file mode, no timers) and the selection highlight (several selections, limits, the find switches, the open find widget, edits after 300 ms, disposing, live settings). Goldens `whitespace_all`, `whitespace_boundary`, `whitespace_trailing`, `whitespace_selection`, `rulers_control`, `highlight_word`, `highlight_selection`, each viewed (zoomed for the dots).
- **Not ported:** the marks of the highlights on the overview ruler, `editor.selectedTextMatchMode`, `editor.renderWhitespace`'s `experimentalWhitespaceRendering` variants (only the SVG look), the `[U+XXXX]` text VS Code shows for the directional formatting characters (a 2-pixel bar here), the word highlight of language servers (read and write access: area 3), highlights in other files (`multiFile` marks the file itself).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the word highlight uses the editor's word separators for both the word and the whole-word search (VS Code's textual provider uses its usual separators for the search). The highlights are computed on the UI thread: a document of a million lines searched for a short selection each time it changes would be slow (the selection highlight skips large file mode). The control character box takes the cell width the font gives the character (Go Mono gives the C0 controls a full cell); a character with no width (the bidi marks) gets a bar, so the next character is not covered. The Control Pictures glyph is the one the font fallback finds, or the font's own empty-glyph box. Step 5.5 (minimap) and 5.6 should show these decorations only if SPEC §9.5 says so.

## 5.5 Minimap

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-5.5-minimap
- **Summary:** the minimap of SPEC §9.5. `computeMinimapLayout` (a port of VS Code's `_computeMinimapLayout`: width from the room left by the gutter, scale and pixel ratio, `maxColumn`, left or right, `proportional`, `fill` and `fit` with the scale growing up to one more and sampling of the rows when there are too many) and `createMinimapRenderLayout` (`MinimapLayout.create`: the slider's height and place, the first and last line drawn, the padding at the top, the anti-flicker rule against the last layout) are pure and tested by tables. `minimapPainter` draws the lines into premultiplied RGBA pixels (a block of `scale × 2·scale` pixels per character that is not a space, in the color of its token blended into the background, tabs to their stop, full-width characters two blocks) and keeps the rows of the last frame by key (line, row span, text, colors): a frame draws only the rows whose key changed and copies the others to their new place. `RenderEditorMinimap` shows the image (`ui.NewImageFromRGBA`, drawn at the size of the window), the marks of the selections (a half-transparent bar over each selected line and the covered part of the line), the find matches and the text equal to the selection, and the slider (`mouseover` or `always`; hover and drag colors). The editor widget wraps its body in a `LayoutBuilder`, computes the layout from the size of the editor, gives the text area the room (padding on the minimap's side, and the left inset of the horizontal scrollbar) and puts the minimap next to the vertical scrollbar. The pointer: a press on the slider drags it, a press elsewhere scrolls the clicked row to the middle (proportional) or drags from the middle of the slider (fill, fit), the wheel scrolls the editor. New settings `editor.minimap.enabled`, `side`, `size`, `showSlider`, `scale`, `renderCharacters`, `maxColumn`; new colors `minimap.background`, `foregroundOpacity`, `selectionHighlight`, `findMatchHighlight`, `selectionOccurrenceHighlight`, `minimapSlider.background`, `hoverBackground` and `activeBackground`.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/editor/{minimap,minimap_layout,minimap_render,minimap_paint}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{widget,viewmodel,options,tokens}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `internal/core/{settings/builtin,theme/colors_builtin}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.5.
- **Tests:** layout: 12 layouts (default, capped by `maxColumn`, left, scale 2, two pixels per pixel, `fill` of 10 and 30 rows, `fit` short and long, `fill` with sampling, off, no room), the sampling (582 lines for 1,000 rows, the first lines, row to line), 10 slider and line cases (short and long texts at the top, middle and end, a partial first line, scrolling up and down against the last layout, another text, `fill`) and the slider arithmetic; the expected values come from a second implementation of VS Code's formulas, since VS Code has no test of its layout. Painter: the blocks of a line pixel by pixel (gutter, spaces, tabs, empty line, full-width), colors and opacity, the right edge, scale 2 and one-pixel lines, a translucent background, a frame that changed nothing, one line edited or colored (1 drawn, 9 copied), scrolling by a line (the same pixels as a frame drawn from scratch), padding at the top, a jump, an empty canvas. View model: the pointer with no minimap, a click revealing the row in the middle, a press on the slider and the drag (from where it began, ended by the release), `fill` dragging from the middle, the rows of the sampled lines, hover and the change notices. Widget: the text loses the minimap's width, on the left the text is shifted and clicks in the text land right, off and in a large file, only the changed line is repainted after an edit and after a scroll, a click reveals the line, dragging the slider scrolls, hover, the wheel; goldens `minimap` (a selection on the minimap, the slider) and `minimap_left_find` (left, with find matches), each viewed zoomed.
- **Not ported:** `editor.minimap.autohide`, the section headers (`showMarkSectionHeaders`, `showRegionSectionHeaders`, `sectionHeaderFontSize`...), the character rendering (always blocks, so lines are 3 pixels at scale 1 where VS Code's default is 2), touch gestures, the shadow while the text is scrolled sideways, marks for the diff, the problems and the git changes (later steps), the smooth scroll of a click, the memory VS Code keeps of the last layout against the loop of `fit` with wrapped text.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:**
  ![5.5 minimap](implemented-proof-images/5.5-minimap.png)
  `scripts/proof.sh docs/implemented-proof-images/5.5-minimap.png --after 3s -- internal/gui/editor/widget.go`: the editor in a headless window with a Go file, the minimap at the right of the text next to the scrollbar, its lines in the colors of the tokens, as I saw by viewing the image. (The plan lists no proof for this step.)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the line height of the minimap is that of the blocks mode for every value of `renderCharacters`. The first minimap line of a sampled minimap shows the row VS Code's formula gives it (its code writes the first entry twice). The wheel over the minimap goes through the editor's own wheel handler. The minimap is a stack child above the text and next to the scrollbar; with the horizontal scrollbar visible it covers the bottom of the minimap, as in VS Code. Sticky scroll (5.6) and the breadcrumbs (5.7) sit above or below the editor and need no change; the diff editor (9.x) will give each side its own minimap settings.

## 5.6 Sticky scroll

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-5.6-sticky-scroll
- **Summary:** sticky scroll of SPEC §9.6. `newStickyTree` turns the folding regions (at least two lines) into a tree; `stickyTree.candidates` and `vm.stickyLinesAt(top)` choose the lines that stick for a scroll position (a region sticks when the slot of its depth is below the natural y of its start line, and until the slot reaches the bottom of its end line, when the last sticky line is pushed up by `Offset`), at most `min(editor.stickyScroll.maxLineCount, 25% of the viewport rows)`, never the lines a fold hides. `RenderEditorSticky` paints the lines over the top of the text in `editorStickyScroll.*` colors with the gutter (line numbers, folding controls), the hover, a border and a gradient shadow, clipped to the pushed height; the last line is drawn first so it slides in under the others. The widget is a stack child inset by the left minimap and takes the pointer only over the lines. A click moves the cursor and scrolls the line clear of the sticky lines (`RevealStickyPosition`); a click on the folding control folds; Shift click reveals the end of the range. `RevealPosition` now keeps a revealed row clear of the rows that may be sticky while sticky scroll is on. Settings `editor.stickyScroll.enabled`, `maxLineCount`, `scrollWithEditor` (registered earlier, now read into `Options.StickyScroll`); new colors `editorStickyScroll.background`, `editorStickyScrollGutter.background`, `editorStickyScrollHover.background`, `editorStickyScroll.border`, `editorStickyScroll.shadow`.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/editor/{sticky,sticky_paint}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{widget,viewmodel,options}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `internal/core/{settings/builtin,theme/colors_builtin}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.6.
- **Tests:** tree and candidates tables (nested regions, equal starts, single-line regions, folded lines hidden, the limit by setting and by viewport), the lines for scroll positions in nested code including the push-up at the end of a region, the reveal padding table (on/off, near the top, above the screen), hover and click positions in the view model; widget tests (the lines appear on scroll, clicks land on the line and column, the folding control, Shift click, the pointer passes through below the lines, the minimap inset, off by setting); options read from the settings; goldens `sticky_scroll` (three nested headers) and `sticky_scroll_pushed` (the last header pushed up at the end of its range), each viewed.
- **Not ported:** the outline model of language servers (the candidates come from folding ranges; area 3 may add them), `editor.stickyScroll.defaultModel`, the keyboard commands (`focusStickyScroll`, `selectNext`/`Previous`, `goToFocusedStickyScrollLine`, `selectEditor`), the context menu, sticky lines in the diff editor, animation of the changes.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:** none here; step 5.7's proof shows the sticky lines with the breadcrumbs
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the candidates are folding regions, so a header that VS Code's outline model would show but the indentation or grammar folding does not (or the reverse) differs. The sticky widget is repainted by the scroll controller's listener. The breadcrumbs (5.7) sit above the editor and need no change here.

## 5.7 Breadcrumbs and the list overlay

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-5.7-breadcrumbs
- **Summary:** `kit.ListOverlay` and the breadcrumbs bar (SPEC §10.2, §9.7). `kit.ListModel` is the pure state of a list with a filter (the rows that match, the focused row that skips disabled items, the rows in view, page and end moves, scrolling); `kit.ListOverlay` is a focused popup over it with rows of icon, label and description, keyboard (Up, Down, PageUp, PageDown, Home, End, Enter, Escape, typing and Backspace to filter), clicks, wheel, hover, a tap region that dismisses on a click outside (or in no region of its `TapGroup`) and an option `Key` so a list starts again for new items. The breadcrumbs bar (`editor.Breadcrumbs`, `BreadcrumbItems`, `ReadSiblings`) shows the path of the file (absolute, or relative to a `Root` that step 6.13 will give) with Codicon icons and chevrons; a click on a segment reads the folder that holds it in a goroutine (cancelled with the window's `ctx`, answers of older reads dropped) and shows a list overlay below the segment through an `OverlayPortal` that is laid out from the segment's paint transform; folders are entered (with a `..` row), files are opened through `OnOpen`. The app shell puts the bar above the editor of a file document, follows `breadcrumbs.*` settings live and opens a chosen file in the editor (`showDocument`, `openPath`; the dev command shares `showDocument`). New settings `breadcrumbs.enabled`, `breadcrumbs.filePath`, `breadcrumbs.icons`; new colors `breadcrumb.foreground`, `background`, `focusForeground`, `activeSelectionForeground`, `breadcrumbPicker.background`, `list.activeSelectionBackground`, `list.activeSelectionForeground`, `list.hoverBackground`, `list.focusOutline`.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/kit/{listmodel,listoverlay,theme}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{breadcrumbs,breadcrumbs_widget}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,devopen}.go`, `internal/core/{settings/builtin,theme/colors_builtin}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.7 and §10.2.
- **Tests:** `ListModel`: 20 key sequences (down over a disabled row, scrolling into view, stops at the ends, pages, Home and End, filters with case, none matching, clearing, the scroll reset), shown and in view, `FocusItem` (4 cases and a filtered-out item), a list of disabled rows. `ListOverlay` widget: 17 keyboard cases (choose, move, pages, Escape once, numpad Enter, typing with and without `Filterable`, Backspace), clicks (a row, a disabled row, outside, the focus leaving: dismissed once), the wheel, scrolling to the focus, the initial focus, "No results"; goldens `list_overlay` (an icon list with a description, a disabled row, the focus and the hover) and `list_overlay_filtered`. Breadcrumbs: 15 `BreadcrumbItems` cases (absolute, relative to a root, the root's name as a prefix of another folder, the root itself, unclean paths, `last`, `off`, relative paths), 13 `compareNames`, `ReadSiblings` over a real folder (order, links to folders and files, a broken link) and its two errors by route (`ReadSiblings.BreadcrumbsReadDirFailed`, `ReadSiblings.BreadcrumbsReadCancelled`, metadata `dir`), the settings; widget tests over a fake file system: the segments for each setting, the picker (siblings and opening a file, the file's own siblings, entering a folder and `..`, a second click, another segment, a click outside, Escape, typing to filter, an unreadable folder, a change of file), the real file system, a very long path (no overflow); goldens `breadcrumbs` (with a hovered segment) and `breadcrumbs_picker`; the app: the bar above the editor, a sibling opened in the editor, the settings followed live, a file that vanished is reported and the editor stays.
- **Not ported:** the symbol part of the breadcrumbs (area 3), the other `breadcrumbs.*` settings, the keyboard focus of the bar and its commands, the tree look of the picker, the file icon theme (Codicons stand for it), `files.exclude`, scrolling of a long path, the list of VS Code's `ListView` (smooth scrolling, a scrollbar: rows are windowed by the model).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none
- **Proof:**
  ![5.7 sticky and breadcrumbs](implemented-proof-images/5.7-sticky-breadcrumbs.png)
  `go test ./internal/gui/editor -run TestProofStickyBreadcrumbs -proof`: a Go file scrolled to line 15 with two sticky lines (`func Outer`, `for`) under the breadcrumbs bar (`proj > src > sample.go`, from a fake path), as I saw by viewing the image.
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** `painting.NewEdgeInsetsSymmetric` takes (vertical, horizontal), which cost a clipped list in the first golden; the order is easy to get wrong. The list overlay's Up/Down/Enter/Escape are handled by its own focus node, so with the key dispatcher (SPEC §5.4) they are not commands: the quick input (6.x) will give lists a context key and commands (`list.focusDown`, ...) and the keybindings will own these keys. The picker lists the siblings in one folder at a time (drill-down) where VS Code expands a tree. The bar is placed by the app shell until the workbench (6.x) lays out the editor group; the breadcrumbs of a diff editor (9.x) are not decided.

## 5.8 Snippet parser and variables

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-5.8-snippet-parser
- **Summary:** `core/snippet` reads VS Code's snippet grammar (SPEC §9.9.1): a port of `SnippetParser` (the scanner, the marker tree of text, placeholders, choices, variables, transforms and format strings under a `TextmateSnippet`, the parent links, `Replace`, `Walk`, `Offset`, `EnclosingPlaceholders`, `ToTextmateString`, `Clone`, default values of repeated placeholders with cyclic references cut, the final tab stop, `ParseFragment` for the session of 5.9) and of the variable resolvers over plain values (selection, file, clipboard, comment, time, workspace, random, composite) with `SelectionValuesOf` to gather the selection's values from a document. Transforms run on `core/regex` (ECMAScript syntax, flags `dgimsuvy`, a search limited to one second) and the case shorthands (`upcase`, `downcase`, `capitalize`, `pascalcase`, `camelcase`, `kebabcase`, `snakecase`) handle Unicode letters. Invalid syntax stays text, as in VS Code. Lengths and offsets are bytes.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/snippet/{scanner,marker,parser,transform,variables}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.9.1.
- **Tests:** ported all 54 tests of `snippetParser.test.ts` (as tables: the scanner, escapes, text, the text-and-marker table of 54 cases, transforms, choices, `toTextmateString` and its identity, real-world snippets, default placeholder values, markers' lengths, parents, offsets, `replace`, placeholder order, the shorthand and Unicode case tables, and the regression cases of the issues named in them; the checks that need JavaScript's `RegExp` objects read the pattern and flags of the `Transform` instead) and 16 of the 17 tests of `snippetVariables.test.ts` (the one skipped, #76840, is about VS Code's label service; the others that used its text model, label and workspace services and `sinon` timers are rewritten for the plain-value resolvers, with a fixed moment in several zones). More cases not in VS Code: scanner tokens in bytes, the flags and the failures of `NewTransform` by route (`NewTransform->Transform.init.SnippetInvalidFlags`), `Source()` of patterns, transform replacing (`g`, `i`, `y`, `s`, `m`, empty matches, groups that did not match, multi-byte text), the random and clipboard resolvers, re-indentation of a selection, the overtyped text, `ParseFragment`. A seeded property test (30,000 snippets of the pieces of the syntax) and a fuzz target `FuzzParse` check that every snippet parses to a consistent tree (parents right, no two texts side by side), resolves, clones and serializes to something that parses; the default run covers the corpus of the cases.
- **Not ported:** `OvertypingCapturer` (the session of 5.9 gathers it into `Overtyped`), the label service (`FileResolver` takes paths), localized day and month names, JavaScript's `RegExp` details that `core/regex` does not have (a regular expression of the snippet is checked when the snippet is parsed, and a snippet whose regular expression `core/regex` cannot compile stays text).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none. `FuzzParse` ran 220,000 inputs under `-parallel=1` and a memory limit without a finding.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** `Marker.replace` of a marker that is not found does nothing (VS Code's `splice(-1)` would remove the last child). A placeholder index too big for an int is text. A search of a transform that runs past its limit ends the replacing instead of failing. `CURRENT_TIMEZONE_NAME` is the name of the zone of the moment unless the caller gives the IANA name (5.9 should). CLAUDE.md got a section about memory (a fuzz run without limits was killed for using too much memory).
