Snippet files (`File`, `Snippet`, `Service`): `<language>.json` and `*.code-snippets` with `scope`, prefix arrays, `include`/`exclude`, JSON with comments, `Service.Reload(ctx)`, `Service.Snippets(Query)`, the body insights (`${foo}` turned into a placeholder, trivial, uses the clipboard or the selection), and the Tab completion matching (`NonWhitespacePrefix`, `TabCompletions`). In the editor (`gui/editor/snippets.go`): the commands `jumpToNextSnippetPlaceholder`, `jumpToPrevSnippetPlaceholder`, `leaveSnippet`, `acceptSnippet`, `insertSnippet` and `editor.action.insertSnippet` with their keys and weights, the context keys `inSnippetMode`, `hasNextTabstop`, `hasPrevTabstop`, `inSnippetChoice` and `hasSnippetCompletions`, the setting `editor.tabCompletion`, the four snippet tab stop colors with the tab stops drawn under the text, and the lists (the options of a choice, several snippets for Tab, the Insert Snippet picker) shown below the caret as a `kit.ListOverlay` (new option `StealFocus`, since the autofocus of a list gives way to an editor that has the focus). `document.ChangeDecorationOptions` changes the stickiness of a decoration. The shell makes the process's `snippet.Service` from `<config>/snippets` and gives it to every editor.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/snippet/{session,controller,files}.go`, `internal/core/document/decorations.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{snippets,snippets_widget,display_paint,overlay,widget,folding,viewmodel,options,commands}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/{listoverlay,theme}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{process,app}.go`, `internal/core/{command/builtin,keybinding/defaults,settings/builtin,theme/colors_builtin}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.9.2.

Emetos's own: snippet files (`files_test.go`, 10 test functions: every field, prefix arrays, empty prefix arrays, comments, groups, scope selection with parents, the body insights, include and exclude, reloading, a missing folder, the two failure routes `SnippetDirReadFailed` and `SnippetFileReadFailed`, `NonWhitespacePrefix`, `TabCompletions`); in the editor (`snippets_test.go`, 13 test functions): the command and its tab stops with decorations, typing in a tab stop, the end conditions, Escape with several cursors, mirrors, the choice list (accept and dismiss), Tab completion for one and several snippets and when it applies, Insert Snippet by name, by language and picked, a read-only document (`DocumentReadOnly`), the clipboard read first (with a change in between, no clipboard, a failing read: `SnippetClipboardReadFailed`), the variables from the editor, the commands registered; the Tab and Escape priority rows in `TestDefaultKeymap`; `TestEveryDefaultKeybindingRunsItsCommand` got three states (a snippet at its first and second tab stop, a snippet prefix before the cursor); `ChangeDecorationOptions`. Goldens `snippet_tabstops_dark.png`, `snippet_tabstops_light.png` and `snippet_choices.png` (`internal/gui/editor/testdata/goldens/`, all looked at).

- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the text a cursor typed over (for `TM_SELECTED_TEXT` with an empty selection) is not tracked (`Overtyped` is nil); the language of the document is used for the snippets at the cursor, also inside an embedded language; a snippet that needs `CLIPBOARD` waits for the clipboard and is dropped when the editor changed meanwhile; the snippet list is a `kit.ListOverlay` until the quick input (6.17); `Service.Reload` is called once at startup until the workspace watcher (6.13) calls it; `WORKSPACE_NAME` and `WORKSPACE_FOLDER` stay empty until 6.13 calls `SetSnippetWorkspace`; the Tab completion looks at the snippets at every cursor move, which is cheap only because it returns early without a word before the cursor and without the option.

## 5.10 Emmet markup

- **Date:** 2026-10-23

- **Summary:** `core/emmet` ports Emmet's abbreviation parser and markup output (SPEC §9.10.1): the scanner, tokenizer, parser, conversion to a node tree (child, sibling, climb-up, multiplication, groups, ids, classes, attributes, text, numbering, implicit tags, fields), snippets resolution (`html.json`, `xsl.json`, `pug.json`, variables), attribute merging, implicit tag names, the BEM and label add-ons, `lorem`, and the writers for HTML/XML/XSL, HAML, Pug and Slim with the comment and template options, using the editor's indent and newline (`Config.SetOutput`) and `emmet.syntaxProfiles` / `emmet.variables` (`Config.ApplyProfiles`, `VariablesFromSettings`). The settings `emmet.variables` and `emmet.syntaxProfiles` are registered. `scripts/update-assets.sh` copies the snippets and the license (`third_party/emmet/LICENSE`, `NOTICE`).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/emmet/*.go` and `assets/`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `scripts/update-assets.sh`, `third_party/emmet/`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.10.1.

- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** offsets are bytes; repeat limit 1000 by default (never above 10000), nesting limit 1000, `$` width 64, `lorem` words 10000; the profile mapping of `emmet.syntaxProfiles` is a reconstruction from Emmet's option names (VS Code's `@vscode/emmet-helper` is not in `reference/`), so compare it with the helper if exact parity matters; two Emmet bugs were fixed (a group `)` in an attribute name, a field without a placeholder in a name); `update-assets.sh` was run only as a copy with the new block, not as a whole.

## 5.11 Emmet CSS and expansion

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-5.11-emmet-css
- **Summary:** CSS abbreviations and expansion in the editor (SPEC §9.10.2). `core/emmet` ports Emmet's stylesheet side (the CSS tokenizer and parser, snippets with fuzzy property matching and scoring, colors, number units and the output for css/scss/sass/less/stylus; `Config.Type`, the `Stylesheet*` options, `Config.ApplyPreferences`), the extraction of the abbreviation before the cursor (`ExtractAbbreviation`), the language logic of VS Code's Emmet extension (`SyntaxFor`, `EmmetMode`, `emmet.includeLanguages` and `excludeLanguages`) and `ExpandAt`, which gives the replaced range and the expansion as a snippet. In the editor: the command `editor.emmet.action.expandAbbreviation` ("Emmet: Expand Abbreviation"; each cursor expands its abbreviation or selection, and the expansions go in through `snippet.Controller.Apply`, so tab stops work and it is one undo step), Tab with `emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab` (context key `config.emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab`; Tab priority: snippet tab stop, snippet prefix, Emmet, `tab`), a plain Tab when nothing expands, and the notification "Failed to expand abbreviation" for a failure. New settings `emmet.includeLanguages`, `emmet.excludeLanguages`, `emmet.triggerExpansionOnTab`.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/emmet/{css_*,stylesheet,extract,languages,preferences,expand_at,config,markup}.go` and `assets/css.json`, `internal/gui/editor/{emmet,viewmodel,options,widget,commands}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{keys,app}.go`, `internal/core/{command/builtin,keybinding/defaults,settings/builtin}.go`, `scripts/update-assets.sh`, `NOTICE`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.10.2.
- **Tests:** ported all of Emmet's `test/stylesheet.ts` (117 assertions), `test/extract-abbreviation.ts` (58; one adapted for byte offsets) and `css-abbreviation/test/{tokenizer,parser}.ts` (45 and 43); own tables: `SyntaxFor` (31), `IsAbbreviationValid` (42), `ExpandAt` (50), preferences, the 8 new error tags with routes, `FuzzParseCSS`, `FuzzExtract` (20 s each under `-parallel=1`, no finding) and a seeded random test; in the editor: the command for markup, stylesheet, indentation, included languages, selections and several cursors, tab stops of an expansion, every fallback to Tab, the failure route (`VariablesInvalid`) and a read-only document (`DocumentReadOnly`); Tab priority rows in `TestDefaultKeymap`; a state in `TestEveryDefaultKeybindingRunsItsCommand`; the Tab key through the shell expands and reports a failure.
- **Not ported:** `wrapWithAbbreviation` and the other Emmet commands (balance, match tag, ...), `emmet.preferences` (read by `Config.ApplyPreferences` but not registered), `emmet.extensionsPath`, the completion provider settings, the checks of VS Code that parse the document (cursor in a `<style>` tag, in a CSS comment, in a selector, `parsePartialStylesheet`).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** `@vscode/emmet-helper` is not in `reference/`: `IsAbbreviationValid`, the filters and the preferences are written from its documented behavior, so they may differ in small ways (a markup abbreviation of only digits and dots is rejected by Emetos's own rule); the cursor is assumed to be in markup (html-like languages) or in a rule (stylesheets), including inside an embedded language; a selection of several lines is not expanded; a stylesheet snippet that does not parse is skipped instead of throwing; extraction looks back at most 4096 bytes; `sss` counts as a stylesheet syntax.

## 5.12 Large files and long lines

- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Branch:** feat/step-5.12-large-files
- **Summary:** SPEC §6.4 end to end. Large file mode (from 1.4) was verified feature by feature: the tokenizer, bracket pairs, folding, minimap, word wrap and the highlights already checked it; sticky scroll did not and now does. The window posts one warning (`LargeFileNotice`) for each large document, and the new setting `editor.largeFileOptimizations` (default true) turns the mode off (`Documents.SetLargeFileOptimizations`, `AnnounceLarge`). Long lines: a row is laid out and drawn only up to `editor.stopRenderingLineAfter` bytes (`shownLength`); `geometry` works out columns beyond it with the width of a character (`x`, `rowEnd`, `positionAt`, `rectOf`), and the scroll extent counts the hidden bytes. `editor.maxTokenizationLineLength` was already wired to the grammar (checked). A file that looks binary opens as `editor.Notice` with an "Open Anyway" button (click, Enter, Space) which reads it again with `AllowBinary` (`Documents.OpenFileWith`); a file over the limit fails with `FileTooLarge` and a notification. `BenchmarkOpen200MB` and the rows in `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`. New colors `button.foreground/background/hoverBackground/border` and `descriptionForeground` (also in `kit.Colors`).
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/editor/{geometry,render,notice,sticky}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,devopen,window,theme}.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `internal/core/theme/colors_builtin.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, `docs/SPEC.md` §6.4.
- **Tests:** `shownLength` table (8 cases, no cut inside a character), positions beyond the drawn part (x, row end, the column of a point and back, the rectangle of a character, far to the right), a line cut at a character, a 5 MB single line (word wrap on) that is shown and moved in, the large-file mode table (word wrap, sticky scroll, highlights, with and without; the other features have their tests in `tokens`, `brackets`, `folding`, `minimap`), the notice (click, Enter, Space, another key, no action) and its two goldens `notice_dark.png`/`notice_light.png` (looked at), the shell showing the binary notice and opening anyway (and the failure kept as a notification and the notice staying), the large file notification once and not for a small document, `editor.largeFileOptimizations` off, and the `FileBinary` and `FileTooLarge` routes.
- **Not ported:** VS Code's "renderValidationDecorations", semantic highlighting and word-based suggestions switches of large file mode (those features do not exist yet).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none. `BenchmarkOpen200MB`: 361 ms, 1.15 GB allocated.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** `editor.stopRenderingLineAfter` counts bytes, not UTF-16 units; a limit of zero means no limit (VS Code would draw nothing); a binary file blocks the whole window until the workbench (6.x) has tabs to show the notice in; the benchmark's 200 MB read allocates about 1.15 GB (text and buffer), so the memory of opening the largest file is about six times its size.

## 6.6 Layout parts

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-6.6-layout-parts
- **Summary:** the workbench of SPEC §10.1 in `internal/gui/workbench`: menu bar area (a `kit.MenuBar`, `window.menuBarVisibility` modes), activity bar (48 px), primary side bar (default 300 px, minimum 170, left or right), editor area (the existing editor with its breadcrumbs, the notices), panel (bottom, left, right or top, maximize; hidden by default as in VS Code) and status bar (22 px, left and right slots), laid out with `kit.SplitView` so the editor area absorbs window resizes. The `Layout` model (`State()`/`Restore()` with a JSON-tagged `LayoutState` for 7.5, `OnChange`, `FollowSettings`), 16 commands (`workbench.action.toggleSidebarVisibility` Ctrl+B, `togglePanel` Ctrl+J, `focusSideBar` Ctrl+0, the other toggles, panel positions, maximize, focus commands, `workbench.actions.menubar.focus`), 7 settings (`workbench.sideBar.location`, `workbench.activityBar.location`, `workbench.statusBar.visible`, `workbench.panel.defaultLocation`, `workbench.panel.opensMaximized`, `window.title`, `window.titleSeparator`), 22 part colors, the context keys of VS Code's `contextkeys.ts` (`sideBarVisible`, `sideBarFocus`, `panelVisible`, `panelFocus`, `panelPosition`, `panelMaximized`, `mainEditorAreaVisible`, `editorAreaFocus`, `statusBarFocused`...) with a focus scope for each part, and the window title from the `window.title` template (a port of VS Code's `template`, "sample.go - Emetos" by default) kept up to date. A `kit.DialogHost` sits at the root and the key dispatcher lets no command through while a dialog is shown. The app shell builds the workbench; `newApp` and `newAppBlocked` keep their signatures.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/workbench/{layout,title,workbench,commands}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,window,keys}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/{splitview,theme}.go`, `internal/core/{command/builtin,keybinding/defaults,settings/builtin,theme/colors_builtin}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §10.1.

In `core/command`: `MenuRegistry` (VS Code's menu ids, groups, order, `when`, checked-when, submenus, dynamic submenus through a provider; items are filtered by `when`, sorted by group, order and title, separated between groups, and disabled by the command's precondition; empty groups, unknown commands and untitled items are dropped) with `DefaultMenus` as data (46 rows) and `ViewRegistry` (view containers and views, as VS Code's `ViewsRegistry`/`ViewContainersRegistry`) with `DefaultViews` (the Explorer container and a "Folders" placeholder view). ## 6.9 Editor grid

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-6.9-editor-grid
- **Summary:** several editor groups (SPEC §3.2, §10.3). `editorgroup.Grid` is a tree of splits whose leaves are groups (the root is always a split, a split never has one child, sizes are shares of 1): add, remove, merge, move and copy groups, split in every direction, move and copy editors between groups (a moved editor keeps its input and so its view model), maximize and restore, even sizes, neighbours by box geometry, groups by index and in most-recently-used order, and `RestoreGrid`/state in and out for 7.5. `Model.Clone`, the `Copier` interface and `TextInput.CopyInput` copy an editor into a split. Decoration owners are now per view model (`document.AllocOwners`; each view model reserves 8 owners with the slots autoClosed 1, folding 2, find 3, word 4, selection 5, snippets 6; the first view model of a document keeps owners 1-6; `snippet.Editor.Owner`), so two editors of one document do not collide. The window shows the grid as nested `kit.SplitView`s of groups (a tab strip and an editor each), sash drags recorded in the grid, the active group shown by the `tab.unfocused*` colors on the others, empty groups with the empty state, a focus request retried for up to 6 frames. The VS Code grid commands (split in four directions and orthogonal, focus and move to a neighbour or numbered group, move and copy groups and editors, join, close group, maximize, even widths, editor layouts), the keys, a View > Editor Layout submenu, `workbench.editor.openSideBySideDirection`, `workbench.editor.closeEmptyGroups` and one more setting, 3 colors, and `dev.openFile`'s `group` argument (tags `DevOpenFileInvalid`, `DevOpenFileGroupInvalid`).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/editorgroup/{grid,grid_editors,grid_state,input,textinput,model}.go`, `internal/core/document/decorations.go`, `internal/core/snippet/session.go`, `internal/gui/editor/*` (owners), `internal/gui/app/{gridview,groupcommands,group,devopen,editors}.go`, `internal/core/{command,keybinding,settings,theme}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.2, §7.1, §10.3, §11.2, §15.2.
- **Tests:** about 61 new test functions (33 of grid operation tables: every split direction and operation, closing, merging, maximize and restore, even sizes, neighbours in nested layouts, indices, state round trip; 22 widget tests: splitting through commands and keys, sash drags, focus commands, two editors of one document (an edit in one shows in the other, independent cursors, owners that do not collide), closing a group releasing the document, `dev.openFile` with `group` and its routes), `model_port_test.go` now ports 33 of the 49 tests of `editorGroupModel.test.ts`; 6 goldens (`grid_two_groups`, `grid_three_groups`, `grid_empty_group`, Dark and Light; looked at) and the regenerated View menu goldens of the workbench.
- **Not ported:** the commands that expand a group (`minimizeOtherEditors`, `minimizeOtherEditorsHideSidebar`, `toggleEditorWidths`), `maximizeEditorHideSidebar`, moving by tab index other than for the active editor.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** Ctrl+K Ctrl+\ is only `splitEditorOrthogonal`; maximize hides the other groups and keeps their sizes, as VS Code; in the real app focus moving to a new group takes about 3-4 frames, so a script that follows `dev.openFile` with `group` needs a short pause.

## 6.10 Tab interactions

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-6.10-tab-interactions
- **Summary:** the rest of SPEC §3.2 "Tabs". Tabs drag by the primary button after a 4 px slop with a label that follows the pointer (kept inside the window): reorder in the strip with a drop indicator, move to another group's strip or editor area, drop on an edge of an editor area to split (the zones of VS Code's `editorDropTarget.ts`: a 10% edge ring, thirds or halves choosing the split side by `openSideBySideDirection`; Alt toggles splitting for one drag, Ctrl at the drop copies, Escape or a cancelled pointer cancels, `workbench.editor.splitOnDragAndDrop`). A strip auto-scrolls when the pointer rests within 35 px of its end and a half-hidden tab under the drag is revealed. A right click on a tab opens the `EditorTitleContext` menu (Close, Close Others, Close to the Right, Close Saved, Close All, Copy Path, Copy Relative Path, Keep Open, Pin, Unpin, and a "Split & Move" submenu) through `MenuSource`; every row runs its command with the context `{"groupId","editorIndex"}`, and the close actions act on the clicked tab and its group, not the active one. Commands `copyFilePath`, `copyRelativeFilePath`, `workbench.action.files.copyPathOfActiveFile` with VS Code's Linux keys. `dev.openFile` takes `"preview": true`. The pure-Go drop target model is in `core/editorgroup` (`ZoneAt`, `ZoneRect`, `TabDropIndex`, `DropAllowed`, `Grid.DropOnTabs`, `Grid.DropOnZone`, `Grid.SplitEditor`).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/editorgroup/{droptarget,grid_editors}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/{tabs,tabdrag,bounds,scrollable_element,theme}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{tabdrag,tabmenu,app,gridview,group,groupcommands,devopen}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/widget.go`, `internal/core/{command,keybinding,settings,theme}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §10.2, §10.3, §15.2, `testdata/proof/6.10/`.

## 6.13 Workspace model and watcher

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-6.13-workspace
- **Summary:** `core/workspace` (SPEC §10.5). `Open(ctx, path)` opens the window's folder as a `Workspace` with an `Item` tree read lazily (errors per directory kept on the item, symlink loops not followed), the sort orders of `explorer.sortOrder` (`default`, `mixed`, `filesFirst`, `type`, `modified`, `foldersNestsFiles` like `default`; lexicographic options and reverse) with VS Code's comparers on `golang.org/x/text/collate`, `files.exclude` (dropped at read time), compact folder chains (`explorer.compactFolders`, as `ExplorerCompressionDelegate`), `Refresh`, and `Apply` of watcher events keeping the loaded state. The watcher uses inotify through `golang.org/x/sys/unix` (no cgo): the folder recursively (new subfolders get watches, `files.watcherExclude`/`files.watcherInclude`), the parents of open files outside it, events coalesced over 50 ms with VS Code's `EventCoalescer` rules plus inotify move pairing (cookie renames, atomic save by rename is a `Changed` of the target), own writes dropped through `Suppress(path, FileStat)`, and a polling fallback (no inotify, `ENOSPC`, forced) with one notice per kind. The window wires it: `cmd/emetos <folder>` opens the workspace, settings (user and `<folder>/.emetos/settings.json`) and snippet files are reloaded through the watcher, an `explorer.*`/`files.exclude` change refreshes the tree, the breadcrumbs are folder-relative, the title uses the folder, `WORKSPACE_NAME`, `WORKSPACE_FOLDER` and `RELATIVE_FILEPATH` get values, watcher notices become one warning notification each. Settings registered: `files.exclude`, `files.watcherExclude`, `files.watcherInclude`, `explorer.sortOrder`, `explorer.sortOrderLexicographicOptions`, `explorer.sortOrderReverse`, `explorer.compactFolders`.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/workspace/*.go`, `internal/gui/app/{workspace,window,process,app,editors,gridview}.go`, `cmd/emetos/main.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `go.mod` (`golang.org/x/sys` is now a direct dependency, same version), `docs/SPEC.md` §10.5, `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`.
- **Tests:** 52 test functions in `core/workspace` (141 passing sub-results: model, apply, coalescing with a fake clock, comparers, real-inotify watcher tests and polling-mode runs: create, change, delete, renames, replace by rename, own writes, nested folders, excluded folders, outside files, unwatch, ctx cancel, `ENOSPC` and init-failure fallbacks, queue overflow), 10 in `gui/app` (folder open and missing folder, settings and snippet reload through the watcher end to end, following open files outside the folder, folder-relative breadcrumbs and title, `WORKSPACE_*` in a snippet); a route test for every new tag (three of them skip as root); no goldens changed; no proof image.
- **Not ported:** multi-root workspaces, file nesting, `explorer.excludeGitIgnore`, Open Folder and Close Folder commands, the explorer view (6.14), file operations, `files.exclude` in the Problems panel and the breadcrumbs picker, renames in the polling fallback, keybindings reload (no user keymap yet), `Suppress` wiring (`TODO(step 7.1)`: no save command exists yet).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none (the step has none; the explorer comes in 6.14)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** `gluttertest.Tester` does not dispose the widget tree at the end of a test, so state `Lifetime()`s are never cancelled; workaround `ProcessOptions.WatchFiles` (tests leave it off, the editor sets it) in `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`.
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** names compare with the root collation, not the user's locale; a first save of a new file by rename arrives as `Changed` (`Apply` adds unknown paths); symlinked folders are not watched unless listed in `files.watcherInclude`; `Watch` reads the tree synchronously, so the app calls it from a goroutine.

## 6.14 Explorer view

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-6.14-explorer
- **Summary:** the Explorer (SPEC §3.2, §10.8). `theme.FileIconTheme` reads VS Code's icon theme JSON (`iconDefinitions`, `fileExtensions`, `fileNames`, `languageIds`, `folderNames`/`folderNamesExpanded`, `light` and `highContrast` sections) with VS Code's lookup order (name, extension chain from the longest, language id, default) and bundles the Seti mapping (`iconassets/vs-seti-icon-theme.json`, copied by `scripts/update-assets.sh`, `NOTICE` updated). The explorer view (`internal/gui/views/explorer`) is a `kit.Tree` over `workspace.Workspace`: rows with Seti icons, compact chains, lazy directory reads off the UI thread, refresh on `Workspace.OnChange` keeping expansion, click opens a preview and double-click pins, keyboard, title actions Refresh Explorer and Collapse Folders, reveal of the active file (`explorer.autoReveal`: true/false/`focusNoScroll`), the Open Editors section (grouped by editor group, dirty dot or close button, hidden by default as in VS Code and shown with Ctrl+K E), and the welcome content with an "Open Folder" button. `workbench.action.files.openFolder` (Ctrl+K Ctrl+O) opens the portal folder chooser on a goroutine and replaces the window's workspace; `workbench.action.closeFolder` (Ctrl+K F). Commands: `workbench.view.explorer`, the focus commands, `showActiveFileInExplorer`, `revealInExplorer` (tab menu row "Reveal in Explorer View"), `refreshFilesExplorer`, `collapseExplorerFolders`; File menu rows "Open Folder..." and "Close Folder"; settings `explorer.autoReveal`, `explorer.openEditors.visible`, `explorer.openEditors.sortOrder`, `workbench.iconTheme`; context keys `workbenchState`, `isFileSystemResource` and the explorer visibility keys.

## 6.15 Explorer file operations

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-6.15-file-operations
- **Summary:** the file operations of SPEC §10.5. `workspace.FileOps` (core, `ctx` on every I/O call): create file and folder, rename (also case-only), move (refuses to overwrite without confirmation, refuses to move a folder into itself), copy with VS Code's incremental names (`explorer.incrementalNaming` `simple`, `smart`, `disabled`: `x copy.txt`, `x copy 2.txt`), recursive copy with symlinks kept as links, trash by the freedesktop spec (`Trash/files` and `info/*.trashinfo`, cross-device fallback, unique names), permanent delete, restore, and an undo/redo history (create, rename, move, copy, trash) whose results (`OpResult`, `PathMove`) the window uses to re-point editors; `ValidateFileName`/`CleanNewName` port the name checks of the explorer (empty, separators, `.` and `..`, whitespace, 255 bytes, already exists). The explorer has inline inputs for New File/New Folder and Rename (stem selected, the error balloon under the input), a delete confirmation `kit.Dialog` with "Do not ask me again" (writes `explorer.confirmDelete`), cut/copy/paste with dimmed cut rows and overwrite confirmation, drag to move with auto-expand on hover and `explorer.confirmDragAndDrop` (Ctrl copies), drops from the file manager through `DragTarget`/`ExternalDragData`, the `ExplorerContext` menu and the New File/New Folder title actions. Editors follow renames, moves and deletes (`Document.SetURI`: tab labels, language, title and breadcrumbs follow, dirty state and view state kept; clean editors of deleted files close, dirty ones stay) and undo re-points them back. Commands: `explorer.newFile`, `explorer.newFolder`, `renameFile` (F2), `moveFileToTrash`/`deleteFile` (Delete, Shift+Delete), `filesExplorer.cut/copy/paste/cancelCut`, `explorer.openToSide`, `revealFileInOS`, `undo`/`redo` while the tree has the focus, `copyFilePath` and `copyRelativeFilePath` with the new separator settings; 12 settings; 11 context keys.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/workspace/{names,fileops}.go`, `internal/core/document/document.go`, `internal/core/editorgroup/textinput.go`, `internal/gui/views/explorer/{edit,dnd,body,view}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/tree.go`, `internal/gui/app/{fileops,explorer,editors,menus,tabmenu,window,workspace,app}.go`, `internal/core/{command,keybinding,settings}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §10.5 ("As built (step 6.15)"), §10.8.
- **Tests:** 4 in `names_test.go`, 26 in the core `fileops_test.go` (every operation incl. each naming mode, trash and restore in an isolated `XDG_DATA_HOME`, cross-device fallback, undo/redo, 18 `FileOp*` tags each with a route test), 47 in the app `fileops_test.go` plus `TestExplorerFileKeys` and `TestExplorerContextMenu` (inline inputs, validation, delete dialog and its checkbox, cut/copy/paste, drag and drop with the fake clock, an external drop through `Tester.Drop`, editors following a rename of a file and of a folder, deletes, undo re-pointing editors, commands and keys through the dispatcher); 12 goldens (`explorer_{new_file,input_error,rename,delete_dialog,context_menu,cut}`; Dark and Light; looked at).
- **Not ported:** workspace-root rows, Open With, compare rows, Find in Folder, the terminal, upload/download of the web, dragging files out to other applications, a progress notification for long copies, one overwrite question per dropped item.

## 6.16 Fuzzy scorer

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-6.16-fuzzy-scorer
- **Summary:** `core/fuzzy` (SPEC §10.6), a port of VS Code's `fuzzyScorer.ts` and the `fuzzyScore` part of `filters.ts`: `PrepareQuery` (multi-word values, path separators, quoted contiguous queries), `Scorer` (owns the scratch buffers; one per goroutine) with `ScoreFuzzy`, `ScoreFuzzy2` and `FuzzyScore`, the generic `ScoreItemFuzzy`/`CompareItemsByFuzzyScore` over an `ItemAccessor` (label, description, path) with a hash-keyed `Cache`, `CreateMatches`, `MatchesPrefix`. Scores are identical to the reference. Positions and matches are rune offsets (not UTF-16 units). No function returns an error.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/fuzzy/{runes,match,wordscore,scorer,fuzzy2,query,item,compare}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §10.6, §14, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`.
- **Tests:** all 68 tests of `fuzzyScorer.test.ts` ported (one adapted: the cache-key test checks distinct entries instead of VS Code's hash numbers; Windows-only branches not run), 26 of 41 tests of `filters.test.ts` (the `fuzzyScore` ones), own tests (rune offsets with accents, astral and CJK, a reused `Scorer` equals a fresh one, quick-reject never changes a result, 10,000-rune inputs bounded, separate scorers across goroutines under `-race`), `TestScoring100kPathsWithinTarget` (600 ms bound, skipped with `-short`) and benchmarks over 100,000 generated paths.
- **Not ported:** `anyScore` and the graceful variants (not used by `fuzzyScorer.ts`), 15 `filters.test.ts` tests of filters the package does not contain.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** performance (go1.27.3 linux/amd64, AMD RYZEN AI MAX+ 395): one keystroke over 100,000 generated paths takes 10-19 ms for score and sort without a cache and 14-27 ms with a new cache each time; the SPEC §14 target is under 30 ms, met in every case (details in `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`); path separator is `/` only; names compare with `x/text/collate` instead of `Intl.Collator`; a query-by-target matrix over 2,097,152 cells scores nothing (VS Code allocates without bound).

## 6.18 Quick open

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-6.18-quick-open
- **Summary:** file search in the quick input (SPEC §10.6). `core/workspace.FileIndex` walks the folder in the background (breadth-first, skipping `.git`, `files.exclude`, `search.exclude` and the `.gitignore`/`.ignore` files of every directory through `core/glob`, honoring `search.useIgnoreFiles`, `useGlobalIgnoreFiles`, `useParentIgnoreFiles` and `followSymlinks` with loop protection, unreadable directories counted), offers a consistent `Snapshot()` while the walk runs, applies watcher events incrementally (`Apply`: files, folders, ignore-file changes) and rebuilds on settings changes. `Search` scores every file with `core/fuzzy` off the UI thread (label = name, description = folder, path = relative path), cancellable, deterministic over 1 to 8 workers, top 512; `QuickOpen` puts the recently opened files (`RecentFiles`, in memory until 7.5) first for an empty query and merges matches before the file results, de-duplicated; `ExtractRangeFromFilter` parses `main.go:12:5`, `main.go#12`, `main.go(12,5)`. `quickinput.NewFilesProvider` is the default (no prefix) provider: per keystroke the previous search is cancelled and only the newest result is shown, results refresh while the index grows or the watcher reports changes, "recently opened" and "file results" groups, highlighted names and folders, Seti icons (the new shared `kit.FileIcon`, also used by the explorer), "No matching results", `?` help entry; Enter opens (a kept editor by default as VS Code, a preview when `workbench.editor.enablePreviewFromQuickOpen` and `workbench.editor.enablePreview` are on), Ctrl+Enter and Alt+Enter and the "Open to the Side" button open beside, an open file is activated, `file:line:col` reveals the position, with no folder only the recent files are listed. Settings: `search.exclude`, `search.useIgnoreFiles`, `search.useGlobalIgnoreFiles`, `search.useParentIgnoreFiles`, `search.followSymlinks`, `search.quickOpen.includeHistory`, `search.quickOpen.history.filterSortOrder`, `workbench.editor.enablePreviewFromQuickOpen`; the Go menu row "Go to File..." (Ctrl+P); the context key `inFilesPicker`. The window builds the index with the workspace, feeds it watcher events and the recent list from opened editors, and stops it with the window.

## 7.1 Saving

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-7.1-saving
- **Summary:** saving and reverting (SPEC §3.1, §6.3, §7.1). `document.Save`/`SaveAs`/`Revert` run in three steps: `BeginSave` on the UI thread (runs the participants, captures the text and the version), `SaveJob.Write` on a goroutine (atomic temp-file write through `fileio.Write`, mode kept, owner and group kept best effort, symlink targets written, `WriteOptions.IfUnchanged` fails with `FileModifiedSince` when the file changed since the document last saw it, a deleted file is recreated), and `FinishSave` on the UI thread (the saved version is the version that was written, so a document edited during the write stays dirty and the next save does not conflict with its own write; a second request for the same document waits and runs again). `Overwrite` handles read-only files and Save As over an existing file, `IgnoreConflict` skips the conflict check (the Overwrite button and `files.saveConflictResolution` `overwriteFileOnDisk`). Save participants (`edit.SaveParticipants`, a port of `saveParticipants.ts`): `files.trimTrailingWhitespace`, `files.insertFinalNewline`, `files.trimFinalNewlines`, in that order as one undoable batch, with the cursor-line exception for auto save (`SaveAuto`); language-scoped values honored. Untitled documents: `Documents.NewUntitled` (lowest free `Untitled-N`, `files.defaultLanguage`, dirty means "has text"), Save As re-keys the document, re-detects the language, keeps the undo history, and the editor input follows (`InputCapabilities`). Save As uses the portal dialog on a goroutine; when it is unavailable a quick input path prompt (`Controller.Prompt`, with validation messages) takes over; an existing target asks "Replace". Save All saves the files and runs Save As for each untitled one, stopping when one is cancelled. Errors are notifications with Retry, Save As and, for conflicts, Overwrite and Revert, one per document. `Watcher.Suppress` is called after every write (the `TODO(step 7.1)` of 6.13). "Save with Encoding" is in the encoding picker. Commands: `workbench.action.files.save` (Ctrl+S), `saveAs` (Ctrl+Shift+S), `saveAll`, `revert`, `newUntitledFile` (Ctrl+N), `saveWithoutFormatting` (Ctrl+K S); File menu rows New Text File, Save, Save As..., Save All, Revert File; Save and Save As in the tab menu of a dirty tab; settings `files.trimTrailingWhitespace`, `files.insertFinalNewline`, `files.trimFinalNewlines`, `files.defaultLanguage`, `files.saveConflictResolution`; context keys `activeEditorIsDirty`, `activeEditorCanRevert`, `dirtyWorkingCopies`.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/document/save.go`, `internal/core/edit/saveparticipants.go`, `internal/core/fileio/{write,fsys_linux}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/saveparticipants.go`, `internal/gui/app/{save,window,app,group,statuspickers}.go`, `internal/gui/quickinput/{controller,picker,host}.go`, `internal/core/editorgroup/{textinput,input}.go`, `internal/core/{command,keybinding,settings}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.1, §6.2, §6.3, §7.1, §10.3, §10.5, §10.6, §11.2.
- **Tests:** 24 test functions in `core/document` (writes with every encoding and BOM, LF/CRLF, mode, symlink, recreate, conflict and `Overwrite`, the edit-during-write race, read-only and missing folder routes, Save As identity incl. language re-detection and undo history, Revert, untitled naming, Save All), participant tables (48 subtests incl. the five relevant cases of `saveParticipant.test.ts`, CRLF, empty document, cursors and selections, language override), 36 in `gui/app` (Save As with `FakeDesktop().AnswerFileChooser`: chosen, cancelled, replace; the path prompt through the `Window.SaveDialog` seam; Ctrl+S and Ctrl+K S; the race; untitled; Save All; Revert; the four error notifications with their actions; Save with Encoding; menus and their dimming; keys through the dispatcher; the watcher ignoring our own write but not another program's), route tests for `SaveReadOnlyDocument`, `SaveNoFile`, `SavePathInvalid`, `SaveParticipantFailed`, `SaveEncodingUnknown`, `RevertNoFile`, `FileModifiedSince`, `FileExists`, `SaveDialogFailed`, `SaveAsTargetOpen`; 14 goldens (`save_error`, `save_replace_dialog`, `save_file_menu`, `save_tab_menu`, `save_path_prompt` in the app and `quickinput_prompt_{path,invalid}`; Dark and Light; looked at).
- **Not ported:** formatting on save and code actions on save, a participant timeout, `files.trimTrailingWhitespaceInChangedLines`, admin saves, `saveFiles` and `saveAllInGroup`, the Save All button and the Save row of Open Editors, "New Text File" rows in the empty-group and tab-strip menus, closing prompts (7.2).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ (`gui/app` alone takes about 9.4 minutes under `-race`: run it with `-timeout 40m`) · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.

## 7.2 Closing with unsaved changes

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-7.2-closing
- **Summary:** prompts when editors, groups or the window close (SPEC §10.3, §10.10, §10.11). `editorgroup.PlanClose(grid, targets)` is a pure-Go planner: it returns the dirty editors among the ones about to close whose document no staying editor shows, each document once (a dirty document open in another editor or group does not ask until its last editor closes; an empty untitled document is clean). Every way of closing goes through one app function (`closeConfirmed`/`closeEditors`): tab X and middle click, Ctrl+W, all the close commands (`closeUnmodifiedEditors` never asks), Open Editors X, the switcher's X, Close Folder and Open Folder (the 6.14 refusal is replaced by the dialog). One dialog per batch: "Do you want to save the changes you made to {name}?" or "...to the following N files?" with the names listed (ten, then "...N additional files not shown") and "Your changes will be lost if you don't save them."; buttons Save ("Save All" for several, default), Don't Save, Cancel (Escape). Save runs the 7.1 pipeline in order (untitled documents through Save As); a failed save, a cancelled Save As or a document edited meanwhile cancels the whole close; Don't Save closes without touching the files. While the dialog is open or its saves run, other requests that would ask are ignored (re-entry guard). Window close: `Window.RequestClose(reason)` (per window, for 7.8) runs the planner over all editors and the dialog, then closes through the injectable `Window.Quit` seam or `ExitApplication(Required)`; the Glutter hook for the implicit first window is the app-exit request (an `AppLifecycleListener.OnExitRequested` that calls `RequestClose` and returns cancel at once). Commands `workbench.action.closeWindow` (Alt+F4, Ctrl+Shift+W) and `workbench.action.quit` (Ctrl+Q), File menu rows Close Window and Exit; setting `files.hotExit` (`off`, `onExit`, `onExitAndWindowClose`, default `onExit`), for which `shouldAskBeforeClose` returns "ask" until 7.6.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/editorgroup/closeplan.go`, `internal/gui/app/{closing,app,window,group,groupcommands,explorer,switcher}.go`, `internal/core/{settings,command,keybinding}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.1, §10.3, §10.10, §10.11, §11.2, `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`.
- **Tests:** `closeplan_test.go` (planner tables: clean, one dirty, several, shared across groups, untitled, stray inputs), 24 tests in `closing_test.go` (the message builder for 1, 2, 10, 11 and 12 files; every close path asks and Cancel keeps everything; each answer: Save, Don't Save, Cancel, Escape, Enter, click beside the dialog; untitled Save As chosen and cancelled; a failing save aborts the batch; batch closes; re-entry; switcher and Open Editors X; Close Folder and Open Folder; `Window.RequestClose` with the `Quit` seam called once, only after the answer; the platform request through `ts.RequestWindowClose(ui.ImplicitViewID)` returning cancel and a second request ignored; Close Window and Quit through the dispatcher and the menu rows; the hot-exit table; reopen after Don't Save), 6 goldens (`close_dialog_{one,several,many}`; Dark and Light; looked at), `save_file_menu_{dark,light}` regenerated for the new File rows (looked at).
- **Not ported:** backups and the real hot-exit decision (7.6), per-window prompts and `controller.Destroy()` for several windows (7.8, `TODO(step 7.8)`), `window.confirmBeforeClose` (not in the SPEC), the auto-save shortcut on close (7.3).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ (`gui/app` alone about 10.5 minutes: `-timeout 40m`) · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** "The request to close the implicit first window reaches the app without the window (7.2)": the implicit window's close request is not handed to a controller delegate, it arrives as the app-exit request without a view id; 7.8 needs a regular controller delegate for the other windows.
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the list says "...N additional files not shown" (the reference's wording); Don't Save does not read the file to revert, the documents are dropped with their last editor; a document being saved is asked about like any other; the editor being asked about is not activated before the dialog; a file closed with Don't Save is still recorded for Reopen Closed Editor (untitled ones are not); the editor picker hides when its X would ask.

## 7.3 Auto save

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-7.3-auto-save
- **Summary:** `files.autoSave` (SPEC §3.1, §6.3, §10.3, §10.10). `document.AutoSave` is a pure-Go controller (no I/O, no goroutines, injectable clock and host) with the modes `off`, `afterDelay`, `onFocusChange`, `onWindowChange`: `afterDelay` keeps a timer per document restarted on every edit (the save happens `files.autoSaveDelay` after the last edit; a save, an undo back to clean or a revert cancels it); `onFocusChange` saves when the editor widget loses focus (decided one frame later), when another editor or group becomes active, and when the window loses focus; `onWindowChange` saves everything dirty when the window loses focus. Eligibility: never untitled, virtual or read-only documents; `files.autoSaveWorkspaceFilesOnly` excludes files outside the folder; `files.autoSaveWhenNoErrors` holds a document with an error marker back until the markers change; a document with a Save As dialog open is left alone. A failed save, manual or auto, pauses auto save for that document (VS Code's conflict/error state) until a save succeeds or the file is read again; the single sticky notification per document stays. Only the `afterDelay` timer saves with `SaveAuto` (the trim-whitespace participant keeps the cursor line); focus saves use the new `SaveFocusChange` and `SaveWindowChange` reasons, which trim like an explicit save, as VS Code does. Changing the setting at runtime saves the dirty documents at once when auto save is on (VS Code's `onDidChangeAutoSaveConfiguration`). Closing an editor with `onFocusChange` or `onWindowChange` saves the savable dirty documents first (awaited, in order) and the 7.2 dialog asks only about what is still dirty (a failed save stays in the dialog, Cancel keeps saved content saved); closing the window saves every dirty savable document in any mode but `off` first; "Don't Save" can never be undone by a focus save. `workbench.action.toggleAutoSave` (File > Auto Save, a checked row by `config.files.autoSave != 'off'`; it writes the user settings, or the workspace settings when the workspace sets the key); keybinding `when` clauses now resolve `config.*` settings.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/document/{autosave,save}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{autosave,app,closing,editors,gridview,group,keys,save}.go`, `internal/core/{settings,command}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.1, §6.3, §10.3, §10.10, §11.2.
- **Tests:** 20 test functions in `core/document/autosave_test.go` (fake clock and real files: the debounce with edits at 0, 500 and 900 ms saving once at 1900 ms, cancellations, the delay rule, each mode against each event, eligibility, pause and resume, runtime changes, no timers left after dispose, the reason reaching the participants, `OnClose`, `OnShutdown`), 24 in `gui/app/autosave_test.go` (debounce, tab switching, focusing another widget, window focus with `SetViewFocus`, failure giving one notification and a pause resumed by Overwrite and by Revert, the Save As guard, closing an editor in each mode with the failed-save fallback, a mixed close batch with Cancel, Don't Save not undone, window close, the toggle command for user and workspace targets, the menu check and a click on it, `config.*` in the dispatcher), registration and menu tests; 2 new goldens (`save_file_menu_autosave_{dark,light}`; looked at) and `save_file_menu_{dark,light}` regenerated for the new Auto Save row (looked at).
- **Not ported:** VS Code's short and long delay modes and the `autoSaveAfterShortDelay` key, per-resource auto save overrides, the throttle that stops an auto save from undoing the user's undo, a status bar item.

## 7.4 External changes

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-7.4-external-changes
- **Summary:** files changed on disk (SPEC §3.1, §6.3, §10.3, §10.5). A watcher event is only a trigger: the window stats (and if needed reads) the file on a goroutine and applies the result on the UI thread (`Document.BeginExternal` → `ExternalJob.Probe` → `ApplyExternal`), so repeated or late events are harmless; a look is repeated if the document was saved, edited, reloaded or renamed meanwhile (at most 3 times), a save in flight defers it, a read that fails or a file that changes during the read is retried after 300 ms and 2 s and on the next event. A clean document reloads in its current encoding keeping cursors, scroll and folds (undo history is dropped as `Reload` does, kept when only the mtime changed). A dirty document is never touched: it is marked conflicted and one sticky warning per document says "The file 'x' has changed on disk and you have unsaved changes." with Overwrite (save with `IgnoreConflict`) and Revert (Compare comes with 9.6); the note goes away on save, revert or reload, when the file returns to the same stat, on close, or when the changes are undone (then the clean document reloads). A deleted file leaves the document orphaned with its text and dirty state kept; the tab and the Open Editors row are struck through in `list.errorForeground` (setting `workbench.editor.decorations.colors`), the tooltip reads "title • Deleted", with `workbench.editor.closeOnFileDelete` the clean editors close; saving recreates the file and clears the flag; when the file reappears a clean document reloads, a dirty one with the same stat just clears the flag, a dirty one with another stat is conflicted. Paired `Renamed` events re-point the documents through the code path shared with the Explorer operations (`followPaths`, refactored out of `followFileOp`: tab, language, recent files, history, every open file below a renamed folder; the echo of an own rename changes nothing); an unpaired rename is a delete. Deleted or renamed folders and Rescan events touch every open document below the path; untitled and virtual documents are ignored. Conflicted and orphaned documents are not auto saved (also not on close and shutdown).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/document/{external,document,edits,save,autosave}.go`, `internal/core/editorgroup/{input,textinput}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{external,app,editors,explorer,fileops,gridview,group,save,statuspickers,workspace}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/{tabs,theme}.go`, `internal/gui/views/explorer/openeditors.go`, `internal/core/{settings,theme}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.1, §6.3, §10.3, §10.5, §11.2.
- **Tests:** 27 top-level tests in `core/document/external_test.go` (every case in a temp folder, route tests for `ExternalNoFile`, `ExternalStatFailed`, `ExternalReadFailed`), 24 in `gui/app/external_test.go` (events through `Window.onFileEvents`; view preservation; the single notification and both buttons; auto save blocking; the 7.2 prompt; the `closeOnFileDelete` table; save recreating a file; directory rename and delete; Rescan; read-failure retries; two end-to-end tests with the real inotify watcher: the folder and a file outside it), the tabs strikethrough widget test, a deleted-input test, a color test; 4 goldens (`external_changes_{dark,light}`, `tabs_deleted_{dark,light}`; looked at).
- **Not ported:** Compare (9.6, `TODO(step 9.6)`), `workbench.editor.decorations.badges`, "(deleted)" in the tab name.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none new (the widget tester never disposes a shell, so `Dispose` hits `AncestorLookupOnInactiveElement`, as noted in 7.2; the watcher tests start and cancel the watching themselves).
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** a dirty document gets a notification as soon as the file changes (VS Code only reports a conflict at save time); Reload drops undo history; two goldens of tests not touched by this step (`grid_empty_group_light`, `tabs_drop_overlay_center_dark`) failed once in the first 13-minute `gui/app` race run and passed on rerun: load-dependent flakiness (see the test-determinism follow-up).

## 7.5 UI state and restore

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-7.5-ui-state
- **Summary:** the window's UI state is saved and restored (SPEC §10.10). `core/storage.StateStore` keeps `state.json` (global: recently opened folders and files with VS Code's MRU rules, 500 each, no repeats, git temp files skipped; the last window; the last geometry; the id of the empty window) and `windows/<id>.json` (one per window) under `XDG_STATE_HOME/emetos`, JSON with `version` 1, mode 0600 (folders 0700), written atomically through a debounced writer (a change is encoded on the UI thread 250 ms later and the store writes 1 s after that, as the SPEC says; close, quit and shell disposal write at once; a failed write is logged and retried on the next change; a corrupt or newer file is ignored with a warning and overwritten later). The window id is `WindowID(folder)`: the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the cleaned absolute path; the window without a folder gets a random id kept in `state.json`. A window's state holds the folder, the layout, the geometry (size, maximized, full screen; no position), the editor groups (tree and sizes, editors with pinned, preview and sticky state, order of use, active editor) with each editor's view state (cursors, scroll, folds; `ViewState` is now versioned JSON), the expanded Explorer folders and the quick-open recent list. Restore (`Window.PrepareRestore`, in `Run` before the window shows): the saved files open synchronously and the shell builds the grid from them; editors whose files are gone are dropped with one notification ("1 editor could not be restored because its file no longer exists." / "N editors could not be restored because their files no longer exist."); with no arguments the last window's folder reopens; a file argument opens on top of the restored editors; `window.restoreWindows=none` restores nothing; the geometry goes through `WindowConfig` (`window.restoreFullscreen` for full screen). File > Open Recent (Reopen Closed Editor, folders, files (10 each, only existing paths, `~` paths), More..., Clear Recently Opened...) and Ctrl+R (`workbench.action.openRecent`: a picker with "folders" and "files" sections and a remove button per row); `workbench.action.clearRecentFiles` asks for confirmation as VS Code does and clears both lists, the quick-open list and the editor history. Settings `window.restoreWindows` (default `all`), `window.restoreFullscreen` (false). Also in this commit range: the test infrastructure fix (below).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/storage/{windowid,recent,statefiles,writer,statestore,paths}.go`, `internal/core/storage/storagetest/clock.go`, `internal/core/editorgroup/{serialize,grid_state}.go`, `internal/core/workspace/recent.go`, `internal/gui/editor/viewstate_json.go`, `internal/gui/app/{state,recent,app,process,window,group,editors,explorer,workspace,closing,save}.go`, `internal/core/{command,keybinding,settings}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.1, §10.3, §10.10 ("UI state as built"), §11.2.
- **Tests:** 17 in `core/storage/state_test.go` plus the internal writer test and the clock test (round trips of every piece, debounce and flush with the manual clock, write failure and retry, corrupt and newer files, MRU rules, window id derivation, route tests for 9 tags: `StateFileUnreadable`, `StateFileCorrupt`, `StateVersionUnsupported`, `StateEncodeFailed`, `StateWriteFailed`, `StateFlushCancelled`, `WindowIDInvalid`, `WindowFolderNotAbsolute`, `WindowIDUnavailable`), 3 for the view state JSON (`ViewStateInvalid`, `ViewStateVersionUnsupported`), `SerializeWith` and layout JSON tests, 36 in `gui/app/state_test.go` (the end-to-end restart through `RequestClose` and a new window over the same isolated state, dropped files with the notice, a file argument on top, `none`, geometry through a fake controller, the startup folder, the recent menu, the picker, remove and clear, Ctrl+R through the dispatcher, the folder switch; tags `LayoutStateInvalid`, `SavedEditorNotAFile`, `OpenRecentPathInvalid`), 4 new goldens (`open_recent_{menu,picker}_{dark,light}`; looked at) and the File menu goldens regenerated for the new row (`save_file_menu_{dark,light}`, `save_file_menu_autosave_{dark,light}`; looked at).
- **Not ported:** hot exit and untitled documents (7.6), multi-window restore (7.8), persisted navigation stacks and recently closed editors, the active panel tab and side bar container (only the layout is saved), `workbench.action.quickOpenRecent`, the picker's open-in-window and unsaved-folder buttons.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ (`gui/app` about 5 minutes) · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none (the geometry goes through `WindowConfig`; the real controller notifies on metric changes, confirmed by reading Glutter's `refresh()`, but the geometry tracking was only tested through a fake controller)
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** opening a recent folder or using Open Folder in a live window does not restore that folder's saved state (restore happens at window start; 7.8 is the place); a file on the command line always opens on top of the restored editors (VS Code's `all`, `folders`, `one` replace them); with one window `preserve`, `all`, `folders` and `one` act the same; the picker placeholder is "Select to open"; the state files use the store's own atomic writer (mode 0600) rather than `fileio.Write` (0644); corrupt files are ignored and overwritten, not renamed.
- **Test infrastructure (same branch, commit 57e483a):** golden tests were flaky under load because `textmate` limits one pattern search to 100 ms (`regex.ScanTimeout`): on a loaded machine under `-race` the first search of a grammar timed out and the line was cached as plain. Tests now use `testutil.ScanTimeout` (30 s; `ProcessOptions.ScanTimeout` for the app) and every golden goes through `testutil.MatchesGolden(ts, name)`, which first settles every tokenizer in the tree (`testutil.SettleTokens`; `(*Editor).Tokenizer()` makes them findable). `testutil.New` now unmounts the widget tree at cleanup (marked `// Glutter:`, with a note in `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`), so windows and goroutines no longer pile up across tests and the `gui/app` race test went from 821 s to 282 s; `shellState` keeps `win` and `binding` from `InitState` so its `Dispose` paths work on an unmounted tree. `TestTheBreaksAreMadeInSteps` and the word wrap step loops no longer depend on wall time. Two more flakes fixed: `TestViewExpandsLazily` waits for the rows to load, and the save path prompt goldens pump little so the caret (which blinks every 500 ms of pumped time after the focus) is always on (the two goldens were regenerated with the caret visible; looked at).

## 7.6 Hot exit

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-7.6-hot-exit
- **Summary:** backups of unsaved work and their restore (SPEC §3.1, §10.10 "Hot exit as built (step 7.6)", §10.11). `storage.BackupStore` keeps one JSON file per document at `$XDG_STATE_HOME/emetos/backups/<window id>/<SHA-256 hex of the URI>.json` (mode 0600, atomic; fields `version`, `uri`, `content`, `encoding`, `bom`, `lineEnding`, `language`, `stat` (mtime ns, size, mode), `orphaned`), with `Write`, `Read`, `List`, `Discard`, `DiscardAll`, `Check` (removes unusable files and leftover `.tmp` files at start) and the crash marker `running` in the same folder. `storage.BackupTracker` (injectable clock) writes a backup 1 s after the last change of a dirty document (a burst makes one write), discards it when the document is clean again, closed with Save or Don't Save, or renamed (it moves), skips empty untitled and read-only documents and texts over 100 MiB (the user is told once and closing falls back to the dialog), and `Prepare`/`BackupBatch.Write` read the texts on the UI thread and write on a goroutine. `closeDecision(hotExit, reason, hasFolder, otherWindows)` replaces the 7.2 stub: `off` always asks; `onExit` backs up on quit or when the last window closes and asks when another window closes; `onExitAndWindowClose` also backs up when a window with a folder closes; with one window both modes back up on close; if a backup fails the 7.2 dialog asks instead and Cancel keeps the window open; Save or Don't Save in the dialog removes the window's backups; closing an editor, group or folder still asks and removes that document's backup. The signal path writes the backups and removes the marker. Restore (inside `PrepareRestore`, whatever `files.hotExit` says at start and `window.restoreWindows`): a file with a backup is read from disk and the backup text is put over it as one undoable edit (`Document.RestoreUnsaved`: dirty, one undo returns to the disk text, encoding, BOM, line ending and language from the backup); untitled backups come back with their numbers (`TextInput.Serialize` now serializes untitled inputs as `untitled:N`; new untitled documents take the lowest free number); a file changed on disk since the backup is restored conflicted with the backup's stat (the 7.4 sticky notification is the only one: "'x' has changed on disk since its backup was made..." with Overwrite and Revert; a save raises `FileModifiedSince`); a deleted file is restored orphaned with one warning for all such files; a backup that no saved editor shows opens at the end of the active group in URI order; a leftover crash marker with restored backups gives one info notification ("Emetos did not close properly the last time. The unsaved changes of N files were restored from backups."); a backup equal to the disk text or unrestorable is removed.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/storage/{backup,backuptracker}.go`, `internal/core/document/restore.go`, `internal/core/editorgroup/textinput.go`, `internal/gui/app/{backup,backuprestore,closing,state,window,group,app,process,external}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.1, §10.3, §10.10, §10.11.
- **Tests:** 8 in `storage/backup_test.go`, 19 in `backuptracker_test.go` (fake clock: delay and bursts, discards, rename, orphaned flag, empty untitled, mode `off`, shutdown flush, no timers left), 3 in `document/restore_test.go`, 19 in `gui/app/backup_test.go` (quit backs up without a dialog and the next start restores dirty text, untitled documents, groups and cursors; mode `off` asks and discards; changed on disk; deleted; a crash restored with `off` at the next start; a backup write failure falls back to the dialog; an editor close still asks; untitled numbering; a corrupt backup ignored; backups without editors; the signal path; the decision table); route tests for `BackupWriteFailed`, `BackupTooLarge`, `BackupURIInvalid`, `BackupReadFailed`, `BackupNotFound`, `BackupCorrupt`, `BackupDiscardFailed`, `BackupMarkerFailed`, `BackupWaitCancelled`, `SavedUntitledWithoutBackup` (`BackupEncodeFailed` has none: `json.Marshal` of a `Backup` cannot fail); no goldens (no new UI); the test helpers `newTestWindow`, `settingsWindow` and `newStateEnv` default to `files.hotExit: off` so the 7.2 dialog tests keep working.
- **Not ported:** multi-window rules (7.8, `otherWindows()` is a stub with `TODO(step 7.8)`), remote files, `window.confirmBeforeClose`, a "Restore Backup" command.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the SPEC's flat single-JSON layout is used instead of VS Code's `file/` and `untitled/` folders with a header line; no `backups.json` model (the folder is the model); the crash marker lives in the window's backup folder (the SPEC only said its state folder); `onExitAndWindowClose` follows VS Code and the 7.2 note (a window with a folder), not the original "any window" sentence of §10.10 (one line in `closeDecision`); read-only documents get no backup.

## 7.7 Command line and single instance

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-7.7-command-line
- **Summary:** SPEC §10.11's command line and `core/ipc`. `cmd/emetos` has a hand-written parser (`parseArgs`: `--x v`, `--x=v`, `-xv`, clustered `-nw`, `--`, repeated options, value counts) for `-n/--new-window`, `-r/--reuse-window`, `-g/--goto file[:line[:col]]`, `-d/--diff a b`, `-m/--merge p1 p2 base result`, `-w/--wait`, `--profile`, `--log`, `-v/--version`, `-h/--help`; `--help` and `--version` exit 0 before the window, the socket or the log is touched, a wrong command line exits 2, a failed request exits 1. `core/ipc` (the only package that imports `net`): a unix-socket server and client at `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/emetos-<uid>.sock` with `emetos-<uid>.lock` beside it (fallback `<tmp>/emetos-<uid>/emetos.sock` in a real 0700 folder owned by the user; a path over 107 bytes is `IPCSocketPathTooLong` and the editor then starts without single instance), socket mode 0600, one JSON object per line at most 1 MiB (`{version:1,type:"open",pid,cwd,open:{folder,files[{path,line,column}],diff,merge,wait,newWindow,reuseWindow,profile}}`, replies `ok`, `done` or `error` with `exitCode` and `message`), the peer uid checked with `SO_PEERCRED`, a request must arrive within 5 s, a non-wait reply within 30 s. The race of two launches: `Join` takes an exclusive `flock` on the lock file, tries to connect, and if nobody answers listens before releasing the lock (a refused connect removes the stale socket first), so exactly one launch becomes the instance and the others hand over; a path that is not a socket of the user is `IPCSocketBusy` and left alone; `Close` removes the socket only if it is still the file this server made (device and inode). `--wait` keeps the connection open and the instance replies `done` when every file of that launch is closed (the window closing first gives `done` 0; files asked for but none opened gives 1; a killed client drops the connection and cancels the wait). In the running window (`Window.HandleLaunch`, requests that arrive before the window is on screen wait in the `Launcher` queue): the window is raised through the controller's `Activate`, a different folder opens through Open Folder with the dirty prompts (Cancel keeps the folder but the files still open), files open kept with the first shown and the `--goto` position revealed and clamped, `--diff` and `--merge` show "The diff editor is not available yet." / "The merge editor is not available yet." and reply exit 1, `--profile` shows a warning and is ignored (`TODO(step 8.6)`), `--new-window` shows "Multiple windows are not available yet: this window is used." (7.8). The first instance's own command line goes through the same code (`Options.Open`).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/ipc/{message,location,join,server,client}.go`, `cmd/emetos/{main,args}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{launch,app,window,group,explorer,process}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §10.11, §12, §15.1, `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`.
- **Tests:** 32 in `core/ipc` (in-process: round trip, bad version, type, JSON and oversize messages, peer rejection, handler panic, stale socket takeover, a socket owned by another instance kept, concurrent clients, wait and done, a dropped client cancels the wait, graceful shutdown, lock timeout, a race of 6 joins; two-process tests that re-exec the test binary (`EMETOS_IPC_HELPER`, no window, each in its own process group with a deadline): hand-over with the cwd, `--wait` released over stdin, a killed client cancelling the wait, a stale socket after `kill -9` taken over by the next launch, and the 4-way race of launches over 3 rounds with exactly one server; every `IPC*` tag with a route test), 14 in `cmd/emetos` (parser tables for every option form and tag `ArgsUnknownOption`, `ArgsMissingValue`, `ArgsValueUnexpected`, `ArgsRepeated`, `ArgsConflict`, `ArgsStdinUnsupported`, `ArgsGotoInvalid`, `ArgsProfileInvalid`, `LogLevelInvalid`, `PathInvalid`; `run` exit codes; `handOver` against a real in-process server), 25 in `gui/app/launch_test.go` (`HandleLaunch`, `Launcher`, startup arguments, `WatchClose`); no goldens; nothing registered.
- **Not ported:** a new window for `--new-window` (7.8), the diff and merge editors (9.6, 9.7), profiles (8.6), `window.openFoldersInNewWindow` and `window.openFilesInNewWindow` (the SPEC lists neither and with one window they have no effect).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** the 6.6 entry now also covers `Activate`: `BaseWindowController` lacks it, so the window is raised through an interface assertion.
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the SPEC gave no fallback without `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (added the tmp folder one) and no exit code for the diff and merge notices (1, so `git difftool` sees a failure); the parser lives in `cmd/emetos` because SPEC §5.2 has no `cli` package; positional paths are literal (`main.go:3` is a file by that name) and positions come only from `--goto`, parsed strictly; the old `flag`-based parser is gone, so `-version` with one dash no longer works; the race detector makes every exiting helper process sleep 1 s, so the helpers run with `GORACE=atexit_sleep_ms=0`; the 43 MB binary `emetos` that `go build ./cmd/emetos` leaves in the repo root had been committed in step 3.1: it is untracked now and `/emetos` is in `.gitignore` (it stays in the history).

## 7.8 Multiple windows

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-7.8-multiple-windows
- **Summary:** several windows in one process (SPEC §5.1, §10.10, §10.11). `Process.Windows` (`app.Windows`) is the manager: the windows in creation order, the active one, `Open`, `NewWindow`, `OpenFolder` (by the placement rules), `HandleLaunch` (the IPC routing) and `RequestQuit`. Shared by all windows in the `Process`: the command definitions, menus, views, keymap, themes, languages, snippets, the state store and the launcher; per window: its own forked `command.Registry` (`Registry.Fork()`), its own `notify.Service`, shell, editors, backups, explorer, quick input and folder (settings changes reach every window through the shared store; the recent lists and the state are written from the UI thread only). The implicit first window is entry 0 of a Glutter `WindowManager` (shown through `windowHost`); other windows are regular window controllers with their own `windowDelegate`; a closed regular window is unregistered and destroyed, the implicit one is hidden and retired (Glutter cannot drop it). New Window (`workbench.action.newWindow`, Ctrl+Shift+N, File menu) opens an empty window with its own state id (the primary empty id or a fresh random one). Opening a folder follows VS Code's rules for each source (menu, recent, IPC, dev command): a folder already open elsewhere focuses that window, otherwise `window.openFoldersInNewWindow` (`default`, `on`, `off`) decides (Ctrl on a recent row opens a new window; `--new-window` and `--reuse-window` are honored), files follow `window.openFilesInNewWindow`; the same-window case disposes the window's services and recreates them after the 7.2 prompts (`replaceWindow`: a keyed rebuild of the shell), which also restores the new folder's saved state (the 7.5 follow-up). Closing one window while others remain asks per the 7.6 table and only about that window's documents; quit (Ctrl+Q, app exit request) asks window after window (two-phase `closeAsk` then `commitClose`), Cancel anywhere keeps everything, hot exit backs up all windows without asking, and closing the last window quits once. Restore (`Process.startupPlan`): the global state keeps `OpenWindows` and `LastWindow`; `window.restoreWindows` `preserve`, `all`, `folders`, `one`, `none` with command-line folders and files on top; windows with backups always restore. `workbench.action.switchWindow` ("Switch Window...", a picker of the windows); `dev.openFolderInNewWindow {"path"}`; `StateStore.DeleteWindow` removes the state of an explicitly closed extra empty window. Settings `window.openFilesInNewWindow` (default `off`), `window.openFoldersInNewWindow` (default `default`), `window.newWindowDimensions` (default `default`).
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/app/{windows,windows_commands,startup,process,window,app,closing,backup,launch,explorer,recent,state,autosave,devcommands,devnotify}.go`, `internal/core/command/{command,builtin,menus_default}.go`, `internal/core/keybinding/defaults.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `internal/core/storage/{statefiles,statestore,writer,backup}.go`, `internal/gui/workbench/workbench.go`, `internal/gui/views/problems/view.go`, `scripts/proof.sh`, `docs/SPEC.md` §5.1, §10.10, §10.11, §11.2, §15.2, `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`, `testdata/proof/7.8/`.
- **Tests:** 31 in `gui/app/windows_test.go` (the manager through `Windows()` and the harness, close order and Cancel, quit with dirty windows in two windows, restore of two windows in every mode, same-window folder replacement with leak checks, IPC routing incl. `--new-window` and `--wait` across windows, focus and active window, auto save on window change, the Switch Window picker, `parseOpenFolderInNewWindow` with tag `DevOpenFolderInvalid`), 7 more in `core/command`, `core/settings` and `core/storage` (`Fork`, the settings, `HasBackups`, `OpenWindows`, `DeleteWindow` with tags `WindowIDInvalid` and `StateDeleteFailed`), the keybinding and File menu rows; the six File menu and Open Recent goldens regenerated for the new "New Window  Ctrl+Shift+N" row (looked at).

## 8.1 Settings editor

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-8.1-settings-editor
- **Summary:** the settings UI (SPEC §3.2, §10.3 "Editor kinds", §11.1 "The settings editor as built"). Core: `settings.DefaultTOC`/`BuildCategories` port `settingsLayout.ts` (settings no layout group places go under "Extensions/<Segment>"), `WordifyKey`/`KeyToDisplayFormat`/`Title` port `settingKeyToDisplayFormat` ("Editor: Font Size"), `settings.UIModel` is the model of the UI (categories, rows by kind, `SetValue`, `SetText` with validation (tag `SettingInputInvalid`), `Reset`, the User/Workspace visibility rule: application and machine settings are hidden in the Workspace tab), `Store.Reset` removes only the key keeping the comments of other lines, and the `TextSource` seam (`Store.SetTextSource`): while an editor is open on a settings file the store reads that layer from the document text (mutex-guarded copy for the watcher goroutine) and a write applies its edit to the document as one undo step; a clean document is saved right away, a dirty one is only edited and the disk is left alone, with no document open the file is written as before; the store reloads when a document becomes clean (revert, reload, undo) and after each store edit; every settings writer goes through it, including the Auto Save toggle. GUI: `editorgroup.SettingsInput` (type id `emetos.settings`, a singleton that is never dirty, is not backed up and never asks on close; its view state (tab, category, scroll, query) is saved with the window state and restored) and the editor widget in `gui/settingsui`: a search box (a plain case-insensitive match of every word against the key, title and description; 8.2 replaces it), User and Workspace tabs (Workspace disabled without a folder), an "Open Settings (JSON)" button, the category tree, and variable-height rows with a modified bar, a checkbox, a number or text input with an error box for invalid values (nothing written until valid), an enum dropdown, and an "Edit in settings.json" link for arrays, objects and multi-type values, a gear menu with Reset Setting and Copy Setting ID. The editor-type seam: `gui/app/panes.go` defines `editorKinds()` (`editorKind{typeID, restore, newPane, icon}`; a `pane` has `build`, `focus`, `hasFocus`, `dispose`); the shell shows a pane below the tab strip, tabs use the icon of the kind; saving, history, the status bar and the editor commands do nothing for a pane input; 8.3, 9.5 and 9.7 add entries. Commands `workbench.action.openSettings` ("Settings", Ctrl+, , left out of the palette as in VS Code), `workbench.action.openSettings2` ("Open Settings (UI)"), `workbench.action.openWorkspaceSettings` (palette `when workbenchState != 'empty'`); the first two accept a search string or `{"query"}`; menu rows File > Preferences > Settings and the Manage menu Settings row; setting `workbench.settings.editor` (`ui` default, `json` makes Ctrl+, open the user `settings.json`); the `settings.*` colors of VS Code.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/settings/{store,builtin,textsource,layout,display,uimodel}.go`, `internal/core/editorgroup/settingsinput.go`, `internal/gui/settingsui/{editor,row,tabs,text}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{panes,settingspane,settingsdocs,window,workspace,group,gridview,state,groupcommands}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/{theme,listoverlay}.go`, `internal/core/{command,keybinding,theme}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.2, §10.3, §11.1, §11.2.
- **Tests:** 11 in `core/settings/uimodel_test.go` and 1 in `textsource_test.go` (categories, titles, visibility, validation, `Reset` incl. comments, the `TextSource` rule for open dirty, open clean and closed), 3 in `editorgroup/settingsinput_test.go`, 9 widget tests and a golden test in `gui/settingsui`, 21 in `gui/app/settings_test.go` (Ctrl+, opens and reveals instead of duplicating; each kind of row writes the right value to the isolated user file; an invalid number shows the error and writes nothing; Reset keeps the comments; the Workspace tab; the category tree; search; changes from outside; restore through the 7.5 state; the dirty `settings.json` case through the UI; commands and keys through the dispatcher), a color test in `gui/kit`; route tests for `SettingInputInvalid` and `SettingsInputInvalid`; 10 new goldens (`settings_{top,invalid,workspace,link,bool}_{dark,light}`; looked at `settings_top_dark` and `settings_invalid_light`) and 6 regenerated File menu goldens for the new Preferences row (`open_recent_menu_{dark,light}`, `save_file_menu_{dark,light}`, `save_file_menu_autosave_{dark,light}`; looked at two). Test fix on the way: the `tapDialog` helpers pump before looking for the dialog button (`TestABackupWriteThatFailsAtQuitFallsBackToTheDialog` of 7.6 failed once because it tapped the dialog in the frame that created it).
- **Not ported:** `@modified`, `@lang:` and `@id:` filters (8.2), per-language blocks, counts, a sticky header, a narrow layout, list and object widgets (arrays and objects use the link), description links and `#setting#` references (drawn as links, they do nothing yet), settings of a folder that is not the window's, a virtualized list (about 180 rows), TOC groups no Emetos setting can fill.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none (8.2 has it)
