## 8.2 Settings search and JSON views

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-8.2-settings-search
- **Summary:** search and the JSON commands (SPEC §7.1, §10.3, §11.1, §11.2). `settings.ParseQuery` (VS Code's regexes) and `UIModel.Search(target, text, LanguageResolver)`: `@modified` (settings with a value in the tab's file), `@id:a,b` (comma lists and several `@id:`; case-sensitive; a trailing `*` is a prefix), `@lang:<id or name>` (the language-overridable settings with the values of that language's `[lang]` block; editing writes the block, Reset removes the key and then the empty block), `@tag:`, `@feature:`, `@ext:`, `@hasPolicy`, `@stable` are stripped and ignored with a hint line; free text must match every word (case-insensitive) in the key (camelCase split), the title, the enum values or the description; ranking whole key word 4, key or title part 3, enum value 2, whole description word 2, description part 1, a whole typed key +1000; results are grouped by category in layout order with rows sorted by score (ties keep layout order), Commonly Used hidden while searching; `SearchResult.Summary()` gives "N Settings Found", "1 Setting Found", "No Settings Found". The editor shows the summary and the hints under the search box, a clear button and Escape to clear, tree labels with counts ("Font (4)"), and a "Language: Go" header with a note in `@lang:` mode. `workbench.action.openSettings` takes a string, `{"query"}` or `{"revealSetting":{"key"}}` (query `@id:key`). Commands (category Preferences, palette, VS Code ids): `workbench.action.openSettingsJson` ("Open User Settings (JSON)"), `workbench.action.openWorkspaceSettingsFile` ("Open Workspace Settings (JSON)", palette `when workbenchState != 'empty'`), `workbench.action.openRawDefaultSettings` ("Open Default Settings (JSON)"), `workbench.action.openGlobalSettings` ("Open User Settings"); the JSON commands create a missing file as `{\n}\n` and `revealSetting.key` puts the cursor on the key; the editor's "Open Settings (JSON)" button and the "Edit in settings.json" link now run these commands. `emetos-virtual:` read-only documents (`core/document/virtual.go`: `VirtualRegistry` with `Register`, `Has`, `Paths`, `Generate`, `VirtualProvider{Label,Language,Text}`; `Process.Virtual` registers `/defaultSettings.json` labelled "Default Settings", language `jsonc`; `Documents.OpenVirtual` regenerates on every open and refreshes an open document; never dirty, not saved, not backed up, serialized by URI and restored through the provider, an unknown provider drops the editor silently; Save and Save As on any read-only document say "Cannot save ... read-only" quietly). `settings.DefaultSettingsJSON` generates the default settings document: JSONC, tab-indented, a group comment per category ("// Text Editor > Font"), the description wrapped at 80 columns, allowed values, per-value descriptions, minimum, maximum and deprecated comments, `"key": default,` with no trailing comma, every registered setting once in layout order.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/settings/{search,defaultjson,uimodel,store}.go`, `internal/core/document/virtual.go`, `internal/core/editorgroup/textinput.go`, `internal/gui/settingsui/editor.go`, `internal/gui/app/{settingspane,virtualdocs,save,state}.go`, `internal/core/command/{builtin,menus_default}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §7.1, §10.3, §11.1, §11.2, `testdata/proof/8.2/config/settings.json`.
- **Tests:** 9 functions in `core/settings/search_test.go` (the `ParseQuery` table of 22 cases, a `Search` table of about 46 cases: each filter alone and combined, several ids, `@lang` with the block present and absent, case, word order, camelCase, empty query, hints, `@lang:zzz`, the Workspace tab, ranking, language rows with edit and reset keeping comments and the empty block removal), 3 in `defaultjson_test.go` (every registered setting exactly once, strict JSONC parse, defaults round trip, order, comments), 3 in `core/document/virtual_test.go`, 6 widget tests in `gui/settingsui/search_test.go`, 11 in `gui/app/settings_json_test.go` (the argument forms, file creation, `revealSetting`, the workspace file, the palette `when`, the button and link spies, the read-only default settings document, reopen regenerates, restore after restart, not backed up) plus a golden test; route tests for `VirtualProviderInvalid`, `VirtualProviderDuplicate`, `VirtualURIUnknown`, `VirtualGenerateFailed` (the tag `SavedEditorNotAFile` was removed as unreachable); 8 new goldens (`settings_{font,lang,empty}_{dark,light}` and `default_settings_{dark,light}`; looked at `settings_font_{dark,light}`, `settings_lang_dark`, `settings_empty_light`, `default_settings_{dark,light}`; not looked at: `settings_empty_dark`, `settings_lang_light` (looked at by me), the agent did not open `settings_empty_dark`) and 8 settingsui goldens regenerated for the count line, clear button, tree counts and ranking (`settings_{bool,invalid,link,workspace}_{dark,light}`; the agent viewed only `settings_workspace_dark`).
- **Not ported:** `@tag:`, `@feature:`, `@ext:`, `@hasPolicy`, `@stable`, the filter suggestions dropdown, fuzzy and remote search, "Also modified in" in the `@lang:` view, `revealSetting.edit`, `[lang]` blocks in the default settings document.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** `docs/implemented-proof-images/8.2-settings.png`, made with `scripts/proof.sh docs/implemented-proof-images/8.2-settings.png --config testdata/proof/8.2/config --commands '[["workbench.action.openSettings","font"]]' --` (the fixture sets only `editor.fontFamily` plus a comment line, so the modified bar shows on Font Family): "6 Settings Found", the tree "Text Editor (6)" and "Font (4)", rows Line Height, Mouse Wheel Zoom, Font Family (modified bar), Font Ligatures, Font Size

  ![8.2 settings](implemented-proof-images/8.2-settings.png)

## 8.3 Keybindings editor

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-8.3-keybindings-editor
- **Summary:** the keybindings UI (SPEC §3.2, §10.3, §11.2, §11.3 "The keybindings editor as built"). Core (`core/keybinding`): `Model` builds the rows from the default registry and the user file (a user `-command` entry hides the default binding; commands without bindings are listed), `Fetch` searches (free text over the command id, title, when and key label with highlight ranges, a quoted chord for an exact sequence match including two-chord sequences, `@source:user|default` with `system` as an alias of `default`), `FindConflicts` (another command with exactly the same chord sequence whose `when` clauses can both hold, unless one provably excludes the other through `Implies`/`Negate`; prefix shadowing is not reported), and `Editing` ports `keybindingEditing.ts` as minimal JSONC text edits (`core/jsonc.DiffEdit`) that keep comments and layout, entries written in the order key, command, when, args: `Add`; `Edit` (a user row is edited in place found by `FileEntry`, a default row gets the new entry plus `{key: oldKey, command: "-cmd", when: oldWhen}`); `UpdateWhen` (validated first: "Not a when-clause: ..."); `Remove` (a user entry is deleted, a default one gets the `-cmd` entry); `Reset` (deletes every entry of the command, `-command` entries included); `Overwrite` (deletes the conflicting user entries and adds `-cmd` entries for default ones); a missing, empty or comments-only file becomes the header comment plus `[]`. The same open-dirty / open-clean / closed rule as the settings applies to `keybindings.json` (a dirty document is edited in place, VS Code refuses), and a saved typed `keybindings.json` now takes effect; `Resolver.Replace` reloads the live bindings after each edit so a new binding works at once and a removed one stops. `DefaultKeybindingsJSON` generates the read-only `emetos-virtual:/defaultKeybindings.json` document (VS Code's line layout, no per-entry title comments). UI (`gui/keybindingsui`): a search box (placeholder "Type to search in keybindings", the record keys button, a clear button), the table Command | Keybinding | When | Source with highlighted matches, the id under the title, key caps (`kit.NewKeyCaps`), a hover pencil, double click or Enter to change, a context menu (Change Keybinding, Add Keybinding, Remove, Reset, Copy, Copy Command ID, Copy Command Title, Change When Expression, Show Same Keybindings), and the define box (records up to two chords through the dispatcher's recording mode, "N existing command(s) have this keybinding", clicking that line filters the table; Escape clears the recording and then closes the box; Backspace is recorded as a key, as in VS Code); with conflicts, Enter opens the "Keybinding conflicts" dialog with Keep Both (default), Overwrite and Cancel. `editorgroup.KeybindingsInput` (type id `emetos.keybindings`, a never-dirty singleton like `SettingsInput`, serialized with query, command and key) is the second entry of `editorKinds()`; tab label "Keyboard Shortcuts" (icon record-keys, since the Codicons have no keyboard glyph). Commands: `workbench.action.openGlobalKeybindings` ("Open Keyboard Shortcuts", Ctrl+K Ctrl+S, takes a string or `{"query"}`), `openGlobalKeybindingsFile` (creates `[]`), `openDefaultKeybindingsFile`, rows "Keyboard Shortcuts" in File > Preferences and the Manage menu, and `keybindings.editor.*` (`searchKeybindings` Ctrl+F, `clearSearchResults` Escape, `recordSearchKeys` Alt+K, `focusKeybindings` Ctrl+Down, `defineKeybinding` Enter, `addKeybinding` Ctrl+K Ctrl+A, `defineWhenExpression` Ctrl+K Ctrl+E, `removeKeybinding` Delete, `copyKeybindingEntry` Ctrl+C, and `resetKeybinding`, `showConflicts`, `copyCommandKeybindingEntry`, `copyCommandTitle` without keys) under the context keys `inKeybindings`, `inKeybindingsSearch`, `keybindingsSearchHasValue`, `keybindingFocus`, `whenFocus`; colors `tree.tableColumnsBorder`, `tree.tableOddRowsBackground`, `keybindingTable.headerBackground`, `keybindingTable.rowsBackground`.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/keybinding/{model,search,conflicts,editing,defaultjson,resolver,userfile,defaults}.go`, `internal/core/jsonc/edit.go`, `internal/core/editorgroup/keybindingsinput.go`, `internal/gui/keybindingsui/{editor,row,define,record}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/{keycaps,list,theme}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{userkeys,keybindingspane,panes,settingsdocs,virtualdocs}.go`, `internal/core/{command,theme}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.2, §10.3, §11.2, §11.3, `testdata/proof/8.3/config/keybindings.json`.
- **Tests:** 10 in `core/keybinding/model_test.go`, 10 in `editing_test.go` (every operation on an empty file and a file with comments, user and default entries, invalid files), 3 in `editorgroup/keybindingsinput_test.go`, 24 widget tests and a golden test in `gui/keybindingsui`, 1 in `gui/kit/keycaps_test.go`, 29 in `gui/app/keybindings_test.go` (Ctrl+K Ctrl+S opens and reveals; each argument form; search; the record button; the define box records `ctrl+shift+9`; Enter writes `keybindings.json` and the new binding works through the real dispatcher at once; the conflict dialog end to end for Overwrite, Keep Both and Cancel; Remove of a default adds the `-command` entry and the dispatcher stops reacting; Reset; Change When validation; the clipboard; the context menu; state restore; the menu rows; the dirty-document rule); route tests for `KeybindingsEditFileInvalid`, `KeybindingsEditNotArray`, `KeybindingEditKeyMissing`, `KeybindingEditCommandMissing`, `KeybindingEditUnbound`, `KeybindingEntryNotFound`, `KeybindingsEditFailed`, `KeybindingsFileWriteFailed`, `KeybindingsFileCreateFailed`, `KeybindingsInputInvalid` (`KeybindingsTableInvalid` has none: it is raised only if the model produces duplicate row ids); 18 goldens (`keybindings_{table,search,id,keys,empty,recording,define,conflicts,when}_{dark,light}`; the agent looked at all, I looked at `keybindings_conflicts_dark`; a "Recording Keys" badge that wrapped to two lines was fixed with `MaxLines 1`).
- **Not ported:** sort by precedence, "Show Default/User/System Keybindings", search history, resizable columns, `when` autocomplete, `args` editing, VS Code's JSON-editor define widget, the accept and reject `when` commands (Enter and Escape in the box do it), editor title menu actions, `@ext:` filters.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** `docs/implemented-proof-images/8.3-keybindings.png`, made with `scripts/proof.sh docs/implemented-proof-images/8.3-keybindings.png --config testdata/proof/8.3/config --commands '[["workbench.action.openGlobalKeybindings","copy line"]]' --` (re-run by me after the last code change; the fixture has one comment line and one user binding `ctrl+alt+d` on `editor.action.copyLinesDownAction`); it shows the Keyboard Shortcuts tab searching "copy line": the User row (Ctrl+Alt+D) first and the two Default rows (Copy Line Down, Copy Line Up)

  ![8.3 keybindings](implemented-proof-images/8.3-keybindings.png)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** Keep Both is the default dialog button and Keep Both and Overwrite behave the same at runtime (later bindings win; Overwrite also removes the others from the file); Reset removes all entries of the command; a dirty `keybindings.json` is edited in place; a `when` such as `isLinux` is serialised as `true` by the when-clause parser.

## 8.4 Theme and icon pickers

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-8.4-theme-pickers
- **Summary:** choosing themes (SPEC §3.2 "Themes", §8.4, §10.6, §10.8, §11.2). Core: `theme.Service.PickerGroups()` lists the themes for the picker grouped light, dark, high contrast (empty groups left out; Dark Modern or Light Modern first, then by label, user themes included) with `PickDescription` (the VS Code descriptions such as "Default Dark"), `FileIconChoices()` (None, "Disable File Icons", whose setting value is `null`, and "Seti (Visual Studio Code)", `vs-seti`), and `Service.Preview(name)`, `EndPreview()`, `Previewing()` (an `Apply` with different settings ends a preview, so a change in settings.json wins). Quick input: `PickOptions.OnActive(item, ok)` fires when the active item changes (not for the `ActiveID` item; mouse hover does not change it) and `PickOptions.OnHide(accepted)` fires once when the pick ends (accept, Escape, blur or being replaced by another quick input). App: `workbench.action.selectTheme` (Color Theme, Ctrl+K Ctrl+T) opens "Select Color Theme (Up/Down Keys to Preview)" with the theme in use active; moving the active item previews that theme in all windows without writing anything; Enter writes `workbench.colorTheme` (the label; also when it equals the default) to the workspace `settings.json` when the folder's file defines the key, otherwise to the user file, keeping comments (a settings file with syntax errors is not written: the theme reverts with the notification "Cannot save the color theme: ..."); Escape, blur, another quick input replacing the picker, or the window going away revert. `workbench.action.selectIconTheme` (File Icon Theme, no key) opens "Select File Icon Theme (Up/Down Keys to Preview)": preview, Enter writing `null` or `"vs-seti"` with the same target rule, Escape revert; `Process.PreviewFileIconTheme`, `EndFileIconThemePreview`, `FileIconThemePreview`, `OnFileIconThemePreview` make the explorer, Open Editors, quick open, recent and the editor switcher refresh on a preview, and None draws no icon column. A `ThemesSubMenu` ("Themes": Color Theme, File Icon Theme) is in File > Preferences and in the Manage menu.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/theme/{pick,service}.go`, `internal/gui/quickinput/controller.go`, `internal/gui/app/{themepickers,app,explorer,groupcommands,process,quickinput,theme}.go`, `internal/core/{command,keybinding}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.2, §8.4, §10.6, §10.8, §11.2, `testdata/proof/8.4/`.
- **Tests:** 8 in `core/theme/pick_test.go` (grouping and order, descriptions, the choices, `Preview` with the route `Service.find.ThemeNotFound`), `TestControllerPickHooks`, 22 in `gui/app/themepickers_test.go` (open with the current item active; Down previews while the settings file is unchanged; Escape reverts; Enter writes with comments kept; the workspace-override target rule; blur reverts; user themes; two windows both change and revert; an external settings change ends the preview; the icon picker with None removing icons from the explorer rows and quick open and Seti restoring them; menus and palette), 5 golden tests, `TestThemeMenus`; 13 new goldens (`theme_picker_{dark,light}`, `theme_picker_preview_{light_over_dark,dark_over_light,high_contrast_over_dark}`, `icon_theme_picker_{dark,light}`, `explorer_no_icons_{dark,light}`, `themes_menu_{dark,light}`, `manage_menu_themes_{dark,light}`; the agent looked at all of them).
- **Not ported:** Browse and Install Additional Color Themes, the auto-detect color scheme button and the preferred dark and light themes, Product Icon Theme, Try New Default Themes, the `workbench.colorCustomizations` UI, VS Code's 200 ms preview delay.

## 8.5 Zoom, zen mode, full screen

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-8.5-zoom-zen
- **Summary:** SPEC §10.9. `kit.Zoom` sits in `WidgetsApp.Builder` above the navigator (pages and every overlay are inside it): a `LayoutBuilder` giving an `OverflowBox` a child of `Transform.scale(f)` laid out in size/f, with `MediaQuery.size` = size/f and `devicePixelRatio` × f; layout, painting and hit testing go through the same matrix, text stays sharp (Glutter draws glyph outlines under the transform), the tree does not depend on the factor so widget state survives a zoom change; window coordinates equal overlay coordinates × f, so `kit.GlobalToOverlay` is used by the context menu and the tab-drag label layer. The factor is 1.2^level (`settings.ZoomFactor`, levels -8..8 as the reference's `MAX_ZOOM_LEVEL`, not 9). `window.zoomLevel` (application scope) and `window.zoomPerWindow` (the actions change the window's own level, kept in the window state field `ZoomLevel`; with `zoomPerWindow` false they write the setting; a settings change resets every window). Commands `workbench.action.zoomIn` (Ctrl+=, Ctrl+Shift+=, Ctrl+NumPad_Add), `zoomOut` (Ctrl+-, Ctrl+NumPad_Subtract), `zoomReset` (Ctrl+NumPad0). Full screen: `workbench.action.toggleFullScreen` (F11) calls `SetFullscreen` on the window controller by interface assertion (`BaseWindowController` lacks it; a window without a controller fails with `FullScreenUnavailable`); the state comes from the controller listener, so the `isFullscreen` context key, the check mark and the saved geometry stay right. Zen mode (`workbench.action.toggleZenMode`, Ctrl+K Z; `workbench.action.exitZenMode` on `escape escape` when `inZenMode`): the zen state lives in `LayoutState.Zen` (what was hidden, which parts the user changed meanwhile, whether zen turned on full screen, the centered layout or do-not-disturb); entering hides the side bar, panel and menu bar always, the activity bar and status bar per their settings, and applies `zenMode.fullScreen`, `zenMode.centerLayout` and `zenMode.silentNotifications`; tabs (`zenMode.showTabs`) and line numbers are derived overrides, the settings files are never written (a test asserts it byte for byte); exiting restores the hidden parts except the ones the user changed during zen and turns off what zen turned on; leaving full screen by F11 ends zen when zen made the full screen; the `zenMode.*` settings apply live; `zenMode.restore` brings a window back in zen. Centered layout (`workbench.action.toggleCenteredLayout`): margins of 0.1909 of the width each (at least 60 px, the editor keeps at least 220 px; fixed-width mode 900 px), only with at most one column of groups or a maximized group (nothing is lost when a second group opens), saved in `LayoutState.CenteredLayout`. View > Appearance rows Full Screen, Zen Mode, Centered Layout (checked) and Zoom In, Zoom Out, Reset Zoom; settings `zenMode.{fullScreen,centerLayout,showTabs,hideStatusBar,hideActivityBar,hideLineNumbers,restore,silentNotifications}`, `workbench.editor.centeredLayoutAutoResize`, `workbench.editor.centeredLayoutFixedWidth`; context keys `isFullscreen`, `inZenMode`, `isCenteredLayout`.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/kit/{zoom,contextmenu}.go`, `internal/core/settings/{zoom,builtin}.go`, `internal/gui/workbench/{layout,workbench,commands}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{zoom,zen,centered,app,window,windows_commands,state,editors,gridview,group,panes,tabmenu,tabdrag}.go`, `internal/core/storage/statefiles.go`, `internal/core/{command,keybinding}`, `docs/SPEC.md` §10.9, §10.10, §11.2, `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`.
- **Tests:** 4 in `core/settings/zoom_test.go`, 7 in `kit/zoom_test.go` (constraints and MediaQuery under zoom, hit testing, scroll and drag, state kept across factor changes, a context menu under zoom at three factors), 10 in `app/zoom_test.go` (the keys through the dispatcher including numpad, per-window and all-window zoom, two-window propagation, persistence), 15 in `app/zen_test.go` (each `zenMode.*` setting alone, live changes, byte-for-byte settings files, restart with `zenMode.restore` on and off, two windows, Ctrl+K Z, Escape Escape, F11 interplay, full screen through a fake controller and the real tester window controller), 5 in `app/centered_test.go`, 3 in `workbench/layout_test.go`; route test for `FullScreenUnavailable`; 14 new goldens (`zoom_in_*`, `zoom_out_*`, `zen_mode_*`, `zen_mode_single_tab_*`, `centered_layout_*`, `appearance_menu_*` in the app and `zoom_{dark,light}` in kit) and `settings_top_{dark,light}` regenerated for the new "Zen Mode" entry in the table of contents (the agent viewed all; I looked at `zoom_in_dark` and `zen_mode_light`). Existing tests changed: `TestChordsAndModifierKeys` and `TestTheChordMessageShowsAndClears` used Ctrl+K Z as an unbound chord (zen mode now), they use Ctrl+K Y; `TestEveryDefaultKeybindingRunsItsCommand` refocuses the editor per subtest because the new `exitZenMode` subtest unfocuses it.
- **Not ported:** draggable centered margins, `workbench.action.zenHideEditorTabs`, `zenShowMultipleEditorTabs`, `zenShowSingleEditorTab` and the zen Tab Bar submenu, the editor font zoom commands changing window zoom, `window.density`, zoom for webviews, the diff-editor rule for centering.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.

- **Manual checks:** pending, see docs/MANUAL-CHECKS.md ("8.5 Full screen and zoom on a real compositor")

- **Deviations and follow-ups:** `zenMode.hideTabs` is not registered (`zenMode.showTabs` replaces it, as in the reference); at zoom level 8 the window is about 300 px wide and the parts overflow; a 1 px border is anti-aliased at fractional levels; the wheel scrolls the same number of logical pixels (f times as many on screen); in full screen the `classic` menu bar behaves like `toggle`.

## 8.6 Profiles

- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Branch:** feat/step-8.6-profiles
- **Summary:** SPEC §3.2 "Profiles", §10.10–§10.12, §15.2. Core: `storage.Profiles` (`NewProfiles(paths, opts)`) with `List` (the default first, the others by name, case-insensitive), `Lookup`, `Exists`, `Path`, `Create` (`CreateEmpty` or `CreateFrom(profile)`, copying only `settings.json`, `keybindings.json` and `snippets/`, atomically through a temporary folder and a rename), `Ensure` (creates an empty profile for an unknown `--profile`), `Rename` (moves the config and state folders, rolls back on failure, rewrites the references), `Delete`, `LastProfile`/`SetLastProfile`; `ValidateName`/`ValidProfileName` is the single name rule (1–64 letters, digits, spaces, `-`, `_`; no leading/trailing space; case-insensitive clashes; `Default` reserved), also used by `cmd/emetos` for `ArgsProfileInvalid`. `GlobalState.LastProfiles` and `OpenWindow.Profile` are additive fields. `settings.Store.SetUserPath` and `snippet.Service.SetDir` rebind a layer in place (a stale reload is discarded). App: each `Window` has a profile (`Profile()`, `ProfileName()`, `OnProfileChange`) and `SwitchProfile(ctx, name)` rebinds, without closing anything, the user settings layer (the store's change events drive every `Settings.OnChange` consumer: title, autosave, backups, editors, explorer, themes, languages, zoom, zen, the settings editor), the keybindings (resolver replaced, keybindings editor rebuilt), the snippets, the config watcher and the state store (the old profile's window state is flushed first; later saves go to the new profile; the UI state is not restored on a switch). Themes and snippets became per-window services (`Window.Themes`, `Window.Snippets`). Launch rules: `--profile` > the restored window's profile > the folder's last profile > the default; a running instance opens a new window for a folder not yet open, focuses a folder already open with another profile with the notice "The folder is already open with the profile 'X'.". The `TODO(step 8.6)` warning is gone. `dev.switchProfile {"name"}` is the entry point for tests and for 8.6b.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/storage/{profiles,statefiles,statestore}.go`, `internal/core/settings/store.go`, `internal/core/snippet/files.go`, `cmd/emetos/{args,main}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{profile,process,window,windows,windows_commands,devcommands,startup,state,launch,workspace,userkeys,settingsdocs,keybindingspane,theme,themepickers,editors,explorer,app}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §10.10–§10.12, §15.2.
- **Tests:** 20 in `core/storage/profiles_test.go` (name tables, clashes, create sources and the exact file set, atomicity with injected failures, rename, delete, default immutable, order, last profile and additive `state.json`), `TestStoreSetUserPath`, `TestServiceSetDir`, 16 in `gui/app/profiles_test.go` (live switch of theme, font size, tab size, a rebound key, snippets with editors, cursors, scroll and documents untouched, switching back, both editors rebuilt, an old `settings.json` kept as a file, state saved to the new profile, two windows with different profiles, the watcher following the switch, `--profile` rules for a first and a running instance, last profile per folder, restore with profiles, failure routes for `dev.switchProfile`); route tests for `ProfileNameInvalid`, `ProfileNameReserved`, `ProfileExists`, `ProfileNotFound`, `ProfileDefaultImmutable`, `ProfileListFailed`, `ProfileCreateFailed`, `ProfileCopyFailed`, `ProfileRenameFailed`, `ProfileDeleteFailed`, `ProfileFolderInvalid`, `ProfileSwitchNotFound`, `ProfileSwitchFailed`, `DevSwitchProfileInvalid`. Existing tests changed: `TestValidProfileName` moved to storage, two notice expectations in `launch_test.go`, `windows_test.go` and the theme picker tests use `Window.Themes`/`Window.Snippets`.
- **Not ported:** profile-specific extension lists, temporary profiles, sync, `${profileName}` in the window title (8.6b), commands, pickers, status bar item and export/import (8.6b).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** no shared refcounted per-profile runtime: the settings store, key resolver and user keys were already per window, so windows with different profiles simply own services over their profile's files; user themes, `state.json`, backups and recents are shared by all profiles; the restore record is `OpenWindow.Profile` (no `WindowState.Profile`); a folder without a record opens with the default profile (VS Code uses the active window's); `files.associations` follows the last window that applied it because the language registry is process-wide; `window.restoreWindows` is still read from the default profile's settings at start.

## 9.1 Line diff

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9.1-line-diff
- **Summary:** SPEC §9.11, `core/diff`. A port of VS Code's `defaultLinesDiffComputer` up to, and excluding, the character refinement: `ComputeLines(ctx, original, modified, Options{IgnoreTrimWhitespace, MaxComputationTime, Now})` gives `LinesDiff{Changes []LineRangeMapping, HitTimeout}` (1-based line ranges with an exclusive end; `Inner []RangeMapping` is there for 9.2). Lines are hashed trimmed (JS-style `trim`); dynamic programming when both texts together have fewer than 1700 lines, Myers otherwise (DP scores an equal line 0.1 when empty, `1 + ln(1 + utf16len)` otherwise, 0.99 when equal only after trimming); the line-stage heuristics `optimizeSequenceDiffs` (join by shifting, shift to the best boundary score by indentation) and `removeVeryShortMatchingLinesBetweenDiffs`; the shortcuts of `computeDiff`; the line-stage part of `scanForWhitespaceChanges` (a line equal after trimming but not as written is a change, unless `IgnoreTrimWhitespace`). A timeout (`DateTimeout`, injectable clock) returns the trivial one-change diff with `HitTimeout`; the context is checked every 1024 steps (`DiffCancelled`). `Apply` rebuilds the modified lines from the original and the changes; `Validate` checks sortedness, bounds and equal unchanged gaps (`DiffInvalid`).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/diff/{doc,types,timeout,sequence,dp,myers,optimize,lines,apply}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.11.
- **Tests:** 31 top-level tests (188 with subtests, 99.5% coverage): 30 line diff table cases (each also `Validate`d and `Apply`d), heuristics, 500 random sequence pairs (Myers equals the LCS-optimal cost), the 1700 threshold, 600 seeded random `Apply == modified` cases, large cases up to 4000 lines, timeout with a stepping fake clock, cancellation mid-DP and mid-Myers, route tests for `DiffCancelled`, `DiffTimeoutInvalid`, `DiffAlignmentBroken` and 17 `DiffInvalid` cases; `fixtures_test.go` runs the 58 fixture folders of VS Code: 53 match `advanced.expected.diff.json` on the line ranges, the other 5 (`class-replacement`, `noisy-move1`, `shifting-twice`, `ts-confusing-2`, `ts-example1`) differ only where 9.2's refinement splits or trims a block and are validated and applied only (skipped when `reference/` is absent).
- **Not ported:** moved code detection (SPEC §2), the character stage (9.2).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** columns are byte-based, not UTF-16; no settings registered (9.2 registers `diffEditor.maxComputationTime`, the diff editor `diffEditor.ignoreTrimWhitespace`); after the limit the line stage gives the trivial diff like VS Code's algorithm, 9.2 confirms the SPEC wording ("the unrefined line diff").

## 9.2 Character diff and limits

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9.2-char-diff
- **Summary:** SPEC §9.11, `core/diff`. `Compute(ctx, original, modified, Options)` runs the line stage of 9.1 and then VS Code's `refineDiff` on every changed block: `LinesSliceCharSequence` (code points as elements, `translateOffset/Range`, `findWordContaining`, `findSubWordContaining`, `extendToFullLines`, boundary scores by character category), dynamic programming under 500 characters and Myers above, `optimizeSequenceDiffs`, `extendDiffsToEntireWordIfAppropriate` (the subword pass only with `Options.ExtendToSubwords`, which VS Code's diff editor does not set), `removeShortMatches`, `removeVeryShortMatchingTextBetweenLongDiffs`, the equal-lines white-space scan, and `lineRangeMappingFromRangeMappings`; `Inner` holds the character changes. One clock covers both stages: after the limit the block being aligned and every block after it get one character change over the whole block (as the reference does), blocks refined earlier keep theirs, `HitTimeout` is set; the SPEC wording "the unrefined line diff" was corrected (`ComputeLines` stays for callers that want lines only). `Validate` also checks the inner ranges. Columns are byte columns (SPEC D6); VS Code's UTF-16 limits count code points here. Setting `diffEditor.maxComputationTime` registered (number, 5000, 0 = no limit, scope language-overridable, category Text Editor); nothing reads it yet (9.5), `diffEditor.ignoreTrimWhitespace` comes with its consumer.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/diff/{charseq,charopt,rangemap,refine,lines,types,apply,doc}.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.11, the settings editor groups paragraph.
- **Tests:** 68 top-level tests in `core/diff` (98.8% coverage): `TestVSCodeFixtures` compares all 58 VS Code fixtures with `advanced.expected.diff.json` (line blocks and every character change, UTF-16 columns converted, `trimws` folders with ignore trim whitespace, moves ignored; no skips; a mutation of the `removeShortMatches` threshold makes 7 fail), the ported `LinesSliceCharSequence`, `lineRangeMapping` and Myers tests of `defaultLinesDiffComputer.test.ts`, each heuristic, `Compute` tables (white space with and without ignore, byte columns, emoji, subwords), time limits (line stage with DP and Myers, character stage, none, a real 100 ms), cancellation in 5 places, 700 seeded random cases (columns exact, the changes rebuild the modified text), a 3000-line file with 60 edits, route tests for `DiffRangeInvalid`, `DiffAlignmentBroken` and the `Validate` inner cases; `TestDefaultRegistry` row and a Diff Editor group test in `core/settings`. Goldens `settings_top_{dark,light}` regenerated: the table of contents shows "Diff Editor" under Text Editor (viewed; nothing else changed).
- **Not ported:** moved code (SPEC §2).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ (the gui/app suite ran before the last change, which touches `core/diff` only) · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.

## 9.3 Three-way merge model

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9.3-merge-model
- **Summary:** SPEC §9.11, `core/diff` (no UI). The marker parser `ParseConflictMarkers(lines)` ports `mergeConflictParser.ts` (`<<<<<<<`, optional `|||||||`, `=======`, `>>>>>>>` at column 0, labels, CRLF; as in the reference an unbalanced scan gives up, it does not restart). `MergeInputs` (Base, Input1 = incoming, Input2 = current, labels, EOL, `MarkedConflicts`) comes from three files (`MergeInputsFromFiles`) or from a marker file (`MergeInputsFromMarkers`, `MergeInputsFromMarkerText`: the base is the `|||||||` sections or empty regions; a conflict with no base stays an unhandled conflict through `MarkedConflicts`). `ModifiedBaseRangesFromDiffs` is the diff3 of `modifiedBaseRange.ts` and `mapping.ts`: changes whose base ranges intersect or touch are joined (one unchanged line keeps them apart), with `IsEqualChange`, `IsConflicting`, `TextForState` and the states `RangeState` (base, input1, input2, both with the order of the inputs). `MergeModel` (`NewMergeModel(ctx, inputs, opts)`): the result starts as the base with every change only one input made (or both made alike) applied and every conflict holding the base text, unhandled; regions are tracked through edits (`ApplyResultEdit`; a manual edit makes a region unrecognized or the state its text equals); `Accept(i, Choice)` with `AcceptBase`, `AcceptInput1` (incoming), `AcceptInput2` (current) and `AcceptBoth` (current then incoming), `AcceptAll`, `Reset`, `Toggle`, `SetState`, `SetHandled`, `Conflicts`, `UnhandledCount`, `HandledCount`, `Result`, `ResultText` (joined with the file's EOL: CRLF in, CRLF out) and the `Edit{StartLine, EndLine, NewLines}` each change returns, so the editor can apply it as one undoable edit. The model is a value API, not safe for concurrent use.
- **Main files:** `internal/core/diff/{markers,mergeinputs,modifiedbase,mergestate,mergemodel,doc}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.11.
- **Tests:** 184 passing tests and subtests (the merge-conflict extension has no tests in `reference/`, so the cases are our own): 56 parser tables (balanced, diff3, labels, unbalanced, stray markers, 8-character markers, CRLF, empty sections, file start and end), 52 diff3 and state tests (one input changed, identical, differing, adjacent, one line between, same-place insertions, deletion against modification, empty base and inputs, the git documentation example, range invariants), 83 model tests (every choice, shifting of later conflicts, `AcceptAll`, `Reset`, `Toggle`, manual edits, counts, the marker flow with and without a base, CRLF), 900 seeded random cases in 3 tests (union merges, every choice against an independent expected text, random operations with region invariants after each step); route tests for `MergeConflictIndexInvalid`, `MergeChoiceInvalid`, `MergeStateInvalid`, `MergeEditInvalid`, `MergeEOLInvalid`, `MergeMarkersNotFound`, `MergeRangeWithoutChange`, `DiffCancelled` and `DiffTimeoutInvalid`.
- **Not ported:** smart combination of both inputs, re-deriving the states from a result text, `getResultValueWithConflictMarkers`, `mergeEditor.diffAlgorithm`.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** `AcceptBoth` is current then incoming (SPEC), not VS Code's smart combination; a state of input 1 with input 2 switched off keeps the state (VS Code returns the state of input 2, which looks like a slip); the `--merge` command line wiring and the UI are 9.7 and 9.8.

## 9.4 View zones

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9.4-view-zones
- **Summary:** SPEC §9.1. Rows inserted between the lines of an editor (VS Code's view zones / whitespace). `ViewModel` API: `AddZone(ZoneSpec{AfterLine, Rows, Ordinal, ShowInHiddenAreas, Widget})`, `ChangeZone`, `SetZoneRows`, `SetZoneAfterLine`, `RemoveZone`, `ChangeZones` (a batch with one notification), `Zone`, `Zones`, `ZoneAtRow`, `ZoneAtY`, `SetZonePressHandler`, `DisposeZones`, `RenderEditorText.ZoneAt`. Units are whole rows (`Rows >= 1`), so the row Y stays uniform and the scroll, minimap and scrollbar math is untouched. `AfterLine` counts the lines above (0: above the first line); zones sort by the row they follow, then `Ordinal`, then insertion; a zone after a wrapped line comes after all its rows; lookups are binary searches over prefix sums (O(log z)). Folding follows `viewZones.ts`: a zone is shown only if the line after it is not hidden (so a zone after a fold header is hidden, one after the last hidden line is shown after the header row), `ShowInHiddenAreas` forces it. Zones follow edits as VS Code's `onLinesDeleted/Inserted` do (lines inserted above push the zone down; deleting the anchor moves it after the line before the deleted ones; shrinking clamps; `SetText` puts every zone after line 1). The projections gained `RowZone`, `LineZoneRows`, `ZoneRows`, `ZonesIn`, `ZoneRowsBefore` (the identity, folded and wrapped ones return no zones; no zoned projection exists without zones). Zone rows: no line number, folding control or glyph margin content; no current-line highlight, selection, find matches, range highlights, guides, whitespace marks or fold background; a press moves no cursor and starts no selection (the press handler hears the primary button); dragging over a zone maps to the nearest text row; Up/Down skip zone rows, Page Up/Down count the viewport rows minus zone rows; reveal uses the text row; the scroll extent includes zones; the minimap draws them blank. The optional widget is laid out in the zone's rows, as wide as the text area, scrolls vertically but not horizontally, clipped; only zones within 20 rows of the viewport are built.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/editor/{zones,zoneproj,zonelayer,projection,wrapproj,viewmodel,widget,pointer,render,geometry,gutter,overlay,display_paint,guides,find_paint,minimap_paint}.go`, `internal/gui/app/editors.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §9.1.
- **Tests:** 24 top-level tests and 52 subtests in `zones_test.go` and `zones_widget_test.go`: projection tables with zones, folds and wraps together, a seeded random test (300 rounds against a naive oracle with every projection query checked), `followLines` tables, edit following, shrink and clamp, add/change/remove and batch notification, route tests for `EditorZoneNotFound` and `EditorZoneInvalid`, widget tests (scroll extent, reveal, press and handler, Up/Down/Page Down, drag, selection rows and current line, wrapped lines, folding, widget placement while scrolling, minimap, ruler, sticky scroll); 4 goldens `zones_{selection,cursor}_{dark,light}` (wrap, a fold, a blank zone, a colored widget zone, a hidden zone; viewed); every earlier golden is unchanged; no existing test changed.
- **Not built:** pixel and zero heights, per-zone horizontal scroll, `afterColumn`, margin widgets in the gutter, zones in the minimap or ruler, accessibility for zones, a zone widget taking the mouse before the editor, a cursor shape change over zones.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the folding rule is VS Code's (a zone after a fold header is hidden), not "a zone after the header stays visible"; the widget layer rebuilds on every scroll tick (a custom render object could avoid it).

## 9.5 Side-by-side diff editor

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9.5-diff-editor
- **Summary:** SPEC §3.1 "Diff editor", §9.11. `gui/diffeditor`: two editors in a fixed 50/50 row with a 1 px divider and an overview ruler on the right; the original side read-only (new `editor.Options.ReadOnly`, every edit path refused with `EditorReadOnly`), the modified side editable when its document is a file. `ComputeAlignment` (a Glutter-free port of `computeRangeAlignment`, wrapped lines included) gives the view zones of step 9.4 that align both sides, drawn with a diagonal fill (`diffEditor.diagonalFill`). Changed lines and characters are background marks (new `editor.BackgroundMark`, `SetBackgroundMarks`) in the `diffEditor.*` colors, with gutter strips; scrolling is synced both ways (new `AddScrollListener`, `ScrollTo`). The diff runs on a goroutine 200 ms after the last change (`DebounceDelay`), a new change or setting cancels it, results of an older document version are dropped; `diffEditor.maxComputationTime` and `diffEditor.ignoreTrimWhitespace` (registered here, default true) apply live; a timed-out result is shown as computed. Next/previous change (`workbench.action.compareEditor.nextChange`/`previousChange`, Alt+F5/Shift+Alt+F5 when `textCompareEditorVisible`) wraps around and centers the change; `focusPrimarySide`, `focusSecondarySide`, `focusOtherSide`, `diffEditor.switchSide`. `editorgroup.DiffInput` (type `emetos.diff`, title "a ↔ b"): save, Save All, auto save and closing act on the modified document (`EditedDocument`), `PlanClose` treats inputs editing the same document as one, serialization as `{original, modified}` URIs with restore (a missing file drops the editor). `dev.openDiff {"original","modified"}` takes paths, `file://`, `emetos-virtual:` or untitled URIs. 12 colors and the context keys `textCompareEditorVisible`, `textCompareEditorActive`, `isInDiffEditor`, `diffEditorOriginalWritable`, `diffEditorModifiedWritable` registered.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/diffeditor/{align,navigate,translate,colors,options,model,filler,ruler,widget}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{readonly,marks,viewmodel,commands,clipboard,gutter,overlay,pointer,widget}.go`, `internal/core/editorgroup/{diffinput,input,textinput,closeplan}.go`, `internal/core/{command,keybinding,settings,theme}`, `internal/gui/app/{diffpane,app,autosave,closing,devcommands,editors,gridview,group,groupcommands,panes,save,state}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.1, §9.11, §15.2.
- **Tests:** 37 test functions in `gui/diffeditor` (alignment tables and a property test over 400 seeds, wrapped lines, navigation, position translation, options, the model), 18 in `app/diffpane_test.go` (a kept tab, read-only original, dirty mark, Ctrl+S and Save All on the modified side, the close prompt, a document shared with a text editor, splitting, Alt+F5/Shift+Alt+F5, focus commands, `switchSide`, live settings, `dev.openDiff` routes `DevOpenDiffInvalid` and `DevOpenDiffSideMissing`, virtual and untitled sides, restore, missing files, hot exit), 7 in `editor` (read-only with the `EditorReadOnly` route, marks), 7 in `editorgroup` (`DiffInputInvalid` route), a theme colors test; goldens `diff_{dark,light}` and `diff_wrapped_{dark,light}` (viewed); no earlier golden changed. Existing tests changed: the settings registry rows and the Diff Editor group, the app-command skip maps of `workbench_test.go` and `commands_cover_test.go`, `TestEveryDefaultKeybindingRunsItsCommand` (a diff editor state for Alt+F5).
- **Not ported:** collapsing unchanged regions, moved code, a sash, the accessible diff viewer (F7), revert arrows, an editable original, swap sides, editor title buttons, the inline view (9.6).

## 9.6 Inline diff and compare commands

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9.6-inline-diff
- **Summary:** SPEC §3.1, §9.11, §10.5, §10.11. The inline view: one editor (the modified one); each change with original lines gets a view zone above it (`AfterLine` = the modified start line - 1, `Rows` = the original lines) whose widget (`editor.NewLinesView`) draws the deleted lines with the original's font, syntax and bracket colors, `removedLineBackground`, the removed-character marks and a minus sign in the zone's gutter strip (`ZoneSpec.Gutter`, `GutterGlyph`), following the horizontal scroll. The mode is inline when `diffEditor.renderSideBySide` is off, or `diffEditor.useInlineViewWhenSpaceIsLimited` is on and the width is at most `diffEditor.renderSideBySideInlineBreakpoint` (900); switching keeps the same modified editor element (cursor and scroll kept); the ruler and next/previous change work in both modes. Commands `toggle.diff.renderSideBySide`, `diffEditor.toggleUseInlineViewWhenSpaceIsLimited`, `diffEditor.setViewMode.{inline,sideBySide,automatic}`. Compare entry points: `workbench.files.action.compareWithSaved` (Ctrl+K D; left `emetos-virtual:/saved/<path>` re-read on save and on disk changes, title "a.go (in file) ↔ a.go"), `compareWithClipboard` (Ctrl+K C), `compareFileWith` (quick open with `ShowOptions.OnFile`), the Explorer's `selectForCompare`, `compareFiles` and `compareSelected` (group `3_compare`, `resourceSelectedForCompare`, `listDoubleSelection`); `emetos --diff a b` opens the diff for the first instance and over the socket (`--wait` ends when the diff tab closes), the "not available yet" notice is gone; the "changed on disk" notice and the `FileModifiedSince` save failure get a first **Compare** button. `VirtualRegistry.RegisterPrefix` serves the virtual originals.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/diffeditor/{inline,model,widget,ruler}.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{linesview,zonegutter,zones,gutter}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{compare,app,diffpane,external,fileops,group,groupcommands,launch,process,save,virtualdocs}.go`, `internal/gui/quickinput/{controller,registry,files}.go`, `internal/core/{command,document,keybinding,settings}`, `testdata/proof/9.6/`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.1, §6.3, §7.1, §9.1, §9.11, §10.5, §10.11.

New goldens `diff_inline_{dark,light}`; `external_changes_{dark,light}` and `save_error_{dark,light}` regenerated for the Compare button (all viewed). Existing tests changed: `TestLaunchNotices`, `TestLauncherHandleErrorReplies`, `TestStartupDiffOfTheFirstInstance`, a subtest of `windows_test.go` (a diff now opens; merge keeps the notice), the Explorer context menu tests, the button lists of `backup_test.go`, `external_test.go`, `save_test.go`, the settings registry rows, the app-bound command lists, the diff editor rig (inline off by default at its 900 px width).

## 9.7 Merge editor layout

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9.7-merge-layout
- **Summary:** SPEC §3.1 "Merge editor", §9.11, §10.11. `gui/mergeeditor`: Incoming (left, read-only) and Current (right, read-only) over the Result (editable), each with a header (title and label; the result shows "N Conflicts Remaining"). The inputs are aligned with each other by diagonal-fill spacer zones (a port of `getAlignments`/`computeViewZones`); the result follows by scroll mapping (`_synchronizeScrolling`) from the model's regions, using the true top line. Changed ranges and word changes are marked in the inputs, conflicts are outlined in the result (unhandled/handled, focused/unfocused, new `editor.LineBlock`), checkboxes in the inputs' gutter reflect each conflict's state and toggle it (`Session.Toggle`, one undoable edit). Navigation `merge.goToNextUnhandledConflict`/`goToPreviousUnhandledConflict` (precondition `isMergeEditor`, wrapping, revealing in the three editors). `editorgroup.MergeInput` (`emetos.merge`, "Merging: <file>", two flavors: a file with conflict markers, whose text is replaced by the base-derived result as one undoable edit and left dirty, and `--merge` files, whose existing result text is kept with conflicts whose state differs from base marked handled), serialized and restored. Opening a file with complete conflict markers shows "This file has merge conflicts." with **Resolve in Merge Editor** (`git.openMergeEditor`; not for restored backups or reopened tabs); `emetos --merge path1 path2 base result` opens it for the first instance and over the socket (`--wait` ends when the tab closes, exit 1 until step 9.8's Complete Merge). Colors `merge.*` and `mergeEditor.*` with Dark, Light and HC defaults; context keys `isMergeEditor`, `isMergeResultEditor`, `mergeEditorResultUri`, `mergeEditorLayout`.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/mergeeditor/{align,session,scrollmap,navigate,checkbox,options,model,gutter,widget}.go`, `internal/core/editorgroup/mergeinput.go`, `internal/gui/editor/{blocks,overlay,viewmodel}.go`, `internal/core/diff/mergemodel.go`, `internal/core/theme/colors_builtin.go`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `internal/gui/app/{mergepane,app,group,groupcommands,launch,navigation,panes,state}.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.1, §9.11, §10.11.
- **Tests:** 38 test functions in `mergeeditor` (alignment, scroll mapping, navigation order, checkbox states, the session, the widget), 25 in `app/mergepane_test.go` (both ways of opening, the notice only for files that open, labels, counts, one undo restores the markers, dirty/save/close/serialize/restore, `--merge` for the first instance and over the socket, `--wait`, route tests for `MergeOpenInvalid`, `MergeOpenSideInvalid`, `MergeResultUnreadable`), 5 in `editorgroup/mergeinput_test.go` (`MergeInputInvalid`, `MergeInputIncomplete`, `MergeInputFilesIncomplete`, `MergeInputResultNotFile`), 2 for line blocks, color tests; goldens `merge_{dark,light}` and `merge_resolved_{dark,light}` (viewed). Existing tests changed: `TestLaunchNotices` (merge row removed), `TestLauncherHandleErrorReplies`, a subtest of `windows_test.go`, the app-bound command list of `commands_cover_test.go`.
- **Not ported:** the base view, layout toggles, deletion markers, word marks in the result, the checkbox context menu, a manual edit marking a conflict handled, the accept actions and Complete Merge (9.8).

## 9.8 Merge editor actions

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9.8-merge-actions
- **Summary:** SPEC §3.1, §9.11, §10.11, §15.2. Every conflict has a row of action links above it in all three editors (one-row view zones in `editorCodeLens.foreground`): in the inputs "Accept Incoming"/"Accept Current" and "Accept Combination (Incoming First)"/"(Current First)"; in the result the state ("No Changes Accepted", "Incoming", "Incoming + Current", "Current + Incoming", "Manual Resolution") with "Remove Incoming"/"Remove Current" and "Reset to base". The input headers get an accept-all button, the result header a Reset Result button and the primary **Complete Merge** button. `Session.Accept`, `SetState`, `AcceptAll`, `AcceptAllCombination`, `ResetResult` (and the checkboxes) each make one undoable edit; undo and redo give back the handled flags (kept per document version, as VS Code's `MarkAsHandledUndoRedoElement`); a manual edit does not mark a conflict handled (as `mergeEditorModel.ts`; the 9.7 SPEC text was corrected). Complete Merge (`mergeEditor.acceptMerge`, Ctrl+Enter when `isMergeEditor`) asks "Do you want to complete the merge of <file>?" ("The file contains unhandled conflicts.", Complete with Conflicts / Cancel) when conflicts remain, saves the result (a failed save keeps the editor open), then for a marker file opens the file in a text editor in the same group and closes the merge tab, and for `--merge` writes the `result` path (created if missing) and closes the tab; `--merge --wait` exits 0 after Complete Merge and 1 ("The merge was not completed.") when the tab or window closes otherwise. Commands `merge.acceptAllInput1`, `merge.acceptAllInput2`, `mergeEditor.resetResultToBaseAndAutoMerge`, `mergeEditor.acceptAllCombination`, `merge.toggleActiveConflictInput1/2`, `mergeEditor.acceptMerge`. Dev commands `dev.openMergeEditor {"path"}` (or the four-file form; relative to the folder, else the cwd) and `dev.acceptMerge {"conflict","side"}`. The merge editor's labels show file names, never paths.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/mergeeditor/{actions,actionrow,session,model,widget}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{devmerge,mergepane,launch,app,devcommands}.go`, `internal/core/{command,keybinding,theme}`, `internal/gui/kit/theme.go`, `testdata/proof/9.8/conflicted.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §3.1, §9.11, §10.11, §15.2.

## 9.9 Error routes audit

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9.9-error-routes
- **Summary:** CLAUDE.md "Error handling", the errors package's `AI_GUIDE.md`, SPEC §5.3, §13. `scripts/error-tags.sh` (a wrapper of `go run ./internal/archtest/cmd/errortags`, about 1 s) prints every Tag with its place, and with `--untested` the Tags no test asserts (a route in `HasRoute`, a `.Tag` comparison, a route-shaped or table string in a folder whose tests assert routes or tags, a sentinel given to `errors.Is`; comments and fake errors do not count); a non-literal Tag is an error. The 13 untested Tags got tests (`BackupEmpty`, `BackupEncodeFailed`, `CodiconFormatFailed`, `DevOpenFileFailed`, `EditorClipboardUnavailable`, `EditorClipboardFailed`, `JSONCNoValue`, `JSONCDecodeFailed`, `KeybindingsTableInvalid` (later removed, below), `QuickInputCommandMissing` (later removed), `WindowCreateFailed`, `WindowMissing`, `ErrorTagsExceptionsReadFailed`), with small seams where a failure could not happen (`BackupStoreOptions.Marshal`, the generator's formatter, `keybinding.NewModelOfRows`); `docs/ERROR-TAGS-UNTESTED.md` is not needed. R4: the error-building helpers were inlined into the functions that detect the failures (`core/diff` `errInvalid`/`errCancelled`, `core/contextkey` `errInvalidRegex`, `core/document` `tagged`, `core/emmet` `fail`/`preferenceInvalid`/`invalid`/`cssEmptyValueError`, `core/regex` `(*parser).fail` at 25 sites, `core/ipc` `listen`'s `fail`, `core/storage` `Profiles.build`, `core/workspace` `cancelled`, `core/editorgroup` `errUnknownGroup` at 20 sites, `core/fileio` `openError`/`writeError`, `gui/app` `errNoWindow`); R5: the test sentinel `errFailed` is a struct literal. R3: errors from this project are no longer re-wrapped (keybindings editing, snippet files, TextMate themes, profile switching, settings and keybindings writes, the IPC client, setting registration, the command handler bindings, virtual providers); the Tags `EditorGroupCommandMissing`, `KeybindingsEditFailed`, `KeybindingsTableInvalid`, `LayoutCommandMissing`, `NotificationCommandMissing`, `ProfileSwitchFailed`, `QuickInputCommandMissing`, `SnippetFileReadFailed` are gone (their failures keep the original Tag and route). Archtest rules `PackageLevelError`, `ErrorHelper`, `NonLiteralTag`, `ProjectErrorRewrapped` (calls into other packages of the module; methods and same-package calls need type information and were reviewed by hand) with testdata, and `TestEveryErrorTagHasARouteTest`, so `go test ./...` fails on a Tag without a test. CLAUDE.md gained the R4 and R5 bullets, the line to read `AI_GUIDE.md`, and `scripts/error-tags.sh --untested` in the Definition of done.
- **Main files:** `internal/archtest/{errortags,errorrules,archtest}.go`, `internal/archtest/cmd/errortags/`, `scripts/error-tags.sh`, the packages listed above, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/SPEC.md` §5.3, §13.
- **Tests:** the new route tests above, the archtest fixtures `package_level_error`, `error_helper`, `non_literal_tag`, `project_error_rewrapped`, `error_tags` and a `clean` guard, the command's tests, `TestErrorTagsScript`, `TestTheFolderOfTheKeybindingsFileCannotBeMade`; route assertions updated wherever a helper's name or a removed wrapper Tag was in a route. No golden changed.
- **Not ported:** a type-aware `ProjectErrorRewrapped` (go/types was too slow and fragile for a test).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · error-tags --untested ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the "tested" rule accepts table strings in folders that assert routes or tags (rewriting hundreds of table tests was out of scope); every Tag fixed in this step has a literal `HasRoute` test.

## 9b.1 Workspaces in one window

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9b.1-workspaces
- **Summary:** SPEC D17, §10.14. `app.Window` is now `app.Workspace` and `app.Windows` is `app.Workspaces` (`NewWorkspace`, `WorkspaceSpec`, `WorkspaceScope`/`WorkspaceOf`/`PeekWorkspace`, `CloseReasonTab`, the tag `WorkspaceMissing`). `Workspaces` holds `WindowTab`s (`ID`, `Kind`, `Workspace`; `TabKind` is `TabWorkspace` until step 11.6) and hosts them in the one implicit window: an `IndexedStack` of slots keyed by each tab's `GlobalKey`, each a `TickerMode` (on for the active tab only) around the unchanged per-workspace page; only the shown tab paints, hit-tests and takes focus; leaving a tab remembers its focus and coming back restores it; only the active tab's key dispatcher uses keys; the window title is the active tab's. Inactive tabs keep their watchers, auto save, backups and file index running. `Open`, `Activate`, `ActivateIndex`, `Move`, `Close`, `NewTab`, `RequestQuit`; a new tab opens after the active one; after a close the most recently active tab becomes active; closing the last tab leaves a No Folder tab; the close button and the app's exit request run the quit flow over every tab. `workbench.action.newWindow` is "New Workspace Tab" and `workbench.action.closeWindow` "Close Workspace Tab". State: `workspaceTabs` (`{id, kind, stateId, folder, profile}`), `activeWorkspaceTab` and `windowGeometry` in `state.json`; an old `openWindows` list migrates to tabs in order (each tab keeps the old window id, so its `windows/<id>.json` and backups are found); `window.restoreWindows` reads as restore tabs. Profiles are per tab. Dev commands `dev.openFolderInNewTab {"path"}` (replacing `dev.openFolderInNewWindow`) and `dev.activateWindowTab {"index"}`. Removed: the window host, the window manager root, `CreateRegularWindowController`, the exit-request hook, the retired implicit window, window focus following, `newWindowSize`, the tags `WindowsUnavailable` and `WindowCreateFailed`.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/app/{tabshost,workspaces,workspacemodel,workspaces_commands,app,keys,startup,state,closing,autosave,devcommands,profile,process,launch}.go`, `internal/core/storage/{statefiles,statestore,windowid,profiles}.go`, `internal/core/command/builtin.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §5, §10.10–§10.14, §15.2, §20, `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`.
- **Tests:** 14 in `workspacetabs_test.go` (activate and move, switching keeps editors, scroll and focus, only the active tab ticks and paints, keys go to the active tab, the window title, inactive tabs' watcher, auto save and backups, leaving a tab is a window change for auto save, 10 tabs opened and closed without leaking goroutines, the dev commands with route tests, `WorkspaceNotInATab`, two tabs with two profiles, window geometry, an old `state.json` restored as tabs), 4 in `workspaces_test.go` (no Glutter window is made, the last tab leaves a No Folder tab, closing an inactive tab brings it to the front for the dialog, the close button quits over every tab), the storage migration test; the window tests of steps 7.5–8.6b rewritten for tabs (list in the agent's report: placement, restore, close, quit, hot exit, settings per tab, commands and notifications per tab, the switcher, profiles, theme pickers); removed as meaningless with one window: `TestAWindowThePlatformCannotMakeIsNotKept`, `TestNewWindowDimensions`, `TestWithoutTheWindowManagerTheWindowsCannotBeOpened`, `TestTheFocusMakesAWindowTheActiveOne`, `TestAutoSaveOnWindowChangeSavesTheWindowThatLostTheFocus`, `TestTheFirstWindowCanCloseWhileOthersStay`. Every golden is unchanged.
- **Not ported:** the strip (9b.2), the full placement rules (9b.3, `TODO(step 9b.3)`), close to tray (9b.4), the switch picker (9b.5).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · error-tags --untested ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** none (a smoke run in the headless compositor showed one workspace as before, and a second tab opened with `dev.openFolderInNewTab`)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** the entry about placing windows was removed; the close-request entry was rewritten (the close button arrives as an app exit request; 9b.4 must tell it apart).
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the File menu rows keep the labels "New Window" and "Close Window" until 9b.2 reworks the menu (changing them now would change 12 goldens); `window.newWindowDimensions` stays registered and does nothing; full screen belongs to the one window.

## 9b.1b Glutter 1.7 and the end of the workarounds

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9b.1b-glutter-1.7
- **Summary:** Emetos is on Glutter v1.7.0 (build, tests and every golden unchanged before any edit). Replaced with the 1.7 APIs: `kit.Hover` on `widgets.NewRawTooltip` (`HoverDelayFunc`, `OnShown`, `OnHidden`, `Controller`; Emetos's own key handler stays because `DismissOnKey` neither ignores a lone modifier nor cancels a hover still waiting for its delay; a press anywhere now hides the hover), the sash on `MouseRegionOpts.HoldCursorWhilePressed` (the whole-view cursor region is gone), the window controller through `widgets.WindowController`/`MaximizableWindowController`/`WindowControllerMaybeOf` (no interface assertions; tests read the title with `Tester.ViewTitle`), `testutil.New` relying on `gluttertest.New`'s unmount, the no-file-chooser tests on `FakeDesktop.DisableFileChooser()`, the panel actions and the Problems filter box leaving the tree again (27.1). Kept, with reasons: `kit.Tree` rebuilds its nodes (1.7 cannot set `expanded` on an existing node, and the model owns expansion; `NodeKey` broke four tree tests); the list and tab drags keep their own controllers (one controller per list works while rows scroll away, targets come from row geometry, scrolling and auto-expand use the binding's timers; the Glutter gaps are gone, so their comments are now design notes); `ProcessOptions.WatchFiles` stays off in tests (the leak is gone, but a live watcher races with tests that stage on-disk changes); the `SaveDialog` field stays for a dialog that fails another way. `scripts/perf.sh` reports the split first draw (renderers tried, GPU context, shaders built and from cache, frame) for three cache states and measures idle without focus headless (a second window takes the focus): 0 frames. `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md` keeps two entries, rewritten: `TreeSliver` expansion (`SetExpanded`/`IsExpanded` asked) and the cold start (a failed Vulkan try costs 560–605 ms headless; Glutter's shader cache saves about 13 ms over Mesa's). The close button's `TODO(step 9b.4)` points at `SetImplicitWindowDelegate`.
- **Main files:** `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `internal/gui/kit/{hover,sash,splitview,tree,listdnd,tabs}.go`, `internal/gui/testutil/testutil.go`, `internal/gui/app/{app,workspaces,workspacemodel,zen,state,save}.go`, `internal/gui/workbench/workbench.go`, `internal/gui/views/problems/view.go`, `scripts/perf.sh`, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`, `docs/SPEC.md` §10.2, §10.9, §20.

## 9b.2 The title bar and the workspace tab strip

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9b.2-title-bar
- **Summary:** SPEC §10.1, §10.9, §10.14. Emetos draws its own title bar (`Decorations: DecorationsApp`): each tab's workbench wraps its body in `widgets.WindowDecoration` (35 px, `titleBar.*` colors, the inactive colors when the window has no focus) with the menu in `Leading`, the strip in `Title` and the window buttons from `WindowInfo.Capabilities`; the separate menu bar row is gone. `window.menuBarVisibility` gains `compact`, the new default: a `≡` button with the menus as submenus (`kit.MenuBarCompact`); `classic`/`visible` show the titles, `toggle` after Alt, `hidden` none; Alt and `workbench.actions.menubar.focus` work in every mode. The strip reuses `kit.Tabs` (`TabsOpts.TitleBar`): Codicon `root-folder`/`window`, disambiguated names (`api — work`), the dirty dot (the close button on hover), a tooltip with the path and the profile, 120–240 px wide, shrinking then scrolling with the active tab in view, middle click closes; `+` opens a menu with **Workspace...** (the portal folder chooser → a new tab or the folder's tab); the context menu has Close, Close Others, Close to the Right, Copy Path, Reveal in File Manager and Switch Profile... (`emetos.workspaceTab.close`, `.closeOthers`, `.closeToTheRight`, `.copyPath`, `.revealInFileManager`, `.openFolder`). Dragging the empty part of the row moves the window, a double click maximizes, a right click shows the window menu. Full screen has no row (the menu gets its own row, after Alt for `classic`/`compact`); zen hides the menu and the tabs but keeps the window buttons unless zen is full screen. The File menu rows are "New Workspace Tab" and "Close Workspace Tab". Colors `titleBar.inactiveBackground`/`inactiveForeground`; context keys `workspaceTabCount`, `workspaceTabIsLast`, `activeWorkspaceTabHasFolder` (for the tab whose menu is built).
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/workbench/{titlebar,titlemenu,tabnames,workbench,commands,layout}.go`, `internal/gui/kit/{tabfit,tabs,menubar,theme}.go`, `internal/gui/app/{titlebar,app,group,state}.go`, `internal/core/{settings,theme,command}`, `internal/gui/testutil/testutil.go`, `testdata/proof/9b.2/`, `docs/SPEC.md` §10.1, §10.9, §10.14, §11.2, §15.2.
- **Tests:** 13 in `workbench/titlebar_test.go`, a golden test with 18 cases, 2 for names, 2 for the menu modes, 1 + 6 in `kit` (widths, the title bar tabs), 3 compact menu tests, 17 in `app/titlebar_test.go` (the dirty dot across tabs, close and middle click, the context menu commands, the `+` flow with `FakeDesktop.AnswerFileChooser`, window actions, every `menuBarVisibility` value), `TestWorkspaceTabMenus`. Goldens: 137 regenerated for the row and 24 new (`titlebar_*`, `compact_menu`, `compact_submenu`, `workspace_tabs_{dark,light}`); the agent viewed 15 across editor, explorer, settings, panels, zen, zoom, menus, dialogs, pickers and the grid. Existing tests changed: offsets 30 → 35 px, the menu visibility tests rewritten, the `gui/app` fixtures set `window.menuBarVisibility: classic` where they tap menu titles, editor-tab finders scoped to the editor area, the File menu labels.
- **Not ported:** counter-zoom of the row (zoom scales it), Alt+letter opening a submenu directly in compact, F10 (VS Code binds it on the web only).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · error-tags --untested ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.
- **Proof:** `docs/implemented-proof-images/9b.2-workspace-tabs.png`, made with `scripts/proof.sh docs/implemented-proof-images/9b.2-workspace-tabs.png --commands '[["dev.openFolderInNewTab",{"path":"testdata/proof/9b.2/notes"}],["dev.openFile",{"path":"testdata/proof/9b.2/notes/main.go"}]]' -- testdata/proof/9b.2/api`: the `≡` menu, the workspace tabs `api` and `notes` (active, with its close button), `+`, the maximize and close buttons, and `main.go` open

  ![9b.2 workspace tabs](implemented-proof-images/9b.2-workspace-tabs.png)
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** a new entry: `WindowDecoration` puts the whole bar under one drag, double-tap and secondary-tap detector, so the strip shields its tabs with no-op detectors (`// Glutter:` in `workbench/titlebar.go`); Glutter could let an app name the draggable part of the bar.
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** the row lives inside each tab's workbench (the menus, hovers and context menus need the tab's overlay, theme and focus scopes), so dialogs sit under the row and zoom scales it.

## 9b.3 Launches open tabs

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9b.3-launch-tabs
- **Summary:** SPEC §10.11, §10.12, §10.14, §15.1. Every launch (a hand-over over the socket; the first instance's own command line is still placed by the restore plan) and every Open Folder opens or focuses a tab: a folder that a tab has (through links) becomes active; otherwise `-n` (and Ctrl in Open Recent) opens a new tab after the active one and `-r` replaces the active tab with the unsaved-changes questions (Cancel keeps it; the tab keeps its place and id); otherwise `window.openFoldersInNewWindow` decides (`on` a new tab, `off` the active tab, `default` a new tab, replacing an untouched No Folder tab: no folder, no document, no editor). Open Folder, Open Recent, the empty Explorer's button and Close Folder follow the same rule (7.8's "picked inside the app replaces" exception and `FolderSource` are gone); Close Folder makes the active tab a No Folder tab. Files, `--goto`, `--diff` and `--merge` open in the active tab (`window.openFilesInNewWindow` `on` opens a new No Folder tab for files; `off` and `default` use the active tab); `--profile` keeps the 8.6 rules per tab (a folder not open goes to a new tab when the active tab lacks the profile; an open folder with another profile is focused with its notice) and now covers `--diff`/`--merge`. Every launch first shows and raises the window (`Workspaces.showWindow`: `SetHidden(false)` and `Activate` on Glutter 1.7's implicit controller), so step 9b.4 only adds the hiding. `emetos` with no argument shows the window. `scripts/proof.sh --launch '<args>'` (repeatable) runs further launches against the same isolated runtime folder (`scripts/run-with-launches.sh`). The two settings' descriptions say "tab".
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/app/{workspaces,explorer,titlebar}.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `scripts/{proof,run-with-launches}.sh`, `testdata/proof/9b.3/`, `docs/SPEC.md` §10.11, §10.12, §10.14, §15.1, `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md` (proof method B).
- **Tests:** 19 top-level tests (57 with subtests) in `launchplace_test.go` over a real socket in a private runtime folder: two folders give one window with two tabs and no Glutter window, the same folder (also through a symlink, with `-n`/`-r`) focuses its tab, `-n` after the active one, `-r` with Cancel and Don't Save, the untouched No Folder tab, every `window.openFoldersInNewWindow` value, files and `--goto` in the active tab, `window.openFilesInNewWindow`, a folder with files, `--diff`/`--merge`, `--wait` for files, tabs, diff and merge (exit 0 and 1), the `--profile` rules, show and raise of a hidden window for every launch kind, Open Folder through `FakeDesktop().AnswerFileChooser`, Open Recent with and without Ctrl, Close Folder, an empty launch, a refused folder. Rewritten: `TestPlaceFolder`, `TestPlaceFiles`, `TestOpenFolderFollowsTheSettingForEverySource`, two subtests of `TestLaunchesOpenInTheRightWindow`, three recent-list tests in `state_test.go`, the folder-replacing tests in `explorer_test.go` and `closing_test.go`.
- **Not ported:** VS Code's "new window" meaning of `window.openFilesInNewWindow` `default`.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · error-tags --untested ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.

## 9b.4 The system tray and close to tray

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9b.4-tray
- **Summary:** SPEC §10.14, §17, §5.2, §20. The application icon: a rounded indigo square with a white `</>` mark drawn with Glutter's canvas (`kit.AppIconPainter`, paths only), rendered by a test with the software renderer at 16, 22, 24, 32, 48, 64, 128, 256 and 512 px into `assets/icons/` (a small Go package that embeds them, `icons.PNG(size)`); the window icon is set at start. The tray (`desktop.NewSystemTray`, id `dev.emetos.Emetos`, title "Emetos", tooltip "N workspaces", the pictures drawn at the five tray sizes) wraps the tabs; its menu lists every tab as a radio item (the strip label), then Open Folder..., New Workspace Tab, Hide/Show Emetos and Quit, rebuilt when tabs open, close, move or change folder; a click shows and raises a hidden or background window and hides a window in front. `window.closeToTray` (application scope, default `true`): the close button (through Glutter 1.7's `SetImplicitWindowDelegate`) writes the hot-exit backups, rebuilds the tray menu with "Show Emetos" and hides the window after that frame (no frames while hidden; the workspaces keep running); with the setting off, or no tray (`TrayUnavailable` and the other tray errors are caught by `Workspaces.errorReporter`, logged once, no notification; a hidden window is shown again), it quits as before. Quit (tray, Ctrl+Q, File › Exit) is the quit flow; with unsaved changes and hot exit off the window is shown with the dirty tab first.
- **Main files:** `internal/gui/kit/appicon.go`, `assets/icons/`, `internal/gui/app/{tray,appicon,workspaces,app,state}.go`, `internal/core/settings/builtin.go`, `docs/SPEC.md` §5.2, §10.14, §17, §20.
- **Tests:** 23 in `tray_test.go` with `FakeDesktop` (registration, tooltip, menu labels in order and after tab changes, same-name tabs, click hide/show/raise, Hide/Show item, choosing a tab, Open Folder..., New Workspace Tab, Quit with hot exit while hidden and with a question while hidden, the close button hiding with backups on disk first, no frames while hidden, show cancelling a pending hide, the close button quitting with the setting off, turned off at run time, and with no tray, a real `TrayUnavailable` from a missing session bus, a tray error after hiding, the close button ignored during quit questions, the reporter passing other errors through); 5 in `app/appicon_test.go` (tray and window icons, every size, `TrayIconFromRGBA` with the 512 px picture, route tests of `AppIconMissing` and `AppIconInvalid`); `TestAppIconPNGs` (9 sizes against the files: transparent corners, opaque body, white mark); `assets/icons` test; `TestCloseToTrayIsRegistered`. Existing tests: `runWith` goes through the new `runRoot`; comments only otherwise; no assertion changed.
- **Not ported:** VS Code has no tray.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ · golangci-lint ✅ · error-tags --untested ✅ · go test -race ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ · display tests none.

## 9b.5 Switching and arranging tabs

- **Date:** 2026-10-25
- **Branch:** feat/step-9b.5-switching
- **Summary:** SPEC §10.14, §10.6. `emetos.workspaceTab.next`/`previous` (Ctrl+Alt+PageDown/PageUp, strip order, wrapping), `open1`...`open9` (Ctrl+Alt+1...9; 9 is the last tab; keys only), `switch` (a quick pick: the active tab first as `current`, the others in strip order with the first selected; icon, name, unsaved mark, folder with `~` and shortened from the left, non-default profile; filter on name and description; `workbench.action.switchWindow` runs it too), `moveLeft`/`moveRight` (no default key, stop at the ends); Close Others and Close to the Right (from 9b.2) act on the active tab without an argument. Drag to reorder through `kit.Tabs`'s drag (4 px slop, Escape cancels, a drop line only where the tab would move, the strip scrolls at its ends, a single tab does not drag); a press on another tab activates it on release so a drag never swaps the strip. Context keys `workspaceTabCount`, `activeWorkspaceTabHasFolder`, `workspaceTabIsFirst`, `workspaceTabIsLast`, set in every workspace on each change; View › Workspace Tabs submenu (Next, Previous, Switch..., Move Left/Right, Close Others, Close to the Right, dimmed by those keys). Fixed: a key that changes the active tab was dispatched again by the next tab's dispatcher (the early key handlers all get the same event), so the owner of an event is now fixed at its first dispatcher (`Workspaces.keyEvent`/`keyOwner`). Removed two failed-comparison images committed by mistake in step 3.8 (`scrollbars.actual.png`, `scrollbars.diff.png`) and ignored `*.actual.png`/`*.diff.png`.
