## Fix: Glutter 1.9.4

- **Date:** 2026-10-28
- **Branch:** fix/glutter-1.9.3 (it began with 1.9.3 and ended with 1.9.4)
- **Summary:** Glutter 1.9.3 fixed both entries of `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md` that Emetos had sent: the early key handlers of the focus manager now run also when no node has the focus (its step 29.23), and `UIThread.IsCurrent` compares a goroutine token in about 2 ns instead of parsing `runtime.Stack` (29.24). 1.9.4 (29.26) makes the token one that is never reused.
- **Changes:**
  - `kit.TypeAhead` takes the keys with one early key handler; the hardware handler and the claim of the key it took are gone, and so is the `// Glutter:` comment.
  - The two entries are removed from `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`; the two of step 17d.2 stay (still present in 1.9.4).
  - **A race in Emetos's test rig, not a Glutter bug.** With 1.9.3, `TestTheInputBoxesOfAViewNeverTouchTheClipboardOfTheHost` (its `shift+insert` cases) failed in 19 runs of 20 without the race detector. The manager first blamed the token; the agent showed it was the rig: `offscreen.View` paces its frames by the real clock while the test pumps a fake one, so a key came before the view laid out the pasted text, and 1.9.2's slow check had spent enough real time to hide it. `rigFrameRate` is now 1,000,000 (the pacing never holds a frame back); the test fails 20 of 20 on 1.9.4 without it and passes 40 of 40 with it.
  - The debug build is faster (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md` "After Glutter 1.9.3"): the UI thread of the frame that opens the 700 KB preview 8.3 to 9.1 ms (before 42 to 55 ms), the first frame of the window 10 to 12.5 ms (before 100 to 114 ms). `previewFirstFrameBudget` is 20 ms (was 100 ms).
- **Checks** (the manager's run on top of 17e.3): gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ (also `emetos_display` and `emetos_gopls`) · golangci-lint ✅ (also `--build-tags emetos_display`) · error-tags --untested ✅ · `go mod tidy -diff` clean · go test -race ✅ (98 packages, exit 0) · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ (98 packages, exit 0) · `scripts/realinput.sh` ✅ (the whole suite, exit 0).
- **Glutter issues:** two removed; none new (a wish, not a bug: `offscreen.ViewOpts` could take a clock, so that tests are not paced by the real one).

## Step 17e.2: Search across files

- **Date:** 2026-10-28
- **Branch:** feat/step-17e.2-search
- **Summary:** The Search view of VS Code as the compiled-in extension `emetos.search` (`extensions/search`): the search and replace boxes with Match Case, Whole Word, Regular Expression and Preserve Case, the "files to include" and "files to exclude" boxes, results as a list or a tree, F4 and Shift+F4 through the matches, Replace All with a confirmation, a replace preview as a diff, and Find in Folder... in the Explorer. It runs on a new engine (`internal/core/search`) that finds the first result in under a millisecond in a tree of 20,000 files (500 MB) and the whole search in about 50 ms, against 310 ms for GNU `grep -rF`, and on a new API, `Workspace().FindTextInFiles` (with `NewTextReplacer` and `ReplaceInFiles`).
- **Main files:** `internal/core/search/` (engine, replace, `ParsePatterns`, the walker); `extension/search.go` and `extension/builtin.go` (the API); `internal/gui/exthost/search.go`; `extensions/search/`; `extensions/internal/fakeapi/search.go`; `internal/core/fileio/read.go` (`Decode` exported); `internal/core/command` (Find in Folder... in the Explorer's menu); `internal/gui/app/fileops.go` and `extensions.go`; README (Search), SPEC 10.27 "As built" and the rows of section 14.
- **Tests:** the engine's table-driven tests (globs, excludes, `.gitignore`, binary files, encodings and byte order marks, symbolic link loops, unsaved text, the limit, cancelling with no goroutine left, replace with preserve case and groups, route tests of every tag), the speed test of SPEC section 14 and the benchmark (with GNU grep for comparison); the host's tests (`internal/gui/exthost/search_test.go`); the view's tests with the fake API, 10 goldens (list, tree, replace box, no results, empty; dark and light), the test of the UI thread's longest turn (10.7 ms with 20,000 matches); and the real-input tests `TestRealInputSearch` (Ctrl+Shift+F and typing, F4, Shift+F4, Alt+C, Ctrl+Shift+H, Replace All and its confirmation, and Ctrl+Z in the open file) and `TestRealInputFindInFolder` (right click in the Explorer).
- **Speed** (alone, warm cache, SPEC section 14; `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` "Search (step 17e.2)"): first result 0.6 to 0.8 ms (budget 100 ms); the whole search 50 to 57 ms against 310 to 344 ms for `grep -rF` and `-rFi`.
- **Decisions:** 150 to 159 of `docs/DECISIONS-TO-REVIEW.md`.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ (also `emetos_display` and `emetos_display emetos_gopls`) · golangci-lint ✅ (also `--build-tags emetos_display`) · error-tags --untested ✅ · `go mod tidy -diff` clean · go test -race ✅ (all packages; `internal/gui/app` and `internal/gui/editor` rerun after the fixes of the Explorer menu tests and goldens) · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ (the speed-sensitive tests of `extensions/search` and the terminal emulator failed once while other agents' builds ran, and passed alone) · real-input suite (`scripts/realinput.sh`, no race, alone) all passed, except `TestRealInputBigPreviewStartDoesNotWaitForThePreview` once under load; alone it passed.
- **Proof:**
  ![17e.2 search](implemented-proof-images/17e.2-search.png)
  The Search view in the real window after Ctrl+Shift+H with real keys: "needle" in the search box with Match Case on, "pin" in the replace box, "4 results in 3 files", and each row showing the text taken away in red and the text put in in green, with the hover actions (replace, dismiss) on the last row.
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none new.
- **Deviations and follow-ups:** what decision 156 lists is not built (Open to the Side, the right-click menu of the results, file icons in the rows, and so on).
- **The rebase** (this step was made beside 17d.2, 17e.1 and 17e.3 and was rebased onto them, with the APIs that two steps built merged into one):
  - `Contributes.ContextKeys`: 17d.2's design is kept (`ExtensionContextKeyDeclInvalid` for a name that is not a word, `ExtensionContextKeyDeclDuplicate` for a name listed twice, `instance.ownsContextKey` in the host). The test of 17e.2 for a key declared twice now asserts the Duplicate route; 17e.2's own check, field and fake were the same in every other way.

The seed is right also when the view is made after the editor. `Documents` of the window has `Active` (17e.3) next to `Unsaved` and `OpenFiles` (17e.2).

No display test that remains clicks the activity bar by a fixed position (checked by grep over `cmd/emetos`).

## Fix: the preview's start test measured the driver's noise

- **Date:** 2026-10-28
- **Branch:** fix/preview-start-test-noise
- **Summary:** `TestRealInputBigPreviewStartDoesNotWaitForThePreview` compared one cold start with a small preview and one with the big one against 150 ms; each first frame was 4 to 5 s of llvmpipe's shader compile with an empty Mesa cache, about ±500 ms of noise, so it failed under load (by 241, 410, 471 and 672 ms in the runs of 17c.1 to 17d.3). The starts now share one warmed `MESA_SHADER_CACHE_DIR` and the fastest of three alternating starts of each kind are compared: +2 to +40 ms in five runs, and a temporary 300 ms sleep in the preview's open made it fail as it should. `docs/DESKTOP-PASS.md` D13 has an update.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · `go vet -tags emetos_display ./cmd/...` ✅ · golangci-lint `--build-tags emetos_display` ✅ · the agent's whole real-input suite ✅ (exit 0) · my run after the rebase onto 17e.2: the three `BigPreview` tests, `-count=3`, all pass (the 20 ms budget of the Glutter 1.9.4 fix included). The change is to one display test; the race and cgo-free runs do not build it.

## Step 17d.3: Find, links and the other commands

- **Date:** 2026-10-28
- **Branch:** feat/step-17d.3-find-links
- **Summary:** The terminal has find over the scrollback, links under the pointer, and the commands that put text in it, as VS Code's (SPEC §10.25 "As built (step 17d.3 ...)"). Nothing of xterm.js was used, read or ported (not its search addon, not its link code, not `node_modules`); the link parser and detectors, the find keys and the command behavior are ports of VS Code's own `terminalContrib/find`, `terminalContrib/links`, `linkComputer.ts` and `terminalEnvironment.ts`, each file naming its source.
- **Built:**
  - Find: the widget (VS Code's simple find widget, built from `extension/ui`; Match Case, Whole Word and Regex switches, `3 of 5` / `No results` / `20000+`, Previous, Next, Close, a red border when nothing matches or the pattern does not compile), the search over the emulator's buffer with soft-wrapped lines joined (blocks of 512 rows, cancellable, 20,000 matches at most), the highlights drawn under the text, `focusFind`, `hideFind`, `findNext`, `findPrevious`, `toggleFindRegex`, `toggleFindWholeWord`, `toggleFindCaseSensitive` with VS Code's keys, the context keys `terminalFindVisible`, `terminalFindFocused`, `terminalFindInputFocused`, and a search made again while output arrives.
  - Links: web addresses, file links `path:line:col` (every suffix format of VS Code's parser, the git-diff prefixes, Python and C++ fallbacks) resolved against the OSC 7 folder or the start folder and only under the workspace folder, OSC 8 links with `terminal.integrated.allowedLinkSchemes` and an "Allow {scheme}" notification; the underline and the `Follow link (ctrl + click)` tooltip (Alt when `editor.multiCursorModifier` is `ctrlCmd`); Ctrl+click opens with `Documents().Show` or `Workspace().OpenURI`.
  - Commands: `runSelectedText`, `runActiveFile`, `sendSequence` (with the variables), `rename`, `renameWithArgs`, `selectDefaultShell`, `focusAtIndex1` to `9` and (at the rebase) `searchWorkspace`.
  - Extension API: `Settings().Update`, `ui.NewToggle` (and the fake and host implementations); test tools `Client.ClickHolding`, `App.CtrlClick`.
- **Not built:** the word links, the multi-line detectors (ripgrep, git hunks), the detector by cell attributes, links to folders, the find overview ruler and border colors, lookahead and backreferences in find patterns (Go's RE2), per-line folders for links (step 17d.5).
- **Tests:** the link parser against VS Code's table (every suffix format, three links in a line), the web and file detectors (the link computer's cases, and the local detector's 15 links x 35 formats x 4 wrappings, on cells the emulator wrote; the Windows, WSL and file-service cases are skipped), the search (wrapped lines, the scrollback in blocks, the limit, cancel, a pattern that does not compile, wide characters), the pane (widget, highlights, underline, tooltip, Ctrl+click and Alt+click), every command over the fake API, `Settings().Update` (host and fake), `ClickHolding`; 2,774 passing subtests in `extensions/terminal` in all. Goldens: `pane_find_{dark,light}.png`, `pane_find_none_{dark,light}.png`, `pane_link_{dark,light}.png` (all looked at). Real-input tests (`cmd/emetos/terminal_find_display_test.go`): Ctrl+F finds a word, Enter, Shift+Enter, F3, Shift+F3, Alt+C, Alt+R and Escape work from the input; Ctrl+click on `main.go:3:1` printed by the shell opens `main.go` at line 3 (a click without Ctrl does not); the hover on an address; Run Selected Text from an editor (the editor keeps the keyboard); Run Active File, Send Sequence and Rename from the palette.
- **Decisions:** 170 to 179 of `docs/DECISIONS-TO-REVIEW.md` (174 also holds `searchWorkspace` and the privilege).
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ (also `emetos_display`, and `emetos_display emetos_gopls`) · golangci-lint ✅ (also `--build-tags emetos_display`) · error-tags --untested ✅ · `go mod tidy -diff` clean · go test -race ✅ (my run, exit 0) · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ (the run under load had `TestThroughputBudgets` of the emulator and `TestHostKeyPinning` of `sshlogin` fail, neither touched by this step; both pass when run alone) · `scripts/realinput.sh` whole suite ✅ (exit 0, with `-race`; the first run had `TestRealInputBigPreviewStartDoesNotWaitForThePreview`, a timing test of the preview, fail by 672 ms under load, and it passes alone and in the second run).
- **Proof:** `17d.3-find.png` and `17d.3-link.png` of the real-input tests (the widget with 3 of 4 matches highlighted; the hovered `main.go:3:1` underlined with its tooltip).
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** none new.
- **The rebase** (this step was made beside 17e.1, 17e.2 and 17e.3 and was rebased onto them, with the APIs that two steps built merged into one):
  - Conflicts: `docs/DECISIONS-TO-REVIEW.md` and `docs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (both sides kept, this step's last) and the struct of `extensions/internal/fakeapi/fakeapi.go` (`updates` of `Settings().Update` next to the `active`, `clipboard` and `locationAnswers` fields of 17e.3).
  - `ui.Toggle`, `ToggleOpts` and `NewToggle`: 17e.2 and this step each exported them from `extension/ui` over `kit.Toggle` with the same signature, so git merged both and the package did not compile. 17e.2's one set is kept (its comment now names the terminal too); this step's copy is deleted. `TestTheWidgetsAreTheKitsAndUseTheTheme` keeps its toggle (it taps one) and the terminal's code did not change.
  - `Settings().Update`: neither 17e.2 nor 17e.3 writes a setting, so there is only this one.
  - The editor in front: the terminal still follows the core events, and asks `Documents().Active()` (17e.3) when no event has told of an editor, so `runSelectedText`, `runActiveFile` and the `${file}` variables are right when the command is what activated the extension (`TestTheCommandsFindTheEditorInFrontWithoutAnEvent`).
  - The clipboard: the terminal copies and pastes with the window's clipboard service (`binding.Clipboard()`, text and the primary selection) from the pane, which `Clipboard().WriteText` (write only, no primary selection) cannot replace; nothing was built twice, so nothing changed.
  - `searchWorkspace` (Ctrl+Shift+F with text selected in the terminal) is built: it runs `workbench.action.findInFiles` with `{query, triggerSearch: true}` through `emetos.commands.execute`. That needs the privilege `commands.execute` (a request to a topic of the Search extension would not activate it), so the Terminal declares it and the host's allowlist became a map from extension to the privileges it may declare (`exthost.privilegedExtensions`: Relay both, the Terminal `commands.execute` alone; `extension.TerminalID`; SPEC D21 and §10.19 "Privileges"). Tests: `TestSearchWorkspaceRunsFindInFilesWithTheSelection`, `TestSearchWorkspaceSaysWhenTheSearchIsNotThere`, `TestTheManifestDeclaresWhatItNeeds` (the privilege and the key), `TestTheTerminalMayDeclareOnlyCommandsExecute`, `TestTheRealRegistrationOfRelayIsGrantedItsPrivilegesAndNothingIsLeftOut` (now lists both extensions), `TestRealInputTerminalSearchWorkspaceOpensTheSearchViewWithTheSelection` (a double click on a word printed by the shell, Ctrl+Shift+F, the Search view shows the file that has the word). No new error Tag.
  - The rename test of this step (`TestRenameAsksForTheNameAndRenameWithArgsTakesIt`) compared the box's value with the tab title read after the command, and the title follows the foreground process, which the pty reports on its own goroutine; under `-race` it failed every time on the rebased base. It now accepts the title just before or just after the command.
  - Checks after the rebase onto `main` (`74a34ef`): gofmt, go vet (plain, `emetos_display`, `emetos_display emetos_gopls`), golangci-lint (plain and `--build-tags emetos_display ./cmd/... ./internal/testenv/...`), error-tags --untested, `go mod tidy -diff`, go test -race (exit 0), CGO_ENABLED=0 build and test, and `scripts/realinput.sh --alone` (exit 0). The CGO_ENABLED=0 run had `TestTheUIThreadStaysUnderAFrameWhileAHugeSearchStreams` fail (19.5 ms against 16 ms) while other agents' builds ran, and it passes alone (11.4 ms, three runs); a first race run had `TestGoldenExtensionsPageProblem` fail because the refusal message had changed, which is why the message for an extension that is not on the allowlist is the old one again.

## Step 17d.4: The terminal's events and Relay

- **Date:** 2026-10-28
- **Branch:** feat/step-17d.4-terminal-relay
- **Summary:** The terminals of a workspace are events and requests, and a Relay client watches the host's terminals in a new **Terminals** panel and, where the host allows it, types in them (SPEC §10.25 "As built (step 17d.4 ...)", §10.19 "Terminals"). Nothing of xterm.js was used, read or fetched (not its code, tests, serialize addon or assets), and nothing of any other terminal's code: the panel draws with Emetos's own emulator.
- **Built:**
  - `extensions/terminal`: `emetos.terminal.created/title/output/input/resized/exited/closed` (output in pieces of at most 64 KiB or 50 ms with `seq`, valid UTF-8; input with `by`, left out when the pty does not echo) and `list`, `snapshot` (exactly the screen after piece `seq`), `create` (confined cwd, quiet), `write`, `run`, `kill`; the extension starts with the window; the tab of a terminal a client typed in shows "Relay: <key comment>" until `emetos.relay.disconnected`. Emulator: `AppendValidUTF8`, and the pen restored when the alternate screen is left. `pty.EchoOn`.
  - `extensions/relay`: Allow terminals per key and workspace (`relay.json`, off by default, never for Watch only; checkbox with the warning in Be a Host; checked when a request is read and when it is acted on); `RelayTerminalRefused` for `create`, `write`, `run`, `kill` and any other `emetos.terminal.*` request without it, and for a client that publishes in `emetos.terminal.*`; `by` stamped by the host; the banner says "typing in terminals"; the Hello's workspace carries `terminals` (protocol version 1); the client's Terminals panel (list, snapshot and events with a new snapshot on a gap or reconnect, read-only or taking keys and paste, New Terminal, Kill, a multi-line paste only into a program with bracketed paste); four dev commands (`dev.relay.terminal{New,Select,Type,Kill}`); `dev.terminals` lists remote terminals.
  - Tooling: `internal/archtest` lets `extensions/relay` import `extensions/terminal/emulator`; `display.App.Focus` and the remote fields of `TerminalState`; `fakeapi.AskAs`; `scripts/proof-relay-terminal.sh` and `testdata/proof/17d.4/`.
- **API of `extension/`:** no change.
- **Not built:** a button for `run`; the mouse to the program of a remote terminal; selection, find and links in the client's panel; resizing the host's terminal from the client.

## Step 17d.5: Shell integration

- **Date:** 2026-10-28
- **Branch:** feat/step-17d.5-shell-integration
- **Summary:** The terminal reads VS Code's shell integration: the scripts are bundled and injected into bash, zsh, fish and pwsh, their sequences (OSC 633, 133, 1337) become commands with markers that move with the scrollback and survive reflow, and on them the pane draws decorations, navigates and selects by command, keeps the running command at the top (sticky scroll), runs recent commands and goes to recent folders, starts a split in the shell's folder and offers quick fixes (SPEC §10.25 "As built (step 17d.5 ...)"). Nothing of xterm.js was used, read or fetched (not its code, tests, addons or assets), and nothing of another terminal's code; VS Code's TypeScript outside `browser/xterm/` was read for behavior only.
- **Built:**
  - `extensions/terminal/emulator`: markers (`AddMarker`, `MarkerLine`, `MarkerLines`, `RemoveMarker`, `Markers`) that follow IL, DL, scrolling regions, trimming and reflow, and `Options.MarkCodes` so the `OSCEvent` of 133, 633 and 1337 carries one.
  - `extensions/terminal`: `inject.go` (VS Code's `getShellIntegrationInjection`, the scripts embedded and written to the cache folder, the ZDOTDIR of zsh), `shellint.go` (the commands, their command lines with the nonce rule, exit codes, folders), `quickfix.go`, `history.go` (the four shells' history files and the kept history), `shell_pane.go` (decorations and their menu, navigation, selection, sticky scroll), `terminal_shell.go` (the commands and the wiring), the settings `shellIntegration.{enabled,decorationsEnabled,quickFixEnabled,history}`, `stickyScroll.*` and `splitCwd`, the commands and keys of VS Code's ids, the context key `terminalShellIntegrationActive`.
  - Assets: `scripts/update-assets.sh` copies the scripts; license in `third_party/vscode-shell-integration/`, `NOTICE` 16, `third_party/README.md`.
  - Theme: `terminalStickyScroll.background`, `terminalStickyScrollHover.background`, `terminalStickyScroll.border`.
  - Tooling: `display.TerminalState` fields for decorations and sticky scroll, `cmd/emetos/terminal_shell_display_test.go`.
- **API of `extension/`:** no change.
- **Not built:** the overview ruler, the buttons and Alt+Enter of the recent command pick, "Learn about shell integration", the `…` marker of sticky scroll, `shellIntegration.timeout`, `environmentReporting`, extension-made quick fixes, HTML on the clipboard, PSReadLine's modules; terminal IntelliSense is step 17d.6.
- **Tests:** injection (table-driven, ported from `terminalEnvironment.test.ts`); detection from a recorded real bash session (whole, and cut at every byte offset) and from recordings of the zsh, fish and pwsh scripts' output order (those shells are not installed); a forged nonce; markers across trimming, IL/DL, ED, the alternate screen and reflow with seeded random output; each quick fix matcher with ported cases; the history parsers; the commands over the fake API with a real bash in an isolated `HOME`; goldens `pane_decorations_{dark,light}.png` and `pane_sticky_{dark,light}.png`; real input (`TestRealInputShell*`: blue and red decorations on screen, Ctrl+Up to the first command, Ctrl+Alt+R runs `echo again`, long output keeps its command at the top, `cd /tmp` and a split starts in /tmp).
- **Proof:** `17d.5-shell-integration.png` (method C, real input: the terminal panel with blue circles on `ls main.go` and `echo done`, a red one on the failed `ls missing.txt`, and the sticky overlay `$ seq 1 100` at the top over its output).
- **Decisions:** 190 to 199 of `docs/DECISIONS-TO-REVIEW.md`.
- **Manual checks:** none
- **Glutter issues:** one new: the clipboard carries plain text only (`docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`).

## Fix: the Search view's frame test measured the machine's load

- **Date:** 2026-10-28
- **Branch:** fix/search-frame-test-load
- **Summary:** `TestTheUIThreadStaysUnderAFrameWhileAHugeSearchStreams` (17e.2; SPEC §14 "Search") timed each turn of the UI thread by the wall clock. Alone the longest turn is 9 to 12 ms against 16 ms, but beside the other agents' builds it measured 18.8 to 66 ms and failed three runs. Now each turn is timed by the CPU clock of its thread, the stream runs up to five times and the best run counts, and the limit scales by a fixed reference workload timed in the same run (never below 16 ms; under `-race` both ×4). A new test, `TestTheEngineAsksTheUIThreadOnceForAllItsFilesAndWaitsBetweenAsks`, checks the batching of decision 158 with no clock: 1,000 files that come before the UI thread looks are one ask and one take.
- **Numbers:** 20 of 20 under 24 memory-burning processes and the other agents (before: 5 of 5 failed at load 28); `-race` 5 of 5 (31 to 34 ms against its limit). A tree rebuild made 4 times slower fails it 3 of 3; making the engine ask once per file fails the batching test.
- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · `go vet` (also `CGO_ENABLED=0`) ✅ · golangci-lint on the package ✅ · my run after the rebase onto 17d.5: `go test -race -count=2` and `CGO_ENABLED=0 go test -count=3` of `extensions/search` ✅. Only the test file changed.

## Fix: four tests that failed when the machine was loaded

- **Branch:** fix/load-sensitive-tests
- **`TestThroughputBudgets`** (17d.1; `extensions/terminal/emulator/bench_test.go`) measured MB/s by the wall clock and failed at 311 and 327 MB/s for 400 (plain ASCII), 164 for 200 (scrolling in a region) and 36 for 40 (the worst-case style table) while other builds ran. It now times a run by the CPU clock of its OS thread (`CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID`, the subtest locks its thread), runs a case up to 8 times and fails only when the best run is under the budget, and scales the budget of each run down by how much slower a piece of reference work (`referenceWork`, which does not use the emulator; 8.5 ms of CPU time on a quiet machine) was than on a quiet one. `TestThroughputBudgetsFailASlowEmulator` holds that mechanism without a clock. With the ASCII fast path of `parse` switched off for a moment (`if false && ...`) the test fails (plain ASCII 122 MB/s, scrolling in a region 97 MB/s); with 40 busy loops on the 32 cores it passed 20 of 20 (the budgets of the runs were 157 to 161 MB/s then).
- **`TestTheEventsOfATerminalFromItsStartToItsEnd`** (17d.4; `extensions/terminal/events_test.go`): the test shell turns the terminal's ECHO off as soon as it has started (raw mode, as bash's readline does), and an input event is `Hidden` when ECHO is off (decision 185), so the first event was hidden or not by whether the test typed before or after the shell started. The test now runs the shell with the new argument `--tty-echo` (it leaves ECHO on and lets the terminal echo) and waits for the shell's "ready"; `TestInputToAShellThatTurnedEchoOffIsHidden` holds the other half, also after waiting for "ready".
- **`TestGoldenTheFindWidgetAndItsHighlights`** (17d.3; `extensions/terminal/pane_find_test.go`): `waitSearched` drew frames (80 ms of the fake clock each) while it waited in real time for the search goroutine, so the number of frames, and with it the phase of the find input's blinking caret in the golden image (30 pixels), depended on the load. It now hands over the posted answers without drawing and then draws a fixed number of frames. With the old helper and 40 busy loops 12 of 80 runs differed by 30 pixels; with the new one 0 of 80.
- **`TestDesktopFindAllReferences`** (17e.3; `cmd/emetos/desktop_references_display_test.go`): no click was lost. A result keeps the files that were open in the one before it (`setItems` keeps the expanded keys), and Find All Implementations had opened extra.go (the file nearest to the cursor), so the row was open before the test clicked it: the first click closed it, which the test took for a click that had not opened it. The test now checks that the row is open, clicks (closed), waits 500 ms (two clicks on a row within 400 ms are a double click, which toggles nothing) and clicks again (open). No code changed.

- **Checks:** gofmt ✅ · go vet ✅ (also `emetos_display`, and `emetos_display emetos_gopls`) · golangci-lint ✅ (also `--build-tags emetos_display ./cmd/... ./internal/testenv/...`) · error-tags --untested ✅ · `go mod tidy -diff` clean · go test -race: the whole run had failures only in `internal/gui/app` (and one watcher test of `internal/core/workspace`) when the build's temp folder was moved off the full /tmp (the golden of the save prompt shows a path); `internal/gui/app` alone with the default temp folder ✅ (exit 0, none killed) and `internal/core/workspace` ✅ on rerun · CGO_ENABLED=0 build ✅ · CGO_ENABLED=0 test ✅ (100 packages) · `scripts/realinput.sh` whole suite with `--alone` ✅ (exit 0).


## Step 17d.6: Terminal IntelliSense

- **Date:** 2026-10-28
- **Branch:** feat/step-17d.6-terminal-suggest
- **Summary:** The terminal offers completions under the cursor while a command is typed: the commands of `PATH` and the shell's builtins, the subcommands, options and arguments of 49 commands from VS Code's completion specs, files and folders, and the lines of the history (SPEC §10.25 "Terminal IntelliSense"). The list is the editor's suggest list, shared through `kit` and `extension/ui`. Nothing of xterm.js was used, read or fetched (not its code, tests, addons or assets), and nothing of another terminal's code; VS Code's TypeScript outside `terminal/browser/xterm/` was read for behavior only (one grep slip is told in D219).
- **Built:**
  - `scripts/specs2json` (Go, no dependency): converts the TypeScript subset of the specs to JSON (`specs`, `builtins` and `bash` modes); `scripts/update-assets.sh` runs it for 49 specs (444 KB: git npm npx code cd go ls cat cp mv rm mkdir rmdir touch chmod chown ln grep find head tail sort echo kill killall ps tar curl ssh node python3 sed wc diff du df uniq cut xargs sudo which tree more date env export source clear pwd) and the builtins of bash, zsh and fish into `extensions/terminal/suggestdata/`. Generators are dropped (D117). License in `third_party/vscode-terminal-suggest/`, `NOTICE` 17, `third_party/README.md`.
  - `internal/gui/kit/suggestlist.go`: the list, its rows, icons, bold matches, details and scrollbar, moved out of the editor's `suggest_widget.go`, which now builds its rows with it (the editor's goldens did not change).
  - `extensions/terminal`: `suggest_line.go` (the line and its words, quotes, pipes and redirects), `suggest_spec.go` (the specs walked at the cursor), `suggest_providers.go` (commands, builtins, files, history), `suggest_model.go` (VS Code's ranking), `suggest.go`, `suggest_input.go` (the prompt input read from the screen), `suggest_pane.go` and `suggest_view.go` (the widget at the cursor), the settings `terminal.integrated.suggest.*`, the commands and keys (Ctrl+Space, Tab, Enter, Escape, Up, Down, Page Up/Down, details), the context keys `terminalSuggestEnabled` and `terminalSuggestWidgetVisible`. `emulator.CursorPosition`. Also the history store's limit is now read through an atomic (a race of 17d.5 that these tests found).
  - Tooling: `display.TerminalState` fields of the list; `cmd/emetos/terminal_suggest_display_test.go`.
- **API of `extension/`:** `ui.SuggestRow`, `ui.SuggestListOpts`, `ui.NewSuggestList`, `ui.NewSuggestMessage`, `ui.NewSuggestBox`, `ui.SuggestMatcher`, `ui.SuggestWidth`, `ui.SuggestVerticalPadding` (D211): the smallest set for the terminal to draw the editor's list; the scorer is in `core`, which an extension cannot import.
- **Not built:** the specs of the other 66 commands; generators (so `git checkout` does not list branches); `toggleSuggestionDetailsFocus`; a right prompt or a multi-line command in the input model; the providers of VS Code's `lsp` and extensions; completions from the shell itself; PowerShell, zsh and fish are not installed here, so only their screen reading is tested, from hand-made output.
- **Tests:** table-driven tests of the line parser, the spec walker (real converted specs), the providers over a temp folder, the ranking (ported cases), the settings and their defaults, the input model, the converter (`scripts/specs2json`), the kit list and the `extension/ui` exports (every kind has an icon that exists), the pane over the fake API with a real bash in an isolated `HOME` and a controlled `PATH` with a fake `git` (the real git never runs); goldens `pane_suggest_{dark,light}.png` and `pane_suggest_files_{dark,light}.png` (looked at); real input (`TestRealInputSuggest*`: typing `git che` offers `checkout` and Tab completes it, `cat ` + Ctrl+Space lists the files, nothing opens by itself with the defaults, Escape closes, Down then Enter accepts).
- **Proof:** `17d.6-suggest.png` (method C, real input: the terminal panel after `cat main.go`, `git che` typed, the list with `checkout` selected (with its details icon), `cherry-pick` and `archive`, the match letters in bold).
- **Decisions:** 210 to 219 of `docs/DECISIONS-TO-REVIEW.md`. **210 matters most:** with VS Code's defaults nothing opens by itself (`quickSuggestions` is off), against the plan's wording "typing `gi` offers `git`"; the real-input test turns it on.
